# Don't Die Retired — Operations Runbook

This is the instruction set Claude follows to run dontdieretired.com. It is written so a fresh Claude session with this repo can do the job without any other context.

## 1. What the site is
**Positioning (Robin, 19 Nov 2026; supersedes the older description below where they differ):** Don't Die Retired is **a guide to things worth doing, and how to start.** Things to do is the core: each activity page says how to start, where to go and roughly what it costs, and the full guides and deep hubs (§6u) go further. Stories are the proof that someone went and did it; Planner Plus is how a reader fits the chosen things into their year. Line: "Never retire from life." Promise under it: "Find something worth doing, and how to start." Use that promise wherever the site describes itself (home lead, Start here, About, `site.description`, social bios, the newsletter's welcome). Do not describe the site as for an age group. Where to grow: depth, not breadth. Most effort goes into a few areas we aim to cover better than anyone (endurance events, outdoor adventure, learning a craft or instrument, travel experiences): hubs, a page per event and real filter landing pages, because those are what people search for; the rest are supporting pages. Check Search Console monthly and put effort into whatever already earns impressions.

Older description: a static site Every article is built on a real, named, linked news story. Tone: positive, plain, no hype, no medical claims, no "you must". British spelling.

**The ethos (carry it in every article):** retirement is leaving a job, not leaving life. The site's stance is that the years after 50 are the time to do MORE, not less — to make the most of life rather than resign from it. This applies equally, and is written explicitly for, people who were never physically or mentally active: beginners are the point, not an afterthought. Practically: every article should speak to both the lifelong-active reader and the never-active one (usually via the 'Try this week' box having a true beginner's option); never frame activity as a duty or use guilt; frame it as freedom and appetite; never use "despite their age" or "still" ("still running at 80"). The message is invitation, not instruction.

### 1a. House style (set 29 Sept 2026 — the model is New York Times feature writing)
**Headlines.** One line, sentence case, British spelling. Lead with the person and what they did, because it is interesting or an achievement in its own right — "Betty Brussel broke three swimming world records before lunch". The age is context, not the headline: it goes in the standfirst and the first paragraph, and appears in a headline only when it is itself the record ("oldest woman to finish…"). Never open a headline with an age ("At 83, …", "An 83-year-old…"). Never start with a pronoun ("He…", "She…"), and never use the two-sentence age formula ("She did X at 60. At 65 she…") — it was the house template for the first eleven stories and it reads as a template. Before writing a headline, read the last seven and make this one structurally different (a name, a deed, a place, a colon, a prepositional opening). Present tense for features where it fits. No "still", no "despite", no "never too old", no exclamation marks, no questions, no puns on age. The headline is the promise; the standfirst adds what the headline could not fit.

**Ledes.** Open with a scene, a fact or a detail, not a generalisation. Banned openers: "Most of us…", "Most people…", "There is a type of story…", "Not every story…". Name, age and place on first mention, Times-style: "Jeremy Lockwood, 68, a retired family doctor on the Isle of Wight, …".

**Prose.** Short paragraphs, one idea per sentence, specific numbers over adjectives. Quotes carry the story; attribute with "said" or "told [outlet]", never "enthused", "beamed", "quipped". No rhetorical questions in the first half. Second person only in the practical section and the Try-this box. Cut clichés on sight: "age is just a number", "proving that…", "inspirational", "journey", "incredible". The tone is a well-edited newspaper feature: warm because the facts are, not because the adjectives are.

**Social hooks (Robin, 13 Nov 2026: "please make all future posts to this standard").** The `hook` is the text on every social card and the first line of every Facebook, Instagram and X post, so it must read as correct, natural English when read aloud, not as headline shorthand. Two corrections set the standard:
- "After 42 years and 25,000 hours of flying, Geoff Collis bakes pastries in Eltham. He doesn't miss the cockpit for a day." became "After 42 years and 25,000 hours of flying, Geoff Collis **now** bakes pastries in Eltham. He **hasn't missed** the cockpit for a day."
- "A reporter, a satchel 16 inches wide, and 25,000 miles by steamship, train and rickshaw to beat a fictional record." became "A reporter, a 16 inch wide satchel and 25,000 miles by steamship, train and rickshaw."

The rules behind them: give a sentence the time words it needs ("now", "has", "since") so the change of life is clear; make tenses agree with the idiom ("not for a day" takes "hasn't missed"); put describing words before the noun ("a 16 inch wide satchel", not "a satchel 16 inches wide"); no comma before "and" in a short list; a list-style hook ends when the list ends, with no clause tacked on; shorter is better (aim for under 20 words). Read the hook aloud before committing: if it sounds like a telegram or a translation, rewrite it. **Never change the hook of a piece whose social pack is dated today or yesterday:** a full `python3 build.py` rewrites every pack from its hook, and changed text in that window is posted again (§6d). Correct it from the day after, when the post is out of the window.

### 1b2. The line (set 27 Oct 2026, Robin)
The site's line is **"Never retire from life."** It replaced "Retire from work. Never from life." because it is broader: retirement here is not about stopping paid work, so the line speaks to anyone. Use it on the home page, the link-preview image, the footer tagline and products; never bring back the old wording.

### 1c. Name and wordmark (set 6 Oct 2026)
- **One phrase, one style.** The name is always "Don't Die Retired" in full, with all three words in the same typeface, weight, upright style and colour (the bold serif in dark ink on light, light on dark). The red belongs to the sunrise mark, never to a word. Never italicise, colour or otherwise pick out one or two of the words, and never break a line as "Don't Die" / "Retired".
- **Never "Don't Die" alone**: not as a heading, label, hashtag, abbreviation, image text or social handle. Where a short form is needed, use "DDR" or the mark alone.
- **Always visible.** The full name sits beside the mark in the website header and the app header at every screen width, including small phones. Make it fit by shrinking the type or re-arranging the header, never by hiding it.
- **Files.** `python tools/build_logos.py` rebuilds `brand/logo-horizontal-light.png`, `brand/logo-horizontal-dark.png` and both copies of `og-default.png`. The website header and footer use the `.brand-name` class; the app header matches. Product PDFs and Gumroad covers take the name from `products/build_pdf.js`.
- **What the site is about.** Activity, purpose and making the most of life. Articles, guides, products and affiliate links stay out of longevity, life-extension, anti-ageing, supplements and medical testing, so that the statement in "Our ethos" (About page) stays true. No page names, links to or compares the site with another brand, person or movement in order to describe itself.
- **"Our ethos"** lives on the About page (`/about/#ethos`) with one small footer link. It is meant to be available, not prominent: do not add it to the main menu, the home page, Start here, articles or the app unless Robin asks.

### 1d. Look (refreshed 14 Oct 2026, after feedback that the site looked dated)
Compared with Saga Magazine, Rest Less and AARP, the site was led by an illustration, boxed everything in hairline outlines and set every title in heavy serif. The refresh (the last block of `static/style.css`) keeps the brand (Fraunces, Source Sans 3, crimson, the sunrise mark, topic colours) and changes how it is used:
- **Picture first.** Home opens with the promise, then today's story as a large picture beside its headline. Cards are picture, topic, title, two lines, date: no box, no shadow. The newest story in "Latest" is wider. A photograph (§6l) makes all of this far stronger than the artwork, so give new stories one wherever an honest one exists.
- **White and quiet.** White page, one soft stone colour (`--stone`) for bands and side panels, no outlines on cards or panels. Crimson is for the main action only.
- **Plain titles.** Card and small headings in Source Sans 3 semibold; Fraunces 600 for page headlines, the lead story and section heads. No italic or coloured word in a headline.
- **Solid topic colour.** Topic tiles and the "things to do" tiles (puzzles, Scam checker) and the Get involved panel are solid colour with white text. The topic's colour also labels each card (`[data-cat]` sets `--c`).
- **The app (`/app/`) follows the same rules** (last block of `app/app.css`, 14 Oct 2026): white screens, stone panels with no shadow, sentence-case labels, plain titles in cards, numbers in Source Sans, the story picture first, Fifty at Fifty / puzzles / Scam checker as solid tiles, and a soft pill behind the current tab. When `app.css`, `app.js` or the fonts change, raise the cache name `V` in `app/sw.js` so installed copies update.
- Check any template change at 320, 390, 1040 and 1366px wide, as before.

Repo layout: `site.yaml` (config), `content/articles/*.md`, `content/videos.yaml`, `content/photo_credits.yaml` and `static/photos/` (written by the Photos workflow, §6l), `content/products.yaml`, `templates/`, `static/`, `build.py` → `dist/`; `social/` gets a post pack per article.

## 1b. Who we write for (set 2 Oct 2026 — read before choosing a story)
Readers are **interested, not old**. A fit 50-year-old and an unfit 75-year-old both read this site, and neither wants the other's advice. So:
- **Nobody is excluded by age.** The name implies the audience; the copy never needs to say it. Do not put "over 50", "after 50", "in your 50s", "seniors" or "later in life" in headlines, standfirsts, section blurbs or product names (`new_article.py` rejects such headlines). Readers under 50 are welcome and have their own age option in the profile (`u50`). Mention age in the body only where it changes the facts (bone density, insurance rules, pension ages).
- **Lead with what is interesting** (the feat, the idea, the place, the science), never with the age. Ask: would this be worth reading if the person were 35? If not, find a better story.
- **Spread the ages.** Across any two weeks at least half the people we write about should be in their 50s or early 60s. Stories about people 80+ are the exception, used when the story itself is remarkable.
- **Rotate the level** of the practical advice: about 40% `active`, 30% `advanced`, 30% `starter`. Never assume the reader is unfit. Chair-based or "ten-minute walk" advice belongs only in `starter` pieces.
- **Every article sets** `level` (starter / active / advanced / any; `any` for non-physical topics) and, if there is a person, `subject_age`.
- **Every article has two "Try this" lists**: `try_this` (for someone getting going) and `try_this_plus` (for someone already active or fit: a real stretch, with numbers). Non-physical topics get the same split as "new to this" and "already doing it". The site shows each reader the one that matches their profile.
- Readers set a profile (fitness level, age band, interests) on the site and in the app; it orders their homepage, picks their "Try this", and tags their newsletter sign-up (`level-*`, `age-*`, `int-*`).
- **Never claim a cadence we don't keep.** If the site says daily or weekly, it happens; if a job is missed, catch up the same day and say so in the report.

## 2. Daily job (runs every morning, ~06:00 UK)
0. **Curious days.** On Tuesdays, Thursdays and Saturdays, write a Curious piece instead of steps 1, 2 and 2b (§6o); steps 3 onwards still apply.
1. **Pick the activity, then find the story (Robin, 19 Nov 2026: the daily articles should drive engagement with the site's content).** Choose the Things to do activity first: prefer one with a full guide or a hub, one in season (its `when` includes this month), or one with no `inspire` story yet. Then find a real story about someone doing it. Every daily story must lead to an activity: set `story: <slug>` on the activity (or add a new verified activity), so the home page and the story's foot show "Have a go yourself". If no honest story can be found for the chosen activity, choose another activity; never bend a story to fit. Curious pieces tie to a place or activity to visit where one honestly fits. Then, as before: Web-search for a genuine, recent story of someone 50+ starting or achieving something (sport, study, business, creative, volunteering). Prefer 2024–2026, reputable outlets (BBC, Guardian, regional UK press, ABC AU, CBC, NYT/AP, Guinness). Fetch and read the source. Only use facts and quotes visible on the page. Rotate: women/men; **geography roughly 40% UK, 40% USA, 20% rest of world** (rotate the rest of the world, and the Curious pieces, through every continent: Europe, Africa, Asia, Oceania, South America, Canada and Latin America, and Antarctica when there is a story, so each continent gets articles; Robin, 27 Oct 2026; link the place's activity where the map has one) (the US over-50 audience is ~5× the UK's and pays the highest ad rates); physical/mental; ages 50s–90s; and rotate categories (move / eat / think / money / earn / connect / travel / tech / **ai** (people doing something interesting with artificial intelligence: learning it, teaching it, building or creating with it, or defending others against its misuse; see §6b) / stories / **explore** — science, nature and the outdoors as things to DO: birdwatching, citizen science (BTO, RSPB Garden Birdwatch, iNaturalist, Zooniverse), astronomy, geology and fossil walks, wild swimming, garden rewilding, Wildlife Trust / National Trust / Audubon / Master Naturalist volunteering, U3A and OU science groups — not science news). Write for both audiences: keep British spelling, but gloss UK-only terms on first use — "GP (family doctor)", "NHS (the UK health service)" — give distances/weights in both units where they matter, and prices as £/$ when quoting costs. US-specific hooks worth using when relevant: Medicare's free SilverSneakers gym benefit, US Masters swimming/track, senior games, community colleges for learners.
2. **Write it.** Headline and prose per §1a (read the last seven headlines first; vary the structure). `python new_article.py "Title" --category X --tags a,b --segments restarter,mover --level active --age 57` (it refuses headlines that start with He/She or use the age formula). Fill front matter: `hook` (≤120 chars, used on social + image), `lesson` (one sentence), `standfirst`, `summary`, 3 `try_this` actions, `sources` with URLs. Body 450–700 words: the story, 2–3 verified quotes, a "why this matters" section with the evidence, a practical section. Link to 1–2 related articles.
2b. **Write the plan behind the story.** Create `content/plans/<slug>.md` (front matter: title, article: <slug>, summary, duration, level) with sections: Why this works / Before you start / The plan (table: Week, Do this, How often, Notes — with a true-beginner row) / If it goes wrong / Track it / What next. 500–800 words. This is the email-gated premium layer; it must exist for every article.
2c. **"How to start" guide and "Try it near you" listings.** If today's story is about an activity that has no beginner's guide yet (check `content/local.yaml` → `activities` and the `kind: guide` articles), write one: `content/articles/<date>-<activity>-for-beginners.md`, `kind: guide`, 700–1,000 words per docs/EVERGREEN_BRIEF.md, covering why it is easier than it looks, the honest challenges, the good part (the fun and the evidence), what to bring, and how to start this month. Then add the activity to `content/local.yaml` with 1–3 checked listings per region (uk, us-east, us-central, us-mountain, us-pacific, canada, elsewhere — skip a region rather than pad it). Put `local: <activity-id>` in the front matter of both the guide and the story; the story links to the guide once. Listing rules: every entry is a real place whose own or official page you fetched today (set `checked:`); prices say "from" when they vary; re-check any entry older than six months when you touch that activity; anything paid for gets `sponsored: true` (it renders a label); lesson/experience links on Viator or GetYourGuide get `aff: viator|getyourguide`, and the ids come from `site.yaml` → `affiliates`. If the activity already has a guide, just add `local:` to the story and link the guide. The reader's region comes from the browser time zone only (app.js §5): no IP lookup, no cookie, no consent needed; readers can change it with the selector.
2d. **A film and a photograph for the story (§6l).** Look for a film of the person on the YouTube channel of the broadcaster or organisation that covered them, and for an openly licensed photograph of the place or the activity on Wikimedia Commons. Neither is compulsory: a story with no confirmed film has no film, and a story with no honest photograph keeps its artwork.
3. **Videos: every Monday, Wednesday and Friday** add one film to the TOP of `content/videos.yaml` with `added: <today>`, `level` (starter/active/advanced/any), tags and an honest `why`. The page promises this cadence and shows the last-added date, so do not skip it; if the newest `added` is more than three days old, add one today whatever the weekday.
   - **Verifying it (changed 6 Oct 2026).** The shell cannot reach YouTube, so `curl` always fails; that is not a reason to skip. Verify with the **WebFetch tool**: fetch `https://www.youtube.com/oembed?url=https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ID&format=json` and check the `title` and `author_name` match the film you mean. Copy the title exactly as YouTube gives it.
   - **Backstop.** If WebFetch is refused too, still add the film, with the ID and exact title taken from a WebSearch result on youtube.com. The deploy workflow runs `verify_videos.py` before every build: it asks YouTube about every film and `build.py` leaves off any whose ID YouTube does not confirm or whose title is a different film. So an unverified ID can never go live; it just does not appear.
   - **State at 7 Oct 2026.** In the scheduled cloud run WebFetch refused every YouTube address (oEmbed and watch pages) and any page that had not first appeared in a WebSearch result, so search for a page before fetching it and fetch one page at a time (parallel fetches were rate-limited). The film added that day (`ph9fIpB8PbY`) went in on the backstop with its title from a youtube.com search result; YouTube confirmed it at deploy, but its `channel` is blank because nothing we could open named the channel. Fill it in when a session can read YouTube, and do not put that film on the story page until then (§6l needs the channel). **12 Oct 2026:** the same happened again: `0yz5F9WtmsI` (the Mountain Queen trailer, ID taken from the embed on Himal Press's report of the trailer launch) went in on the backstop; YouTube confirmed it at deploy (20 of 20 verified), but its exact title and channel are unread, so `channel` is blank and the title is Netflix's usual trailer form. Correct both from oEmbed when a session can read it. **14 Oct 2026:** same again: `9CZwjw19SNw` (ABC-style report on Trevor Barry, title from a youtube.com search result) went in on the backstop with `channel` blank, and is not on the story page because the channel is unconfirmed (§6l). The oEmbed address needed a permission nobody was there to give, and the watch page failed to fetch. **16 Oct 2026:** same again: `BcnAZb7z0DE` (Guinness World Records' film of DonnaJean Wilde's 2024 plank record; ID from the embed on Guinness's own news page) went in on the backstop. No page we could read gave its exact title, so the title is our best form of Guinness's usual wording; YouTube's check passed it as the same film at deploy (22 of 22 verified), but correct the title to YouTube's exact wording and fill `channel` from oEmbed when a session can read it. It is not on the story page. **19 Oct 2026:** oEmbed again needed a permission nobody was there to give, but the YouTube **watch page** fetched fine once its URL had appeared in a WebSearch result, and gave the exact title and channel: `RKq1mAWVZHc` went in with both filled (23 of 23 verified at deploy). Try the watch page before falling back. **6 Nov 2026:** the watch page and oEmbed were both refused for `XP00XnxVk_4` (a Zooniverse how-to-start film), so it went in on the backstop with its title from a youtube.com search result and `channel` blank; correct the channel when a session can read oEmbed. **11 Nov 2026:** same again for `PV71eGtrZx4` ("Archery for Beginners: What to Expect at Your First Lesson", embedded in Archery 360's beginners' guide; title from a youtube.com search result): watch page failed and oEmbed was refused, so `channel` is blank. The build job's annotations could not be read either (the GitHub tools give logs only, and the full build log is too long to page through); the build and deploy passed. **16 Nov 2026:** oEmbed refused again for `CYbMwL_PXy0` (embedded on Guinness's Mark Herbst page). A useful trick: it went in with a guessed title, the Videos check held it back and its warning named YouTube's real title and channel ("Widower biking 30,000 kilometres", CTV News), and the film was corrected and re-pushed the same run. The build log is readable with `get_job_logs` (`return_content`, large `tail_lines`): the tool saves it to a file, then grep it for `Videos check`.
   - **Check the result.** After the push, read the `build` job's "Videos check" annotation (`gh api repos/RRHutchins/dontdieretired/commits/<sha>/check-runs`, then `.../check-runs/<id>/annotations`) and put it in the report. A warning means a film was held back: replace it the same day.
   - Blurbs describe only what the title and our own article establish; do not describe scenes you have not watched. Spread the subjects: most films should be about people in their 50s and 60s doing interesting things, not only record-breaking 90-year-olds.
3b. **Fifty at Fifty (§6h).** After the day's story, add one or two new verified challenges to `content/challenges.yaml`, one tied to the day's story where it honestly fits (set `story: <slug>`). Fetch every link before adding it; new ids only.
4. **Build + verify.** `python build.py`. Open `dist/<category>/<slug>/index.html`; confirm sources render, no template errors, image generated.
5. **Publish.** `git add -A && git commit -m "Daily: <title>" && git push` (GitHub Actions deploys to Pages in ~1 min).
6. **Social.** Nothing to run: committing the pack (`social/<date>-<slug>/meta.json`) is enough, and the deploy workflow schedules Facebook, Instagram and X through Buffer (§6d). After the push, read the `social` job's annotation (§6d) and put its result in the report. Image cards in the pack folder are local only: `square.png` (IG/FB), `story.png` (Reels/Stories/TikTok cover), `landscape.png` (X/FB link). The full build also writes the next three weeks of puzzle packs; commit any new `social/*-puzzle-*` folders. Check `content/activity_posts.yaml` reaches at least seven days ahead and add evening posts if not (§6d, three posts a day).
7. **Short video.** Send `short_video_script.md` to the video tool (HeyGen/Pictory/InVideo API) when that account exists; upload to YouTube Shorts, Reels, TikTok via the scheduler.

## 3. Weekly job (Sunday)
- **Newsletter (state at 9 Oct 2026): drafting is built, nothing has been sent yet.** Robin agreed to a weekly email on 9 Oct. One edition a week, to everyone (tags can come later: see below).
  - **How it works.** The Sunday job writes `newsletters/YYYY-WW.md` (front matter: `subject`, `week`, `send_on: <that Sunday>`; body in Markdown, no front matter inside the body) and pushes it. The **Weekly email drafts** workflow (`.github/workflows/newsletter.yml`, `send_newsletter.py`) puts it into Buttondown **as a draft only**; it never sends, skips a subject Buttondown already holds, and ignores editions whose `send_on` has passed. Robin reads the draft in Buttondown and presses send (aim for 12:00 UK Sunday, 7am US Eastern). Check a run with `gh api repos/RRHutchins/dontdieretired/commits/<sha>/check-runs` and read the `draft` job's annotation.
  - **Needs** the repository secret `BUTTONDOWN_API_KEY`. Until it exists the workflow passes with a warning naming the edition that is waiting. **State at 11 Oct 2026:** the secret was not set when the workflow ran on 9 Oct, so the first edition (`2026-41.md`, dated to send 11 Oct) never reached Buttondown and nothing has been sent. When the secret exists, re-date that edition or write the next one; an edition whose `send_on` has passed is ignored.
  - **What goes in an edition:** the week's lead story with its two "Try this" options (getting going, and already fit), three or four more of the week's stories or guides with one plain line each, one thing from Fifty at Fifty, one line on the puzzles if something changed, and the AI line at the foot. Only what the site already says: no new facts, no claims about readers, no prices. Every link must exist in `dist/` before pushing. Headline-style subject, never leading with an age.
  - **`newsletter.sending` stays `false`** until Robin confirms an edition has gone out and the next is drafted; then set it `true` so the site promises a weekly email. If a Sunday is missed after that, say so in the report.
  - **Later:** three versions by level (`level-starter`, `level-active`, `level-advanced` tags) once the list is big enough to matter.
- Link check: `python tools/check_links.py` (§6e); fix or remove anything dead. It covers the Fifty at Fifty links too; re-verify any challenge whose `checked` date is over six months old (`build.py` prints LIST WARNING) and run `node tools/test_list_merge.js`.
- Analytics review (Plausible): read `reports/data/YYYY-WW.json` (see §3a) and log the numbers in `reports/YYYY-WW.md`: totals against the previous four weeks, top pages, entry pages, sources, goals (`newsletter_submit`, `affiliate_click`, `product_click`, `plan_view`), and the `scroll_depth`, `article_fit` and `profile` splits. If the file for the week is missing, say so and say why (read the `Weekly visitor numbers` workflow's annotation).
- Apply the **improvement loop** in §5.

## 3a. Visitor numbers (set up 6 Oct 2026)
**Changed 2 Nov 2026 (Robin): the Plausible trial has ended, so Plausible is switched off** (`site.yaml` → `analytics.plausible_script_id: ""`). Visitor numbers now come from **Cloudflare Web Analytics** (`analytics.cloudflare_token`; free, cookie-free, no custom events). Until a fetch for Cloudflare's numbers is built, `stats.yml` writes nothing and the weekly report says "visitor numbers: see the Cloudflare dashboard" rather than guessing; the event-based checks below (goals, `article_fit`, puzzle and list events) are paused, not deleted, and come back if Plausible is turned on again. The Plausible notes below are kept for that case.

The cloud session cannot reach plausible.io, so the numbers are fetched by GitHub Actions: `.github/workflows/stats.yml` runs `fetch_stats.py` every Sunday 03:17 UTC (and on demand) and commits `reports/data/YYYY-WW.json`: the last seven full days, the four weeks before, top and entry pages, sources, countries, goals and property splits. It needs the repository secret `PLAUSIBLE_API_KEY` (a Plausible **Stats API** key, which Plausible only offers on its Business plan) and the custom events added as Goals in Plausible (Site settings → Goals): `newsletter_submit`, `affiliate_click`, `product_click`, `scroll_depth`, `article_fit`, `profile`, `plan_view`, `share`, `say_vote`, `say_suggest`, `local_click`. Without the secret the job passes with a warning and writes nothing. Check a run: `gh api repos/RRHutchins/dontdieretired/actions/workflows/stats.yml/runs?per_page=1`, then the job's annotations as in §6d. Until a data file exists, the improvement loop in §5 cannot run: say so in the weekly report rather than guessing. **State at 11 Oct 2026:** GitHub listed no run of this workflow at all at 05:10 UTC on its first Sunday, so there was no data file and no annotation; check on the next Sunday whether the schedule fires, and start it by hand (Actions → Weekly visitor numbers → Run workflow) if it has not. The scheduled session has no `gh`; the GitHub tools do the same job (`actions_list` for runs and jobs, and `get_job_logs` with the job id returns the log, where each annotation appears as a `##[notice]` or `##[warning]` line).

## 4. Revenue stack — setup checklist (owner does these once; Claude wires them in `site.yaml`)
| Stream | Account to create | Where it plugs in | Turns on when |
|---|---|---|---|
| Display ads | Google AdSense (day 1); apply to Ezoic at ~10k sessions/mo, Mediavine/Raptive at 50k+ | `ads.*` in site.yaml; slots already in templates; consent banner handles GDPR | AdSense approval (needs ~20+ articles, privacy page — done) |
| Affiliate | Amazon Associates UK + US; later Awin/Impact for fitness brands, Decathlon, Wiggle etc. | `affiliates.amazon_uk_tag`; `content/products.yaml` picks; app.js appends tag | Immediately after approval (must make 3 sales in 180 days to keep Amazon account) |
| Newsletter | Buttondown (free to 100 subs) or Beehiiv | `newsletter.form_action`; segment passed as tag | Day 1 |
| Paid newsletter tier | Buttondown/Beehiiv paid subscriptions | `newsletter.paid_tier_url` | When free list > 500 |
| Digital products | Gumroad (0 monthly fee, ~10% + fees) | `products.store_url`, `content/products.yaml` | Day 1 — Claude writes the PDFs (see §6) |
| Membership | Ko-fi (0% platform fee on memberships) or Patreon | `membership.url` | When list > 1,000 |
| Sponsorship | None — email | `/sponsor/` page + rate card in site.yaml | Pitch from 10k monthly sessions |
| Platform payouts | Meta (FB/IG) monetisation, X Creator Revenue, YouTube Partner, TikTok Creator Rewards | Scheduler posts drive it | At each platform's threshold (see plan document) |
| Lead-gen / courses (later) | Own cohort course or Teachable | `/shop/` | When product sales prove demand |

## 5. Improvement loop (the "if traffic grows, we improve" rule)
Every Sunday, compare this week with the last four. Act on triggers:
- **Article read depth**: if `scroll_depth 90` < 40% of `scroll_depth 50` on a format → shorten, add sub-heads, move "Try this" higher. Test one change for two weeks.
- **Traffic > 1,000 sessions/week** → start the short-video pipeline properly (daily Reel/Short/TikTok from `short_video_script.md`), because video is the cheapest way to multiply reach at that stage.
- **Traffic > 5,000/week** → commission/produce one longer YouTube piece per week (5–8 min "second start" story, script written by Claude); apply to Ezoic.
- **Segment split** shows one profile > 40% → write 2 of 7 weekly articles for that segment; build the next digital product for it.
- **Affiliate clicks > 2% of article views** on a category → add a "kit list" article for that category (higher-intent, better conversion).
- **Newsletter signups < 1% of visitors** → change the lead magnet or move the form above the fold; test two weeks.
- **Product clicks but no sales** → lower the price or add a preview PDF page.
- **Your say (monthly, 1st of the month)** → export `GET /api/admin?token=ADMIN_TOKEN` (votes, interests, suggestions). Then: (1) pick the top-voted open proposal that's feasible and set it `planned` with a one-line `note` of what we'll make and when; set shipped ones `done`; (2) turn the 1–3 best new suggestions into `open` proposals (reword clearly, no names or personal details); (3) weight next month's article rotation towards the top interests; (4) tell readers in the next Sunday newsletter what won and what's coming ("You asked, we did"). Reply by email to any suggestion that left an address and needs an answer. Never publish suggestion text verbatim. No live Q&As or live events: this site is run by AI and everything is written and asynchronous.
- **"Was this for you?" (Plausible event `article_fit`)**: weekly, look at the split of yes / too basic / too much / not my thing by category and level. If "too basic" > 25% on a level, pitch that level harder; if a category draws "not my thing" > 40%, change the angle before writing more of it.
- **Fifty at Fifty (§6h)**: weekly, look at which challenges are added (`list_add`) and done (`list_done`) most, and write guides, plans and "Try it near you" listings for those first. Monthly, report `list_active`: it is the number that decides when accounts are worth building.
- **Any article shared > 50 times** → make a video of it and a follow-up article.

UK VAT; enter price ÷ 1.2 in Gumroad, which adds VAT at checkout): £5 Restart30/Reconnect/Tech Confident; £7 Eat Strong/Sharp/Slow Travel; £9 Strong at 60/Money Reset; £12 Second Act; £29 bundle (£66 separately). ### 6i. Product sources, versions and reviews (set 7 Oct 2026)
- **Every product JSON carries** `version`, `reviewed` (month and year), `about` (how it was checked, the date sources were opened, what changed) and `sources` (group, publisher, title, year, url, supports). `build_pdf.js` renders them as the two closing sections, "About this guide" and "Where this comes from". A product without them must not be sold.
- **A source goes in the list only if its page was opened in that session and supports the point.** Copy the title, publisher and date from the page; never from memory. Fetch one page at a time: many researchers fetching at once get the fetch service rate-limited (HTTP 429) and the run is cut short.
- **Never claim a professional review.** The "What has not happened" box stays until a named, registered professional has signed off that exact version in writing (see the Expert review brief of 30 Sept 2026). Then replace the box with their name, registration and the date, and say so in the corrections log.
- **No invented people in products either:** no "readers tell us", testimonials, member groups or typical-results figures without a named, linked source. Do not describe the newsletter or membership as doing something they are not yet doing: the products say only that the free email list exists.
- **Changing a product:** edit its JSON, re-check any source the change rests on, raise `version`, update `about.changes`, rebuild with `python3 products/fit.py <id>` (needs `products/upgrade.json`, which is private and not in git: the nine codes are also in the Gumroad dashboard under Checkout → Discounts), then tell Robin, because only he can upload to Gumroad. Add a line to the corrections log on `/editorial-policy/` and update `corrections_total` in `build.py` if the count is quoted.
- **Re-check dates.** Money Reset, Second Act, Slow Travel and Tech Confident state rules that change: re-open their sources every April (UK tax year) and October, and whenever a daily run meets a rule change (ETIAS starting, a tax threshold moving, a service renamed). The health guides: once a year, each October.
- **Open items at 7 Oct 2026:** ETIAS start date could not be read on the EU's own page (Slow Travel says "not started on the pages we could read; check"); three papers are cited from university repository copies because the journal pages refuse automated readers (Fiatarone 1990, PROT-AGE 2013, Behm 2016).

### 6j. Editorial policy and corrections (set 7 Oct 2026)
`/editorial-policy/` states the rules the site works to. Keep it true: if a practice changes, change the page in the same commit. When a correction changes the substance of a published article, add a line at the foot of the body, "Corrected <date>: <what changed>", and leave the `date` alone. Add product corrections and any serious article correction to the corrections log on that page.

The site's priority is scaling DDR and increasing sales. Categories: move, eat, think, money, earn, connect, travel, tech, ai, explore, curious (§6o), stories (site.yaml).

## 6b. The AI section (added 2 Oct 2026)
Category `ai` (/ai/). Its job: readers finish better able to use AI and harder to fool with it. It follows docs/EVERGREEN_BRIEF.md → "The AI section" for sourcing, even-handedness between AI makers and the health/money/law limits. There is no AI product yet (readers can vote for an audio course on Your say, proposal `audio-ai`); do not mention one until it is in content/products.yaml.
- **Stories.** About one daily story in ten goes in `ai`: a named person aged 50+ doing something worth reading about with AI. Same rules as every story (§1a, §1b, §7), plus a plan that uses only free tools and includes the two safety habits (check what it tells you; keep private details out).
- **Keeping it current (this is the promise of the section).** AI changes faster than anything else on the site, so on the **first Monday of each month**: (1) re-fetch every source cited by the `ai` guides that is an AI maker's help, policy or privacy page or a scam-reporting route, and correct any guide whose setting name, retention period, phone number or reporting address has changed; (2) search the FTC, FBI IC3, FCC, NCSC, Report Fraud, Which? and AARP for new warnings about AI-enabled scams aimed at consumers and, if there is a new pattern, add a guide or a section and link it from the pillar; (3) check whether a widely used assistant has added or removed something a beginner would notice (a free tier, a temporary-chat mode, a training setting) and update the first-conversation and privacy guides; (4) change `date` only when the substance changed, never to look fresh, and add a line "Updated <date>: <what changed>" at the foot of the body; (5) record what was checked in `reports/ai-YYYY-MM.md`.
- **Between reviews:** if a daily run comes across a major change (a new law on fake reviews or deepfakes, a new official warning, a maker changing its training default), fix the affected guide that day and say so in the report.
- **Done 7 Oct 2026 (was queued from the October review):** the voice-clone guide now covers fake "lifestyle survey" calls (National Trading Standards, February 2026; source used: the Press Association report carried by The Irish News, because the Which? article itself was not found) and the fakes guide covers AI chatbots running fake job interviews (AARP, 19 March 2026). Not queued: "nudify" deepfakes and the Take It Down Act (a harm to children, not a scam our readers meet; revisit only if a grandparenting guide needs it).
- **Weekly evergreen rule (§6a) applies:** `ai` launched with one pillar and six guides. Candidates for the next ones: AI in search results and how to read an AI summary; AI features already on your phone; using AI for letters, complaints and forms; AI for family history; AI for travel planning; AI and job hunting after 50; smart speakers and voice assistants; romance scams and AI-generated profiles; what the law says about AI where you live.

## 6d. Social posting (state at 6 Oct 2026; three a day since 19 Nov 2026)
- **Pinterest: live.** Pinterest itself auto-publishes from `https://dontdieretired.com/feed.xml` (Settings → Create Pins in bulk → Auto-publish). It needs the site to stay claimed, so keep `social_verify.pinterest` in site.yaml, and it needs the `<enclosure>` image on every feed item, so keep that in `write_rss`. Nothing to run. **Since 9 Oct** each feed item's image is a tall 1000×1500 pin (`static/pin/<slug>.png`, the headline in the topic colour), and there is one feed per topic at `feed/<topic>.xml` (`stories` = every daily story) so each can feed its own board. If Robin connects topic feeds, the all-in-one feed should be disconnected in Pinterest, or every article is pinned twice.
- **Facebook, Instagram, X: automatic through Buffer (free plan, 3 channels).** The `social` job in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` runs `post_social.py` after every deploy, using the GitHub Actions secret `BUFFER_API_KEY`. **Do not run it from the cloud session**: the shell cannot reach Buffer and does not have the key. Nothing to do in the daily job beyond committing the article's `social/<date>-<slug>/meta.json` as usual.
  - **What it posts.** Packs dated today or yesterday (UK), never before `START_DATE`. It asks Buffer what it already holds and skips any post whose text is already there, so re-runs and several pushes a day are safe. Changing a pack's post text after it has been sent would post it again, so do not edit a sent pack's `meta.json`.
  - **When.** One slot per article, the first free of 13:00, 18:00, 08:00 UK still ahead that day (`SLOTS_UK`); if none is left, ten minutes after the run. Stories take a slot before guides. One post per channel per article: X rejects duplicates, so the old "two slots per article" idea is dropped.
  - **Hashtags (Robin, 9 Nov 2026: adults are the target market, so no age tags).** Set in `build.py` (`BRAND_HASHTAGS`, `TOPIC_HASHTAGS`, `NO_HASHTAG`) for packs dated from 10 Nov 2026 (`HASHTAGS_FROM`). Instagram: the three brand tags #DontDieRetired #NeverRetireFromLife #TryNewThings, one fixed tag for the topic (move #MoveMore, eat #CookFromScratch, think #KeepLearning, money #MoneyMatters, earn #CareerChange, connect #Community, travel #Adventure, tech #TechTips, ai #AIForEveryone, explore #Outdoors, curious #History, stories #RealStories), then the first two of the article's own tags (so put the searchable subject tags first). X: #DontDieRetired plus one article tag. Facebook: #DontDieRetired. There is no LinkedIn account (Robin, 9 Nov 2026), so packs from 10 Nov have no LinkedIn text. Never #over50, #activeageing, #seniors, #agingwell or #retirementlife; #Retirement only on a post really about retirement (pensions, leaving work). The `men`/`women` tags are for balancing the mix and never become hashtags. Older packs keep their old text on purpose: do not regenerate them.
  - **How each looks.** Facebook: a link post (preview card from the article's og:image). Instagram: the square card `/static/social/<slug>.png` (built by `build.py` for articles up to 7 days old), caption with hashtags and "link in our bio". X: the headline card `/static/img/<slug>.png` with the link in the text. **From 17 Nov 2026** an article with `photo_social: true` gets the photo card instead on all three, and a photo credit in the Facebook and Instagram text (§6l, social posts).
  - **Checking a run.** Logs cannot be read from the cloud session, but the script writes its result as an annotation: `gh api repos/RRHutchins/dontdieretired/commits/<sha>/check-runs` for the `social` job id, then `gh api repos/RRHutchins/dontdieretired/check-runs/<id>/annotations`. A failed `social` job means something was not scheduled; the site itself has still deployed. The annotation also counts the last two days' posts as published, waiting or failed, and gives Buffer's reason for any that failed to publish (shown as a warning; a failed post is retried once, on the next run while its pack is still in the two-day window). `python3 post_social.py --check` in the workflow lists the connected channels and Buffer's input shapes without posting.
  - **Limits.** Free plan: 3,000 API requests per 30 days (a run uses 3 plus one per post), 10 scheduled posts per channel, and one API key. The key was created 6 Oct 2026 with a one-year expiry, so it stops working around 6 Oct 2027: remind the owner in September 2027. If posting starts failing with an authorisation error, the key has expired or been regenerated: the owner makes a new one (Buffer → profile icon → API → Personal Access) and replaces the `BUFFER_API_KEY` secret.
- **Three posts a day (added 18 Nov 2026, Robin: "Generate new contents for social media and puzzles. Arrange daily social media posts").** Each day on Facebook, Instagram and X: **08:00 UK the daily puzzle, 13:00 the story, 18:00 a thing to do** (book and other packs fall to 20:00, 10:30, 15:30). A pack names its slot in `meta.json` (`"slot"`); `post_social.py` gives a pack its own slot when free and ahead, sorts slotted packs first, posts `fb_image` packs to Facebook as a picture post (square card, link in the text) rather than a link post, and never posts a `same_day` pack (the puzzle) after its own date.
  - **Puzzle posts** (`tools/daily_posts.py`, run by every full `python3 build.py`): packs `social/<date>-puzzle-<date>/` for today and 21 days ahead, written from the puzzles already in `puzzle_days.json` / `puzzle_extra.json`, so the card shows exactly the puzzle on the site that day (the Wheel's letters in the page's own shuffled order). Rota by weekday: Mon Wheel, Tue Ladder (steady), Wed Numbers (steady), Thu Sudoku (gentle 6×6), Fri Wheel, Sat Ladder (hard), Sun Numbers (hard). The caption gives yesterday's answer for the Wheel, Ladder and Numbers (the site shows yesterday's answers too); never today's. Cards are drawn in every build (`draw_all`), in the Think blue.
  - **Thing-to-do posts**: a hand-written queue in `content/activity_posts.yaml` (one a day, rules in its header: facts only from the activity's entry, card hook under 20 words read aloud, caption that stands on its own without the card, `photo: true` only after looking at the photo for social use). Cards use `make_photo_card` with the activity's checked Commons photo and its credit; the Facebook and Instagram text carry the full credit. `build.py` checks the file on every build. **The daily run keeps this list at least seven days ahead**, choosing what suits the season (first run: 14 posts, 19 Nov to 2 Dec 2026). If it runs dry, no evening post goes out; nothing breaks.
  - Buffer use: about 9 posts and 10 to 30 API requests a day, well inside the free plan.
- **The feeds are for machines.** `feed.xml` and `feed/<topic>.xml` are raw RSS for reader apps and Pinterest, so a browser shows them as code. People reach them through `/follow/` (footer: "Follow in a feed reader"), which explains this and gives copy buttons; never link the raw `.xml` from a page again (16 Oct 2026, after Robin found the footer RSS link opened code).
- **YouTube: account exists, nothing posted** (no videos yet). **TikTok: no account**; its footer link is hidden by the empty `social.tiktok`.

## 6e. Scam help page (added 6 Oct 2026)
**Name and placement (set 7 Oct 2026, Robin):** readers see it as **Scam checker** everywhere (page title, menus, cards, app); the address stays `/scam-help/`. It is a green button in the header on every screen size, because we want people to feel confident using it often. On phones it sits beside the name and the email link moves into the menu row. Do not rename it or bury it.
`/scam-help/` sends each kind of suspected scam to an existing free checking tool, and has our own six questions for phone calls and doorstep visits, which no tool can check. Data: `content/scam_help.yaml`; page: `templates/scam-help.html`; questions and country switch: `static/scam.js` (the rules are written out in its opening comment).
- **Only what we have confirmed.** A tool or reporting route goes in only after its own official page has been fetched that day and says what the entry says. Never add a phone number, short code, email address or link from memory. If it cannot be confirmed, leave it out and say so in the report.
- **Never claim testing we have not done.** `tested: none` in the YAML makes the page say we have not tested the tools' accuracy ourselves. Change it only after real hands-on testing, and then describe exactly what was tried.
- **Free, no account, no commission.** Tools must be free for the basic check and need no sign-up. No affiliate links on this page. Prefer official and independent bodies to products.
- **Not just for older readers.** Scams catch everyone; the page never frames it by age.
- **Monthly (first Monday, with the AI review in §6b):** re-fetch every tool page and reporting-route source in `content/scam_help.yaml`, correct anything that has changed, try again to confirm the tools listed under `rejected` as "could not confirm" (Who Called Me), set `checked:` to the day you did it, and note it in the monthly report. `build.py` prints a SCAM HELP WARNING when `checked` is more than 120 days old.
- **Weekly:** `python tools/check_links.py` lists links that no longer answer (where the shell cannot reach the web, fetch the links with the web tools instead). Fix or remove a dead link the same day.

## 6f. Share button and the friend welcome (added 6 Oct 2026)
- The `share_button` macro (`templates/_macros.html`) is on every article and guide (top and foot), plans (it shares the story, because plans are email-gated), the videos page and scam help; the app has "Share this story". On phones it opens the device's own share sheet; elsewhere a small menu (WhatsApp, Facebook, X, Email, Copy link). No third-party share widgets.
- Every shared link carries `?via=friend`. The site never learns who a link was sent to, and we never collect or email a friend's address.
- A visitor arriving with `?via=friend` sees one slim, closable line at the top ("A friend thought you'd like this…") with a link to the free weekly email. It sits in the page, never over it. **Never make this more intrusive**: no pop-up, no overlay, and no profile questions on arrival (the home-page profile prompt is switched off for these visitors). One quiet line after the story offers the interests picker.
- Plausible events: `share` (props `via`, `kind`), `friend_arrival`, `friend_signup`. Review them weekly with the other numbers in §3: shares by route, arrivals, and how many arrivals join the email.

## 6g. Daily puzzles: four a day, three levels, one streak (Wheel added 6 Oct 2026; Ladder, Sudoku, Numbers and levels added 7 Oct 2026)
`/puzzle/` has four puzzles every calendar day, the same for everyone, changing at the reader's own midnight: the **Wheel** (nine letters, every word uses the middle one), the **Ladder** (change one letter at a time), **Sudoku** and **Numbers** (reach a target with + − × ÷). They exist to give people a reason to come back each day, and Robin's brief is "variety and a challenge". **No end date (Robin, 18 Nov 2026):** the daily puzzles and their 08:00 social posts run every day indefinitely; the daily publish task extends the puzzle files whenever PUZZLE WARNING appears (puzzle data currently runs to 19 Dec 2027).
- **Levels.** The reader picks Gentle, Steady or Hard (kept in the browser, default Steady). Each level is a *different puzzle*, not the same one with more help, and a reader can mix levels across the four on the same day.

  | | Gentle | Steady | Hard |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Wheel (same wheel for all) | goal 15% of the words | goal 25% ("Good") | goal 60% ("Excellent") |
  | Ladder | 4 letters, 3 changes | 4 letters, 5 changes | 5 letters and 5 changes, or 4 letters and 7, on alternate days |
  | Sudoku | 6x6; wrong numbers show at once | 9x9, single gaps only; Check button | 9x9, needs pairs or locked candidates; Check button |
  | Numbers | 4 numbers, 2 sums | 5 numbers, 3 sums | 6 numbers, 4 or 5 sums |
- **How they are built.** `content/puzzle_days.json` holds the Wheel's nine-letter word and middle letter per date (`tools/build_puzzles.py`; answers are worked out at build time from `tools/wordlist/words.txt`, SCOWL, minus `blocklist.txt`). `content/puzzle_extra.json` holds the other three at all three levels per date (`tools/build_daily.py`, standard library only, seeded by date). `build.py` re-checks each day's puzzles with plain code and writes one small file per day to `dist/puzzle/d/`, only for two days back and fourteen ahead, plus `w4.txt` and `w5.txt` (every word a reader may use as a ladder rung). So an old puzzle really is gone, and the site must be built at least once a fortnight (the daily job does this).
- **Every puzzle is proved before it is used.** `python tools/build_daily.py --check` re-proves all of them with separate code: each ladder is a shortest route through the whole word list, each sudoku has exactly one solution and needs exactly the techniques its level says, each numbers target cannot be reached in fewer sums than its level says. Run it after generating and before committing. Never describe the puzzles as checked unless that run passed.
- **Never change a day that is already in either file**: people may be part-way through it. To stop a word being a Wheel answer or a ladder rung from now on, add it to `tools/wordlist/blocklist.txt` (ladders also honour the puzzle books' `_BLOCK` list); that only affects days generated afterwards.
- **When `build.py` prints PUZZLE WARNING** (under 60 days left in either file): `pip install wordfreq`, `python tools/build_puzzles.py --extend 365`, then `python tools/build_daily.py` and `python tools/build_daily.py --check`, read the new ladder words for anything crude or obscure, build, and commit. Check this in the weekly job.
- **Streak.** A day counts when the reader finishes any one of the four at any level. The streak, best streak, level and today's progress are kept in the reader's browser only (`ddr_puzzle`), and the page says so. A missed day ends it with a kind line; never add warnings, countdown nags, pop-ups or notifications about streaks. Hints are free and never cost a streak.
- **No health claims.** Nothing about memory, "brain training" or dementia. They are there because they are enjoyable.
- **Original work.** Do not copy the name, look or wording of any commercial puzzle. Ladders (Lewis Carroll's "Doublets", 1877), sudoku and arithmetic targets are generic forms; keep our own names and our own look.
- **Accessibility (§6k).** Every puzzle works by keyboard (arrow keys and digits in the sudoku, typing in the Wheel and Ladder), results are announced in a status line, and nothing is under 24px. Keep that when adding a type.
- **Plausible events:** `puzzle_start` (prop `type`), `puzzle_done` (`type`, `level`), `puzzle_level` (`level`), `puzzle_hint` (`type`), `puzzle_good` and `puzzle_excellent` (Wheel), `puzzle_streak` (prop `length`: 1, 2-6, 7-29, 30+). Review weekly (§5): which type and level people finish tells us what to add next (a fifth type should come from the puzzle books' generators in `books/puzzles.py`, which already has thirteen).
- **Puzzle book.** The page mentions the printed puzzle books only once a real purchase link exists in `site.yaml` → `books` (§6s); since 29 Oct 2026 it links the Christmas Edition until 25 Dec.

## 6h. Fifty at Fifty: the challenge catalogue and the reader's own list (stage 1 added 6 Oct 2026)
`/list/` is a catalogue of things worth doing (`content/challenges.yaml`, 102 at launch), one page per activity at `/list/<id>/`, and a list each reader builds for themselves. Robin's idea: "fifty at fifty, 60 at 60". It works at any age, the reader chooses the name and the number, there is no deadline and nobody has to do them all. Everything is free. There are no accounts.
- **Renamed "Things to do" (Robin, 28 Oct 2026; it was "Get involved" from 27 Oct and "Fifty at Fifty" before that).** The menu, page title, breadcrumbs and links say "Things to do"; the home panel and the page kicker say "Have a go". The rest of this note still applies.
- **Renamed "Get involved" (Robin, 27 Oct 2026).** Readers see the feature as **Get involved** everywhere (menu, home, page title, breadcrumbs, story lines, app); the address stays `/list/` and every id, file and Plausible event keeps its old name. "Fifty at Fifty" survives only as one of the names a reader can give their own list (default now "My list"). On the home page it was a full-width panel under today's story (28 Oct 2026); since 13 Nov 2026 it leads the page instead (§6u); the tiles row below holds puzzles and Scam checker. Activity cards are solid in their topic colour (`data-cat`), the whole card opens the activity page with a "How to start and where to go →" cue, and searching shows a results bar (count and search words) that flashes and fades the cards in, scrolling to the results on Enter or a filter change (no motion when reduced motion is set).
- **They are called activities (Robin, 8 Oct 2026).** Readers see "activities", never "challenges", except in an event's own name and in the collection below. The file is still `content/challenges.yaml` and the template `challenge.html`; do not rename them or any id.

Each new one was written from its official page by one helper and every link was then re-opened by a second, independent helper before it went in; links the second pass found dead or mislabelled were removed. - **Inspiration, the map and the build-out (Robin, 27 Oct 2026).** Robin wants every activity to carry someone who has done it, wants many more activities ("much bigger, much more fun"), and wants the work spread over two weeks with quality first.
  - **Totals and groups (Robin, 20 Nov 2026, replacing the 27 Oct rule never to state the total):** Robin wants it obvious how many activities there are and that there are groups to browse. The total is shown only as the build works it out (`list_total_text`: "More than N", rounded down to a multiple of 50) on the home finder, the top of /list/ and the group pages; group counts are worked out at every build. Never type a number into a page, post or email by hand: it would go stale. Groups live in `content/groups.yaml` as rules, so new activities file themselves; one page per group at `/list/<slug>/` (collection.html), tiles at the top of /list/, chips on the home finder and at the foot of every group page. The build prints LIST WARNING for any activity in no group: fix the rule or add `groups: [key]` to the entry. A group slug is permanent once live and must not clash with an id.
  - **`inspire:` items** on an entry (validated by `build.py`): `kind: read` (person, line, title, outlet, url, checked) is an outside article or official page about a real, named person who did this activity, opened in that session; `kind: film` (person, line, id, title, channel, checked) is a YouTube film from the channel of the broadcaster, organisation or person, with the channel confirmed as in §6l (verify_videos.py checks these at deploy; the build drops any YouTube does not confirm). The `line` is our own words, one sentence, only what the page says, leading with what they did, not their age. A site story in `story:` also counts. Never imply the person endorses the site or the activity's organiser. Spread ages and places as in §1b: most people in their 40s to 60s, 80+ only when the feat is remarkable. Cards show a "Someone who did it" or "Film" badge, the activity page shows "Someone who has done it", and the list has an Inspiration filter.
  - **`places:`** only for an activity tied to one place: UK area keys (`north-east`, `north-west`, `yorkshire`, `east-midlands`, `west-midlands`, `east`, `london`, `south-east`, `south-west`, `wales`, `scotland`, `northern-ireland`) or US postal codes. The map on `/list/` colours an area once it has one; the shapes are `content/map_shapes.json`, made once by `tools/build_map.py` from Natural Earth (public domain; the credit prints under the map). Do not tag activities that can be done anywhere.
  - **Two-week plan, 28 Oct to 9 Nov 2026,** run by the scheduled task "Get involved build-out" (15:31 UK daily), separate from the daily article run. Each run: (0) turn **three** existing activities into full guides (below); (1) add **two to four new activities**, first filling grey map areas (US states and UK areas with nothing tied to them) with the best-known, genuinely worthwhile thing to do there, then the thin topics (earn, ai, tech, money, eat); (2) add **three to five inspiration items** to existing activities, starting with the most popular kinds (walks, climbs, swims, runs, skills people dream of); (3) the day's extra job below; (4) build, look at two changed pages, push, confirm the deploy. Quality over count: a weak or unsourced entry is worse than none, so add fewer rather than pad. No near-duplicates of existing activities.
    | Date | Extra job |
    |---|---|
    | 28 Oct | Photos for activities: extend `tools/photos.py` and the Photos workflow so an activity can carry `photo_file` (Commons, same licence rules and credits as §6l), and show it at the top of its card and page. |
    | 29 Oct to 1 Nov | Queue photo searches for every place-tied activity (the mountain, the trail, the place) and choose and check them as in §6l; then the best-known activities elsewhere. |
    | 2 Nov | Review: re-open a sample of a quarter of the inspiration links and new activities added since 27 Oct, fix anything wrong; run `python tools/check_links.py`. |
    | 3 to 5 Nov | Films: add `kind: film` inspiration where a film by the broadcaster or organisation can be confirmed (oEmbed or the watch page via WebFetch; otherwise leave it). |
    | 6 to 8 Nov | Keep going with activities, inspiration and photos; on 8 Nov check the topic pages and the home panel still read well with the larger catalogue. |
    | 9 Nov | Final review as on 2 Nov, then report to Robin (counts added, map areas still grey, photos added, anything held back) and disable the scheduled task. |
  - **The whole world (Robin, 27 Oct 2026: "the map should include all continents… aim for at least one activity in each country, but for interesting countries add more").** The map has a World tab of every country and Antarctica (Natural Earth 1:50m countries; `places:` takes the three-letter code shown in `content/map_shapes.json`, e.g. `JPN`, `ATA`), and `regions: [world]` marks an activity outside the UK and US (the Country filter calls it "Rest of the world"). Clicking the UK or USA on the world map opens their detailed maps. The aim is at least one activity for every country, and several for countries with a lot to offer (France, Italy, Spain, Japan, New Zealand, Canada, Australia, Iceland, Norway and the like): the classic walk, climb, journey, festival, wildlife experience or volunteering programme, always written from an official page (national park, tourist board, organiser). Countries the UK Foreign Office advises against all travel to are left until that changes. This is ongoing work, not just the two-week plan: each run adds a few countries across different continents.
  - **Found on Google, then worth reading (Robin, 27 Oct 2026: "if someone is interested in an activity they put it in Google and they find our specific page, and that page is informative and inspiring. We are not getting enough traffic").** A thin page (why, three steps, a link: about 330 words) rarely ranks. So the main work of the build-out is now **depth**: each run turns **three** existing activities into full guides, ahead of adding new ones. The model is `climb-yr-wyddfa` (about 900 words). A full guide has:
    - `seo_title` worded the way people search ("How to climb Yr Wyddfa (Snowdon): the six paths compared", "How to start lawn bowls: what to expect and where to play"), at most 60 characters, and `description` (one plain sentence, 120 to 155 characters).
    - `detail`: three to five headed sections (`h`, then `p` paragraphs, a `list` or a `table` with `head` and `rows`, and optional `after`). Cover what it is really like, the honest hard parts, what you need and roughly what it involves, how to build up to it, and the choices a beginner faces (routes, classes, kit). A comparison table wherever there are real options.
    - `faq`: four to six questions people actually type ("How long does it take…", "Do I need…", "Can I… if…"), answered in one to three sentences.
    - `sources`: every page the extra text rests on, opened that day (official bodies first). Nothing in the text that a source does not say; numbers in both metric and imperial where they matter.
    - An `inspire` item if it has none.
    Pick in this order: activities people search for most (classic UK and US mountains and trails, learning to swim, sail, ride, dance, play a sport, popular events, volunteering roles people ask about), then the rest. Never pad: a shorter honest guide beats a long vague one.
  - **Photos on activities (built 27 Oct 2026, a day ahead of the table).** An entry in `content/challenges.yaml` takes the same `photo_file`, `photo_caption`, `photo_alt`, `photo_checked` (and optional `photo_label`) as a story, under every rule in §6l. The Photos workflow now also runs when `content/challenges.yaml` changes; `tools/photos.py fetch` saves the files as `static/photos/list-<id>.jpg` and `-800.jpg` and credits them under the key `list-<id>`. Queue searches in `tools/photo_queries.yaml` under the key `list-<id>`. The build shows the photo at the top of the activity page (with caption and credit) and at the top of its card on `/list/`, and lists it on `/photo-credits/` under "On activity pages", only once `photo_checked` is set. No activity has a photo yet: the 29 Oct to 1 Nov job queues and chooses them.

  - **Depth over breadth (3 Nov 2026, Robin).** The site gets too little Google traffic, and about 311 activity pages were thin (around 140 words), with Google leaving pages "crawled, currently not indexed". So the scheduled task "Things to do: full guides" (replacing the build-out above) writes **four full guides a day** to the `climb-yr-wyddfa` standard, and new activities are paused (add one only if it is written to the full-guide standard in the same run, never instead of the four). Quality first: if only three can be done properly, do three and say why. Each guide: `seo_title` as people search (60 characters at most), `description` (120 to 155), three to five `detail` sections with at least one comparison `table` or something a reader cannot easily find elsewhere, four to six `faq` worded as people type them, `sources` each opened that day (official bodies first), today's `checked` dates on permits, prices and dates with the official page given, `safety` where there is real risk, an `inspire:` item, `related:` (below) and link terms, and photo searches queued if it has no photograph. An independent checker agent re-opens every source before the push.

  - **Breadth again, with medium pages (Robin, 7 Nov 2026, replacing the pause on new activities).** Robin: "I want more pages. More art and activities… add hundreds more. Then keep improving the content to support the planner", and the content "should focus on enabling customers to learn about the activities and get involved. Provide links to opportunities. Source opportunities in their areas. Links to related items, like art products on Amazon for painting." Robin chose **medium pages, many** over thin ones. So each full-guides run now also adds **new activities at the medium standard**, aiming for 12 to 20 a run (quality first: fewer if they cannot be sourced properly), and the four full guides a run continue.
    - **The medium standard** (model: `try-pottery-on-the-wheel`, about 450 words): every required field; `why` and three `start` steps; `links` to real opportunities, at least one UK and one US where honest (national finders, guilds, governing bodies, adult-education colleges, libraries, well-known schools), each opened that day; two `detail` sections, one of them a comparison `table` of real ways in (provider, format, cost as last listed); three or four `faq` as people type them; `sources` for every page the text rests on; `seo_title` and `description`; `kit` (Amazon UK search links, `why` says what it is for, never a quality claim); `related` (three or four, any topic, so readers move on through the site); `collection` where one fits; an `inspire` item where a real, named person can be found (otherwise leave it, never pad). Course prices and dates go in the table's "as last listed" column; never promise a date.
    - **Order:** arts, crafts and music first (collection `arts`, page `/list/arts-and-crafts/`), then sports and skills people search to learn (dance, golf, tennis, archery, climbing walls, rowing, cycling skills), then learning (languages, history, science, gardening and food courses), then volunteering roles, then the thin topics (earn, money, tech, ai, eat). No near-duplicates of existing ids. Thin existing pages may also be brought up to the medium standard.
    - **Opportunities in their area:** where a national body has a finder, link it with its region; for activities with a "Try it near you" guide, add checked listings to `content/local.yaml` per §2c. Never invent a local group.
