  - **Robin's idea held for later:** a drawn route line for mountain and trail activities. It must be our own drawing from an official route description, never copied from a map site; ask Robin before starting.
- **Collections.** An entry may carry `collection: <key>`; the keys, labels and page addresses are in `COLLECTIONS` in `build.py`. Today there is one, `military` ("Military physical challenge", page `/list/military-physical-challenges/`, 11 entries including `paras-10`). A collection page address is permanent once live. An event goes in `military` only if its official page shows the public can enter and that it is still run; the text says who organises it and what it is based on, in the page's own terms, and claims no link with the armed forces beyond that. Every one has a `safety` line.
- **Found from search (Robin: "these should drive visits").** Every activity page is in the sitemap, is indexable, has its own title ("<title>: how to start and where to go" where it fits in 60 characters), its own description and breadcrumb data. The catalogue lists all of them as plain links, each topic page links to its first twelve ("Things to do") and to `/list/?topic=<id>`, and each collection has its own page. Keep all of that when changing the templates. Do not create thin near-duplicate activities to chase search terms.
- **Never claim what is not there.** Totals come only from the build (see "Totals and groups" above); never type a total by hand and never write "hundreds" or similar. No member counts, no "people near you", no invented community activity. Doing it with others means only what exists: the invite link, the real clubs in each challenge's links, and the Facebook page.
- **An `id` is permanent.** Readers' saved lists point at it. Never change or reuse one. To retire a challenge, leave the entry out and the reader's list shows "a challenge we no longer list"; do not recycle the id.
- **Every entry:** id, title (a thing you can tick off: "Do a parkrun", not "get fitter"), category, kind, effort, cost, time, setting, company, regions, `why` (two sentences, lead with what is interesting, never with age), `start` (three concrete first steps), `links` (one to three, each fetched on its `checked` date and found to be the official event, governing body or club finder; if it cannot be verified it is left out). Optional: `season`, `safety` (only for real risk, one sentence, no lecture), `guide` and `story` (article slugs), `story_note` (one sentence shown above the story card, to say plainly how the person relates to the challenge, as on `paras-10`), `local` (a `content/local.yaml` activity). `build.py` refuses a malformed entry. No event dates or prices in the text: the official page has those. Money and earn entries are general information, not advice; the page says so. No health claims. No affiliate links unless the id is already in `site.yaml`.
- **Under stories and guides.** An article whose slug is a challenge's `guide` or `story` gets an "Add this to your list" line automatically. A `story` link must not imply the person did, ran or endorsed the challenge unless a source says so.
- **Suggestions** use the profile the reader has already given (interests, starting point) and say why each is suggested, with two from outside their interests. Without a profile there is one quiet line offering the questions; the catalogue is fully usable without answering anything. Age is not used.
- **Invite a friend.** "Do this with someone" makes a link ending `?via=friend&do=<id>`. The friend sees one slim closable line and a one-tap "Add to my list"; nothing identifies either person.
- **Where the list lives.** In the reader's browser only (`ddr_list`), and the page says so, including the iPhone caveats (Safari can clear a site's data after a period without visits; the home-screen app keeps a separate copy). "Save a backup", "Restore from backup" (a merge: it never wipes), "Email my list to myself" (their own mail app) and one gentle reminder at five items.
- **Ready for accounts (do not break this).** All reading and writing of the list goes through `static/list-store.js` (`DDRList`): page code never touches `localStorage` for the list. Data is versioned (`version: 1`); every item has a stable id (the challenge id, or `x-…` for the reader's own), `updated_at`, and a `deleted` flag instead of being removed. `merge()` is a pure function (union, newest change wins, nothing dropped) with tests: run `node tools/test_list_merge.js` after any change to the store. The accounts build replaces load/save with sync and reuses `merge()`. The app's home card only reads the list to show progress. The puzzle streak is still in its own key (`ddr_puzzle`); fold it into the same pattern when accounts are built.
- **In the app.** A "My list" card on the Today screen shows progress and opens `/list/`. It needs a connection (the app's offline cache covers `/app/` only) and the card says so.
- **Plausible events:** `list_add` (id, from), `list_done` (id, or "own"), `list_invite`, `list_invite_arrival`, `list_custom` (a count only: never the reader's words), `list_backup` (save / restore / email), `list_link`, `list_surprise`, `list_print`, and **`list_active`**: once per browser per calendar month when someone with three or more items opens the list. `list_active` is the number Robin uses to decide when accounts are worth building; report it monthly.
- **Stage 2, not built, Robin's decision:** seeing and connecting with other people doing the same challenge. It needs accounts, a database, moderation and safeguarding. Paid extras come after that; nothing free today may be locked later.

### 6w. Kit: consolidated reviews, scores and picks (Robin, 20 Nov 2026)
Robin: "use multiple reviews to select recommended products… consolidate the understanding, provide a score for products and recommend, like best according to reviews, cheapest but good, all-round value etc. The advice can be based on the findings so not the same category every time. Make searching for products on the site better than just a button to Amazon." He chose (20 Nov): our own consensus score shown (replacing the 12 Nov rule that showed only one reviewer's own label and never ranked); price bands with the month, not exact prices; a Kit finder section plus a summary box on activity pages.
- **Data:** one `content/kit/<type>.yaml` per product type (schema and research rules in the brief below): the independent reviews read (8 to 12, UK and US, editorial only, never makers, shops or forums), and for each product every review's own label (copied exactly), award (top = its overall pick or number 1, joint winners allowed; named = any other award; listed), rank, rating with scale, an optional quote of at most 20 words, `best_for` tags each backed by a review, a typical price printed in a UK review (`price_gbp`) or a US review (`price_usd`; never a converted price), our summary in our own words, `agree` points citing reviews, and an Amazon UK search link. `kit_names` ties the type to activities whose `kit:` items have those names.
- **Scores and picks:** `tools/kitscore.py`, explained to readers on `/kit/how-we-score/` (change both together). Score = 10 × (0.4 coverage + 0.3 placement + 0.3 rating). Picks need 2+ reviews: top rated, best budget pick (lowest band), all-round value (middle band), best if budget is no object, and up to three "Reviewers' choice for <tag>" where 2+ reviews back the tag. **Picks by market:** UK readers' picks come only from products at least one UK review recommends, US readers' from US reviews (body[data-region] from app.js; the reader can switch; `static/kit.js`). Scores are the same for everyone.
- **Pages:** `/kit/` (finder: search by product, brand or activity, filter by group), `/kit/<type>/` (picks, every product in 2+ reviews with score, "in n of N reviews", band, price filter, what each review said, what they agree on, the reviews read), `/kit/how-we-score/`. Activity pages whose kit matches a type show a "what N reviews recommend" box with the top three picks; it replaces the older single-reviewer picks box there. Kit appears in the main menu once any type exists.
- **Process for each type:** a research helper (brief `KIT_BRIEF.md`, kept in this section's spirit: search first, one fetch at a time) then an independent checker that re-opens every review and confirms every label, award, rank, rating, quote and price word for word; `python3 tools/kitscore.py` maths is run by the build. Re-read every type's reviews at least once a year, or when a product is replaced.
- **20 Nov 2026, first three types:** binoculars (11 reviews: 6 UK, 5 US), walking boots (10: 4 UK, 6 US; T3 counted as US because only its US edition opened), waterproof jackets (11: 6 UK, 5 US). Re-check: binoculars' Nikon 12x25 S US price ($696.95 printed by Space.com), the Moab 3 Mid GTX and Waterproof treated as one boot, Ramblers' jacket page (Robin, 20 Nov: not strictly independent, but kept because Cotswold Outdoor sells many brands; the page says so in a review `note`), Advnture's jacket page last updated October 2024. Added later on 20 Nov: trekking poles (11 reviews), head torches (10), cycle helmets (11 guides from 9 outlets; one outlet never counts twice for a product). Next types, by how often activities list them: dry bags, changing robes, merino walking socks, daypacks, insulated flasks, running shoes, compact binoculars (fold into binoculars), telescopes, sewing machines.
- **20 Nov 2026, product links higher and thumbnails (Robin: "move the product links higher up the pages, at the bottom people won't see them… add thumbnails, so the pages look less like just text").** Activity pages now put every product box (the seasonal gift section, Book it, the reviewers' picks box, Kit for this, the older picks box) in one `.fl-shop` block straight after "Someone who has done it" and before the guide sections; race-hub guides put their kit box after the first section. Every product link (activity pages, gift pages, stocking fillers, category and shop boxes, the article sidebar, /kit/ and /kit/<type>/) shows a **thumbnail**: a drawn icon of the *type* of item (boot, binoculars, head torch; a ticket for bookings), tinted with the topic colour, from `tools/kiticons.py`, which `build.py` writes as one sprite to `dist/static/kit-icons.svg` and exposes as the `kicon` filter and the `kthumb()` macro. They are our own artwork, never product photos: the house image rules allow only our art, open-licence Commons photos or written permission, and Amazon allows its product images only through the Product Advertising API (open once the Associates account has 3 qualifying sales). If that API is ever used, product photos replace the icons per product. `python3 tools/kiticons.py` lists how kit names are matched (about 13% fall back to a plain tag icon; add an icon and a rule for any common one).

### 6l. Films and photographs on stories (added 7 Oct 2026, Robin: "more photos and videos")
Stories were illustration-only. They may now carry a film and a photograph, under these rules. Both are optional and both fail safe: if anything is missing or unconfirmed the page simply shows the artwork and no film.

**Film of the person (front matter `video: {id, title, channel}`).**
- Only a film that shows the person in the story, on the YouTube channel of the broadcaster that made it, the organisation involved (a university, Guinness World Records, an event organiser) or the person's own channel. Never a re-upload, a compilation or a commentary channel.
- Confirm it the way §2 step 3 does: WebFetch `https://www.youtube.com/oembed?url=https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ID&format=json` and copy `title` and `author_name` exactly into `title` and `channel`. Never type an ID from memory.
- **It must be this person.** Where the title does not name them, the channel plus the particulars in the title (age, place, the feat) must match our sources. If that is not certain, leave the film off and say so in the report. (Left off on 7 Oct 2026 for this reason: a RunThrough film titled "Mike Barnes" that could not be tied to our Mike Barnes.) We cannot watch films, so never describe what happens in one: the caption gives the title and the channel and nothing else.
- `build.py` puts the player after the second paragraph. `verify_videos.py` checks story films with YouTube before every deploy and the build drops any it cannot confirm, so a film its owner removes disappears from our page. The card for a story with a film says "with a film".
- A film that suits the videos page as well can be in both places.

**Photograph (front matter `photo_file`, `photo_caption`, `photo_alt`, `photo_checked`; optional `photo_label`).**
- **What it may show.** The place where the story happened, the thing the story is about, or the activity. A photograph of the actual person is used only if it is on Commons under an open licence or the owner has given written permission. Never a photograph of some other identifiable person standing in for them: prefer pictures with no people, people far off, or hands and equipment only.
- **The caption says what the picture shows** (place, and the year if it matters), taken from the file's own description on Commons, and claims nothing the description and our sources do not support. Do not write "where she trains" unless a source says so. Where people are visible, add "The picture is not of <name>." `photo_alt` describes what can be seen, for readers who cannot see it.
- **How to get one.** The cloud session cannot reach Wikimedia, so GitHub Actions does it (`.github/workflows/photos.yml`, `tools/photos.py`):
  1. Put search phrases in `tools/photo_queries.yaml` (`<slug>: ["phrase", …]`) and push. The workflow writes the candidates (title, size, author, licence, description, a small preview each) to the **`photo-research` branch**, which is never deployed. Fetch it (`git fetch --depth 1 origin "+refs/heads/photo-research:refs/remotes/origin/photo-research"`), read `candidates.json` and look at the previews.
  2. Put the chosen file's exact Commons title in the article as `photo_file: "File:….jpg"`, with `photo_caption` and `photo_alt`, and push. The workflow asks the Commons API for the author and licence, refuses anything that is not public domain, CC0, CC BY or CC BY-SA (or that has no author to credit), downloads the file, saves `static/photos/<slug>.jpg` (1600px wide) and `<slug>-800.jpg`, and commits them with `content/photo_credits.yaml` and `docs/IMAGE_CREDITS.md`. Read the job's "Photos" annotation for anything refused.
  3. `git pull`, **open the downloaded file and look at it** (the Read tool shows images). Check it is what the caption says, that nobody is identifiable who should not be, and that there is no advertising, logo or artwork filling the frame. Correct the alt text to what you actually see. Only then set `photo_checked: <today>` and push. The build shows no photo without that date.
  4. Empty `tools/photo_queries.yaml` (leave the comment lines) when the choice is made.
- **Credit.** The author, the licence (linked) and "via Wikimedia Commons" (linked to the file's page) print under the picture, and again on `/photo-credits/`, which the build writes from `content/photo_credits.yaml`. Never edit that file or `docs/IMAGE_CREDITS.md` by hand.
- **Resize only.** The files are made smaller and nothing else: no cropping, retouching, text or logo on the picture. On the page the frame may trim the edges (CSS), and the sunrise mark sits on the corner as a separate element. That keeps a share-alike licence simple: we are showing the photographer's work, credited, not making a new one.
- **Where it appears.** The story page, the cards on the home page, topic pages and search, and the "Today's story" card. Since 16 Nov 2026 also on social posts, under the rules below. The Pinterest feed image stays the headline card.
- **Social posts (changed 16 Nov 2026, Robin: "Wikimedia photos can be used for social posts if it is legal. The social posts need to become more attractive").** A Commons photo may be used on Facebook, Instagram, X, YouTube and carousel slides when all of these hold:
  1. **Licence:** public domain, CC0, CC BY or CC BY-SA only, fetched and credited by the Photos workflow (`content/photo_credits.yaml`). Never NonCommercial (NC) or NoDerivatives (ND), never a file whose Commons page lists restrictions.
  2. **What it shows:** a place, object or activity, checked by eye. No identifiable person (a licence covers the photographer's copyright, not the subject's right to their own likeness or an implied endorsement), no logo, advert or someone else's artwork, painting, poster or sign filling the frame (UK freedom of panorama covers buildings and sculpture in public places, not two-dimensional artwork).
  3. **Credit on the image:** "Photo: <author> · <licence> · Wikimedia Commons (cropped)", always legible. **Credit in the caption** on Facebook and Instagram (and YouTube posts): author, licence with its link, and the Commons file page. X carries the on-image credit only.
  4. **Crop and resize only.** Text, the sunrise mark and the credit sit on the topic-colour panel beside or below the photo, never on the photo. That keeps a share-alike photo an unaltered (cropped) work shown alongside ours, rather than a new derivative.
  5. **Never imply** the photographer, or anyone in the place, endorses the site or the activity.
  - **Daily posts:** set `photo_social: true` in the article's front matter, in the same push as `photo_checked`, only after checking points 1 and 2 on the downloaded file. `build.py` then draws the photo card (`make_photo_card`) for the pack's square, story and landscape images, the Instagram card and the link-preview image (`static/img/<slug>.png`, which Facebook and X show), and adds the credit line to the Facebook and Instagram text. Only packs dated from `PHOTO_SOCIAL_FROM` (17 Nov 2026) change: older packs are never rewritten (§6d). Without `photo_social: true` the post keeps the headline card.
  - **Carousels:** same rules; a slide's photo panel carries the credit and the caption lists every photo's full credit.
  - This is our reading of the licences, not legal advice. If a photographer objects, remove the photo from social use the same day, as below.
- **Variety.** Not every story needs a photograph, and the artwork stays for the ones without. Avoid the same kind of picture twice running on the home page.
- **If a photographer objects or a licence turns out to be wrong:** remove `photo_file` from the article the same day (the next Photos run deletes the files), tell Robin, and note it in the corrections log if the picture was misleading.
- **Every run does both halves (Robin, 23 Oct 2026: "the content doesn't have enough pictures and relies on the DDR picture too much").** Photos had lapsed after 14 Oct because the search and the choice happen on different pushes. So each daily run, before its own story: (a) fetch `photo-research`, choose photos for any slugs waiting in `tools/photo_queries.yaml` and set `photo_file`; (b) pull, look at any photos downloaded since the last run and set `photo_checked` (and `photo_social: true` if it passes the social rules above, so the day's posts carry the photo); then, after writing the day's piece, (c) add its slug and 2–4 search phrases to the queue. Curious pieces almost always have a good open-licence picture (the object itself, the place, or a public-domain photograph of the person): find one. Report how many of the last 14 pieces have a photograph.
- **14 Nov 2026:** chose, downloaded and checked photos for ten activities (bonsai, hammered dulcimer, fencing (the electrical weapon tester), barbell plates, bridge bidding box, orienteering thumb compass, outdoor table tennis, coasteering at Abereiddi, paragliding, the Ridgeway). Dropped with no honest candidate: judo (twice), British Sign Language, cyanotype, sitar, viol (the Commons author field named the instrument maker, not the photographer). Requeued snorkelling and open water with new phrases; queued the Velcro piece.
- **13 Nov 2026:** chose photos for steel pan, lawn bowls, Nordic walking, tai chi, curling (old stones at Ayr Ice Rink) and kitesurfing (to check next run). Yoga dropped (no honest candidate twice); fencing and judo requeued with equipment-only searches (candidates were named athletes, children, or Brazilian jiu-jitsu). Film `uUb1qjcuI5M` (Guinness's film of Brenda Willison's record, embedded on its own news page) went in on the backstop: oEmbed refused, watch page rate-limited, title from a youtube.com search result, `channel` blank, so it is not on the story page.
- **12 Nov 2026:** photos chosen, downloaded and checked for Sarvjeet Kaur and 19 Things-to-do activities; horse riding and Nellie Bly chosen (to check next run). Steel pan refused at download (no author on Commons), requeued; yoga candidates were promotional or not a yoga room, requeued; first aid had no picture without identifiable people, dropped.
- **23 Oct 2026:** queued searches for the three Curious launch pieces and the eight stories since 16 Oct that had none.
- **14 Oct 2026:** photographs added and checked for the eight newest pieces that had an honest one (including the first two guides: stargazing and birdwatching). None found for Fiona Govan (Sivota) or Diane Morrisey; they keep their artwork.
- **State at 7 Oct 2026.** 11 of the 20 stories have a film; 18 have a photograph. No film could be confirmed for Katie Pahlow, Maurice Newman, Mike Barnes, Martine Barons, Natalie Brock, Margaret Colling, Sandra Riley, Craig Johnston or Lisa Croke. Guides have no photographs yet. Not done, Robin to decide: asking the people in the stories (or the outlets) for their own photographs, which needs a working `hello@` mailbox, and a short film loop at the top of the home page.

### 6m. AI-illustrated story videos (agreed 8 Oct 2026, Robin)
Robin does not want to appear on camera, so the short story videos may use AI-generated video to illustrate the story, under these five conditions. All five apply to every clip; if one cannot be met, the video uses the site's own drawn artwork instead.
1. **Clearly stylised** (cartoon, clay or painted look), never photo-realistic, so nobody could mistake it for real footage.
2. **Never the real person.** Any figure is generic, and neither the narration nor the captions say or imply that it is the person in the story.
3. **Never a real, named place** shown as if it were that place.
4. **Labelled as AI-made**, in the video itself (the closing card says so) and with each platform's own AI label where one exists.
5. **Facts only from the article.** The pictures illustrate the idea; they add no events the story does not contain.
- **One look, so it is recognisable:** the same written style description and reference image for every clip, the sunrise rising at the start and setting behind the web address at the end, the same voice and the same closing card.
- **Look before it goes out:** a session watches frames from every clip and checks the five conditions before the video is posted. Reject any clip with a face that resembles a real person, readable text or logos, or anything uncanny.
- **On the site:** these videos are for social platforms. Article pages keep real photographs and the site's artwork (§6l); an AI-illustrated video may appear on a story page only below the article and labelled as an AI illustration.
- **State at 8 Oct 2026:** agreed in principle; no video tool chosen and no clip made yet. A captions-only sample in the site's own artwork was made on 7 Oct (not published).

### 6o. Curious: inventions, discoveries and feats from history (added 23 Oct 2026, Robin)
Robin felt the daily stories leaned too heavily on what individuals have just achieved. Category `curious` (/curious/) is for pieces that are simply interesting: how something was invented or discovered, and truly exceptional feats from years ago.
- **Cadence.** On **Tuesdays, Thursdays and Saturdays** the day's piece is a Curious piece instead of a personal story (from Tue 27 Oct 2026). Other days are unchanged. A Curious piece counts as the day's article for the catch-up check.
- **Choosing.** Mix inventions, discoveries and historic feats; mix UK, US and the rest of the world; mix eras. Prefer the story behind something readers use or have heard of (the cat's eye, the microwave) and feats that were exceptional by any standard. Not "on this day" filler, not anything still being reported as news (that is a daily story), not war glorification, not life-extension or medical claims. People in a Curious piece may be any age and are usually dead; do not set `subject_age`.
- **Sources (stricter, because myths are common).** At least two independent sources, each opened in the session: museums, archives, national libraries, learned societies and institutes, official bodies, peer-reviewed or well-edited histories (e.g. IEEE Spectrum, Science Museum Group, Royal Museums Greenwich, Smithsonian, national inventors' halls of fame). Wikipedia is a place to find sources, never a source. When accounts differ (dates, numbers, the famous anecdote), say so in the text and say which source says what. Where a famous story is a legend, say it is a legend; that is often the best part of the piece.
- **Format.** Headline per §1a (lead with the person or the thing and what happened; no ages). Body 500–900 words, headed sections. Quotes: at most two short ones, attributed. Both "Try this" lists are required: `try_this` = see it, notice it, look it up (a museum, a place, an everyday check); `try_this_plus` = go deeper (a course, a primary source, a talk or project). Any place, opening time or booking rule in them must come from a page opened that day. No companion plan is needed (§2b applies to personal stories); the X thread wording drops "and a plan" when there is none. Set `art:` to a fitting scene.
- **Not in Real Stories.** `/stories/` and its feed list personal stories only; Curious pieces have their own page and `feed/curious.xml`.
- **Several at once.** If more than one Curious piece is published on the same day, give the later ones `social_date: YYYY-MM-DD` (one a day) so their social posts go out on separate days. It changes only the social pack's date, never the article's date.
- **State at 23 Oct 2026.** Launched with three pieces: Percy Shaw and the cat's eye, Percy Spencer and the microwave oven, Frank Worsley and the James Caird (social posts held to 24, 25 and 26 Oct).

### 6q. Revenue plan (Robin, 27 Oct 2026: "the paid-for guides won't take off; we need to be more advanced in the offering and cost less")
1. **Booking and kit links on activity pages.** `book:` (label, why, url, `aff: viator|getyourguide`) renders a "Book it" box and `kit:` (name, why, Amazon UK search url) a "Kit for this" box. Viator and GetYourGuide earn only once Robin's partner ids are in `site.yaml` → `affiliates`; app.js adds them to the links. Viator added 29 Oct 2026 (`P00328792`; app.js also adds `mcid=42383&medium=link`, Viator's standard affiliate attribution). GetYourGuide added 30 Oct 2026 (`NQBPMKU`, 8% commission; app.js adds it as `partner_id`). Both affiliate programmes now live; prefer `aff:` links on activities where people book a guided trip, course or experience. Put them on activities where people book a trip, course or experience; `why` says what it is for and what to check, never claims about readers or quality.
- **Kit boxes widened (3 Nov 2026, Robin):** 146 more activities got a `kit:` box, so 154 now carry one (walks, climbs, running, swimming and paddling, cycling, sports, nature and stars, garden and food, hobbies, travel). Items come from a shared set of plain search links; each `why` says what the item is for and what to check, never a claim about quality or readers. Two safety lines rest on pages opened that day: the Half Dome gloves (NPS Half Dome page: gloves help on the cables; take them back out) and eclipse viewers (AAS: ISO 12312-2; sunglasses are not safe). **Amazon Associates UK is live (8 Nov 2026, Robin):** the UK tracking id is `norofico-21` (Robin's Associates account, shared with Paws on the Sand); it replaced the placeholder `dontdieretired-21` everywhere. The US id is `norofico-20` (Robin, 8 Nov 2026): app.js sends US readers to amazon.com with it. Robin's US account has its own three-sales-in-180-days clock. The 180-day, three-sales clock starts when he joins, so keep kit boxes on new activities and consider a few kit-list guides early. Planner day 10 (kit at three levels) builds on these boxes.
2. **Specific, cheap challenge plans** (about £3–£4) for named challenges people search for (first three: Kilimanjaro, the Camino, the Yorkshire Three Peaks) instead of pushing the general guides, generated by the guidance engine (§6r). **Not on sale until Robin has looked at the engine's sample plans** (see the note of 27 Oct below): they must be genuinely good, so a plan is not sold until a suitably qualified person has reviewed it, and the site may say so only once that has happened (house rule on professional review).
3. Puzzle and quiz books on KDP for Christmas: the Christmas puzzle book is live on Amazon and on the site (§6s); quiz books (UK and American editions) finished 7 Nov 2026 with covers and KDP sheets, for Robin to upload (books/README.md, "Quiz books").
4. Display ads only from about 10,000 visits a month.
- **Membership removed (27 Oct 2026):** the home, plan and newsletter pages promised a £5/month "Restart Circle" and a "Second Start Plus" tier (monthly letter, ad-free site, private group) that were not running. Those promises and `/members/` are gone. Bring a membership back only when there is something real behind it.

### 6s. Selling the printed puzzle books (29 Oct 2026, Robin: the Christmas Edition is live on Amazon)
- **Where a book lives.** `site.yaml` → `books` (id, title, short, asin, `amazon` link on amazon.co.uk with the UK tag, `live_since`, `until`, `cover`). A book appears on the site only once it has a real Amazon link there; Robin confirmed the Christmas Edition's listing (ASIN `B0HPMJKZ7J`) on 29 Oct 2026. Amazon refuses our fetch tools, so we cannot read the listing ourselves: never state a price, a rating, a delivery date or a ranking. The links carry `data-aff`, so app.js sends US readers to amazon.com with the US tag (KDP paperbacks keep one ASIN across marketplaces; ask Robin to confirm the amazon.com page opens).
- **On the site.** `/puzzle-books/` (templates/puzzle-books.html; facts from `books/build_book.py` and a passing `books/verify.py`: 105 puzzles, seventeen kinds, three parts, 146 pages, 16 point), a panel at the top of `/shop/`, a line under the heading of `/puzzle/` and a third tile on the home page. The tile and the puzzle-page line show only until the book's `until` date (25 Dec 2026 for Christmas); the book page and shop panel stay.
- **Cover image.** `static/img/books/christmas.png` is the front of the cover built by `books/build_book.py xmas`, cropped from the wraparound PDF at 150 dpi. If the cover changes, rebuild it the same way.
- **Social posts.** `content/book_posts.yaml` holds the Christmas run (13 posts, 30 Oct to 15 Dec, about every four days; `until: 2026-12-15`). `python tools/book_posts.py` writes `social/<date>-<slug>/meta.json`; `build.py` draws their cards (`static/social/<slug>.png`, `static/img/<slug>.png`) and the page's link-preview image. The deploy's Buffer job sends each on its date like an article pack, so nothing else needs running, but a pack posts only if a deploy runs on its date or the day after (the daily article does that). Never change a post's text after its date. Facts only from the book; no prices, reviews or delivery promises.
- **The Christmas Quiz Book, UK and American editions (live on Amazon 18 Nov 2026; Robin sent both links).** `site.yaml` → `quiz_books` (UK B0HS44699Y, US B0HS2NR2P4, amazon.co.uk links with the UK tag; app.js sends US readers to amazon.com, same ASIN). Kept out of `books` on purpose: the puzzle-book tile, shop panel and puzzle-page line describe 105 puzzles. Shown as the `#quiz` section of `/puzzle-books/` and a shop panel, only while both editions have a link. Front covers `static/img/books/quiz-uk.png` / `quiz-us.png` are cropped from `books/build_quiz.py` cover PDFs (rebuild them if a cover changes). Social: `content/quiz_posts.yaml`, 14 posts every other day 18 Nov to 15 Dec at 20:00 UK, half of them a question from a round printed in both editions with the book's answer and note further down the caption; `tools/quiz_posts.py` writes packs (on every full build) and draws cards with both covers (fanned on the wide card). Same rules as the puzzle-book posts: facts only from the book, no prices, reviews or delivery promises, never change a post after its date. **No book posts after 15 Dec (Robin, 18 Nov 2026: orders after that may not arrive by Christmas)**: both files carry `until: "2026-12-15"` and both tools skip any later post; the 08:00 daily puzzle posts carry on. Robin has ordered printed copies and will send better material (photos, video) for the posts: when it arrives, use it for posts dated after it arrives, never rewriting a sent one. We do not know whether AI was declared on KDP for the quiz books, so the page says only that the questions were researched and written with AI and every answer checked.
- **Next books.** Add each one to `books` when Robin sends its Amazon link, give it a section on `/puzzle-books/`, and its own post run if it is seasonal.

### 6r. The guidance engine (Robin, 27 Oct 2026: "consolidate the best guidance… rank it by professionalism… look for the best-ranked and most common guidance… make the output safe, include maintaining the body")
Every training plan the site publishes or sells is generated from `engine/` (see `engine/README.md`), never written freehand.
- **Sources** (`engine/sources.yaml`) are ranked by tier: 5 official health guidance (NHS, UK CMOs, UKHSA/NaTHNaC, CDC) or an international consensus statement; 4 peer-reviewed systematic reviews, meta-analyses, position stands and large studies; 3 governing bodies, national coaching bodies and route or event authorities; 2 recognised coaches and elite athletes published by a governing body or reputable outlet; 1 general media (used to find better sources, never enough alone). A source goes in only after it was opened in that session; copy publisher, title and year from the page.
- **Statements** (`engine/guidance.yaml`) are our own words and say only what their sources say. Agreement is counted once per independent group. `python3 tools/engine.py check` ranks them: core and supported statements are used; thin and contested ones are not, and the report lists them as research to do. Where sources disagree, add `against:` and a `note:` saying how it was resolved (the higher-ranked evidence wins).
- **Plans** (`python3 tools/engine.py plan <event>`) always include: before-you-start checks, the progression rules, the week-by-week table, a "Looking after your body" section (strength and balance twice a week, recovery, sleep, bones), fuel, drink and feet, kit, on-the-day advice, a safety box with warning signs and the 999 (911) rule, and the numbered sources with their tiers. The generator refuses a plan shorter than the event's minimum and warns when the starting point would need unsafe week-to-week growth. No doses of any medicine, ever (altitude plans say to see a GP or travel clinic).
- **Every plan explains itself (Robin, 27 Oct 2026).** Statements with `use: why` (a `role`: long, easy, specific, strength, rest, cutback, pack, taper, and a one-line `short`) say why each part of a plan is there. Plans print "What each part of the plan is for" and then "Each week, and why": for every week, what changes and the sourced reason, rotating so each kind of session is explained regularly without repeating word for word. A reason is printed only if its statement is core or supported.
- **Honesty:** a plan says it was generated from the sources listed, and keeps the "not checked by a coach, physiotherapist or doctor" line until a named, qualified person has reviewed that version (house rule).
- **Growing it:** each build-out run adds three to five well-ranked sources and the statements they support, first for thin statements in `REPORT.md`, then for new kinds of challenge (half marathon and 10k, cycling sportives, open-water swims, sprint triathlon, ultra and multi-day trail, Nordic walking, rowing, strength for older adults), each with an event profile. Run `check` before committing; it must pass.
- **18 Nov 2026, full-guides run:** 93 sources, 89 statements (67 core, 22 supported, 0 thin). Added the NHS page Heat exhaustion and heatstroke (last reviewed 28 May 2026, tier 5): it now backs `multiday-heat` and a new core safety statement `heat-warning-signs` (cold drinks while active, light loose clothing, out of the sun 11am to 3pm; the 999 (911) signs), limited by `applies` to multiday plans. The CDC heat pages were not reached (budget). Sample plans regenerated.
- **11 Nov 2026, second full-guides run:** 73 sources, 85 statements (61 core, 24 supported, 0 thin). Added the Cochrane review of exercise to prevent falls in community-dwelling older people (2019, 108 trials; tier 4, PubMed abstract page), WHO's Falls fact sheet (21 October 2026, tier 5) and NHS Every Mind Matters on sleep (tier 5). New core statement `falls-exercise` (balance and functional strength, progressed and kept up; tai chi another option; falls cut by about a quarter, about a third for combined programmes); `sleep-routine` now core. Note: WHO's sheet cites the same Cochrane review for its figure, so the two groups are not fully independent; the statement is core on tier 4 and 5 backing alone. Sample plans regenerated.
- **11 Nov 2026, full-guides run:** 70 sources, 84 statements (59 core, 25 supported, 0 thin). Sample plans in `engine/plans/` are drafts for Robin.

### 6s. My year: the planner (Robin, 28 Oct 2026; build started 1 Nov 2026)
Robin's idea: a reader picks things to do this year, gives each a date or a season, and the planner builds a get-ready plan backwards from that date, with a step and the reason for it, on one timeline that drops into their own calendar and the app's Today screen. In his words it is "an enabler for enjoying life", **not a fitness site, but not moved away from fitness either**: a typical month holds one fitness goal and many other things (a course, a day out, a festival, a weekly habit). Language: "get ready" and "things to look forward to", not "train" and "challenges", except for a physical event's training plan. The plan document is `DDR-My-Year-Planner-Plan.docx` (sent to Robin 28 Oct 2026).
- **Planner Plus, paid only (Robin, 7 Nov 2026; replaces "free first, Plus later").** The planner is a paid product, **Planner Plus**: £2 a month or £20 a year (prices include any VAT due), plus a £20 one-year gift for Christmas. There is no free planner. Non-members see one full worked **sample plan** (and what Plus does), never a plan for themselves. See §6t for the scope, the build and the rules. Robin's words: the planner is "absolutely to help the customer make the most of the year. It is not a training guide, but training guides are part of it." The earlier rule "nothing free may be locked later" is withdrawn for the planner only, because it was never launched or promoted; it still holds for the list, stories, guides and everything else free on the site.
- **Where the settings live.** `content/planner.yaml`: one plan shape per `kind` (label, default `lead_weeks`, the strands in order: book, learn, train, travel, kit, and why), and `seasons`, our reading of every `season` phrase in `content/challenges.yaml` as best months. `content/dates.yaml`: things that happen at the same time every year (months, an optional `next` date with its source, the page it came from, re-checked yearly). `tools/planner.py` checks both and every activity at each build and writes `dist/planner/activities.json` (what the planner pages read); `python3 tools/planner.py` prints a summary.
- **Planning fields on an activity (all optional):** `when: [months]` (overrides the season reading; `[]` = any time), `lead_weeks` (overrides the kind's default; never below an engine event's minimum), `plan_event` (an `engine/events` id, only when the event's own `activity:` does not already point here). The build refuses a malformed one.
- **When writing or editing activities (including the scheduled build-out):** if you give an activity a `season` phrase that is not yet in `content/planner.yaml` → `seasons`, add it there in the same commit with its best months (the build prints a LIST WARNING until you do). Put anything with a fixed yearly date in `content/dates.yaml` from its official page.
- **Training only from the engine (§6r).** The train strand appears only on activities tied to an engine event, and its length is the engine's. The planner never adds sessions to make room for other plans; it only moves flexible steps and says why.
- **Money:** Book, Travel and Kit carry disclosed affiliate links only where a reader would book or buy anyway; non-paying options (local associations, adult education) are linked just the same. "On sale" is never claimed without a live price.
- **Build log.** Day 1 (1 Nov): `content/planner.yaml`, `content/dates.yaml` (2 entries: Edinburgh Fringe, National Book Festival), `tools/planner.py`, the check in `build.py`, `plan_event: marathon` on `marine-corps-marathon`. Five activities have an engine training plan; 79 have best months, 233 are any time.
- **Day 2 (2 Nov): the generator.** `static/planner.js` (`DDRPlan`, pure, UMD like `list-store.js`): `build(activity, settings, {date | month, today})` → `{status: ok|too_soon|past, date, label, notes[], safety, steps[]}`; each step `{id, date, strand, text, detail?, why, links[], items?, flexible}`; `suggest(activity)` gives this year's fixed date, else the start of the next best month that leaves the full run-up. Settings is `dist/planner/activities.json` itself (it now carries `steps` and `notes` from `content/planner.yaml`, plus `place_tied`, `start_at` and the engine event's `prerequisite`). Tests: `node tools/test_planner.js` (runs every real activity at seven kinds of date and checks every word is sourced, order, nothing before today, every safety line kept, engine minimum never broken); look at a plan by eye with `node tools/plan_sample.js <id> <YYYY-MM-DD|YYYY-MM|suggest> [today]` after a build.
  - **How a plan is laid out (decisions):** the reader's date is the day itself (an experience or event), or the first session / the day you begin (a course, habit or project); a month means "ready by the 1st". The activity's three `start` steps are the backbone: first on day one of the plan (with its official links), second a week on, the last on the day for a one-off afternoon or a habit, after the start for a longer course or project, otherwise mid run-up. Where that reads wrongly, set `start_at: [first, second, day]` (names from `steps`) on the activity; done for the Yorkshire Three Peaks. Then: `dates.yaml` → "Check this year's dates" with its source; `book:` → "Compare and book"; the engine → one "Start the N-week training plan" step (day 6 expands it); place-tied → "Sort out getting there"; `kit:` → two weeks before (after the first session for a course); `safety` → the day before, and on the plan itself; habits get "four weeks in: look back", projects of months or a year "a month in: take stock".
  - **Training rules in code:** no train step without an engine event; its length is the engine's default, or the weeks left if fewer but never below `min_weeks`; below that the plan is `too_soon` with **no steps at all** and offers the earliest date that works. With an engine plan, an activity's own start step about training (train/build up/back-to-back) is not dated separately; it is shown with the engine step (Kilimanjaro's "speak to your GP about altitude" is kept this way).
  - **Run-ups:** kinds now have `lead_weeks_local`, the shorter run-up for a one-off afternoon not tied to a place or a fixed date (experience 2, event 4, skill 3, project 1 week), so a climbing-wall taster does not get six weeks of notice. A short run-up squeezes flexible steps towards today and says so; a date outside the best months gets a note naming the season line it came from.
  - **Found and fixed:** `volunteer-with-be-my-eyes` had an unquoted colon in its third start step, so YAML read it as a dictionary and the live page printed `{'Stay logged in': '…'}`. Quoted it; `tools/planner.py` now refuses any start step that is not plain text.
  - Nothing new for readers to see yet: the pages come on day 3. For day 4: plans will need storing through `DDRList` (planned items already carry `date`; add the plan's `month`, step ticks by step id, and moved-step dates, all inside the versioned data and `merge()`).
- **Day 3 (3 Nov): "Plan this" on every activity page.** A "Plan this" button beside "Add to my list" jumps to a Plan this box (after the safety line on the page): the reader picks "On a day" (date) or "Some time in a month" (the next 25 months), filled in from `DDRPlan.suggest()` with the reason (`notes.suggest_fixed|season|any` in `content/planner.yaml`; `suggest()` now returns `why`). "Show my plan" draws the steps grouped by month on a timeline: date, strand tag (Book, Get ready, Train, Getting there, Kit, Stay safe, The day, Afterwards), the step, its detail, its links (UK/US tags), kit, and "Why then:". A too-soon engine date shows the note and a "Use <earliest date>" button, never a plan. "Save this plan to my list" stores it; a saved plan reopens on the page with "Change the date" and "Remove this plan" (the item stays on the list, planned goes back to want). Code: `static/plan-page.js` (page only), data embedded per page as `#pl-data` (the activity's planner entry plus `steps` and `notes`; the 295 KB `activities.json` is not loaded on activity pages).
  - **Storage (`DDRList`, still version 1, additive):** items gain `month` (YYYY-MM) and `plan_made` (YYYY-MM-DD); a date and a month never both (a date wins in `clean()`; a date set on the list clears the month). `DDRList.plan(cid, {date}|{month}|null, today)` and `DDRList.get(id)`. Steps are NOT stored: they are rebuilt with today = `plan_made`, so a saved plan does not shift; steps before the reader's today show "Gone by"; a passed date shows `notes.saved_past`. 13 new tests in `tools/test_list_merge.js` (26 in all).
  - **The list page:** each catalogue row's details now has "Plan this →" or "See the plan →" (to `/list/<id>/#plan`) and shows a planned month; print and email include it.
  - **Affiliate links in plans:** `DDR.affUrl(href, kind)` in `static/app.js` applies the same Amazon/Viator/GetYourGuide ids as the static links; a plan with any shows `notes.affiliate`.
  - **Decisions:** the plan's safety line is its dated "Before you go" step (the page's own "Worth knowing" box stays above), not repeated at the foot of the plan. The `dates` step's reason no longer says "the official page", because the Edinburgh Fringe source in `dates.yaml` is Britannica: it now tells the reader to check the organiser's own site and says the link is where we read when it usually happens. Replace that source with the Fringe Society's own page when a session can open it (day 8). No Plausible events yet (day 12).
  - **For the day 7 checkpoint:** the Marine Corps Marathon has a fixed date each autumn but no `dates.yaml` entry, so it is suggested like an any-time activity; month plans for a course put the third start step ("complete a full season…") two weeks after the 1st, which reads early; the train step says "Start the N-week training plan" with nothing to open yet (day 6).
- **Day 4 (4 Nov): the My year page, `/my-year/`.** All of a reader's plans together. "The year" shows a card per month from this one (at least twelve, further if a plan is later): the days to look forward to (a month plan reads "Some time this month"), how many get-ready steps fall in that month and how many are still to do, and "See <month>". "Month by month" shows every step of every plan in that month on one timeline (each with its activity's name linking to its plan, its links, kit and "Why then:"), with Previous/Next month; a month plan heads its month. Each step has a **Done** tick, and each flexible step not yet gone by has **Move this step** (a date picker held inside its own run-up, or after the day for a look-back; "Put it back" undoes it). A "Worth a look" box flags **clashes**: two or more things on the same day, and two engine training plans that overlap (with the dates); each has "Change the date for …" links to the activity's plan. The planner **moves flexible steps itself**, and says so on the step ("Moved" tag and the reason): off the day of another of the reader's plans, and off a day where two or more plans have more than `busy_day` (3, `content/planner.yaml`) steps; to the nearest free day inside the step's own run-up. Never moved: training, safety, booking dates, the start step, the day itself, anything ticked, anything the reader moved, anything gone by. List items with a date but no saved plan (and the reader's own items) show as the day only, with "Plan this". Items with no date are counted at the foot with a link to the list.
  - **Code:** `static/planner.js` gains `combine(entries, settings, {today})` (pure: rebuilds each saved plan with today = `plan_made`, applies the reader's ticks and moves, flags clashes, moves flexible steps), `applyMarks()` and `window()` (where a step may move). `static/my-year.js` (page only) reads `/planner/activities.json` (354 KB, 85 KB compressed; loaded on this page only) and the list through `DDRList`. Words: `content/planner.yaml` → `notes.moved_you|moved_day|moved_busy|clash_day|clash_train` and `busy_day`; `tools/planner.py` checks them. Template `templates/my-year.html`; styles at the end of `static/style.css` (`.my-*`).
  - **Storage (`DDRList`, still version 1, additive):** a saved plan's item gains `ticks` `{stepId: {done, at}}` and `moves` `{stepId: {date, at}}` (`''` = back where the plan put it), via `DDRList.tick(id, step, done)` and `DDRList.shift(id, step, date)`. They belong to one plan: changing the date or month (on the plan or the list) or re-planning clears them. `merge()` unions them step by step (newest `at` wins) when both copies are the same plan (same date, month and `plan_made`), so ticking different steps on two devices loses neither, and never carries ticks from an old plan into a new one. Tests: 10 new in `tools/test_list_merge.js` (36 in all), 8 new in `tools/test_planner.js` (21 in all, including 150 random mixes of real activities: no step lost, only flexible steps move, never across their own day, same input same output).
  - **Links in:** a saved plan on an activity page now says "See it with your other plans in My year" (opens that month); the My list tab shows "See your plans together, month by month, in My year" when anything has a date or month. **Not in the menu, the sitemap or search yet** (`noindex`): it is empty for anyone without plans; add it to the menu and sitemap at launch (day 14).
  - **Decisions:** clashes are flagged, never fixed for the reader (only they know whether two things on one day is a mistake). Two training plans at once are flagged as a fact ("each plan was built on its own"), not as health advice. Months in the past are reachable with Previous but the year view starts at this month. Ticks are per step and stay on the device like the list.
  - **Left for later:** the activity page's own plan view does not yet show ticks or moves made in My year (it shows the plan as made; `DDRPlan.applyMarks()` is ready for it, small job for day 5 or 6). Ticking a step does not redraw the page, so its "Move this step" stays visible until the next redraw (harmless). The page has no Plausible events yet (day 12).
- **Day 5 (5 Nov): Add to my calendar.** A saved plan on an activity page has **Add to my calendar**; My year has **Add every plan to my calendar** (below the month or year view). Each saves a calendar file (`.ics`, made in the browser: nothing is sent anywhere) with one all-day entry per step still to do: ticked steps and steps gone by are left out, moved steps sit on their new day (My year's file includes the planner's own moves; the activity page's includes the reader's own ticks and moves only, as that page has no other plans to weigh). A month plan's day is an entry on the 1st reading "<title> (some time in <Month Year>)".
  - **What an entry holds:** summary "<activity>: <step>" (the day itself is just the title); description = the step's detail, kit (name, why, link), links (with UK/US), "Why then:", any moved reason, and a link back to the plan page. Words around the step's own: `content/planner.yaml` → `notes.cal_step|cal_day_month|cal_why|cal_plan|cal_help` (checked by `tools/planner.py`). Code: `DDRPlan.ics(groups, settings, {today, stamp, site, name})` in `static/planner.js` (pure; RFC 5545: CRLF, lines folded at 75 octets without splitting a character, text escaped), buttons in `static/plan-page.js` and `static/my-year.js`.
  - **Decisions:** links in the calendar are written **plain, without affiliate tags** (no disclosure can sit beside them there; the plan page carries the tagged links and the disclosure). **No alarms (VALARM):** the planner does not promise reminders; the reader's calendar decides. UIDs are stable per activity and step (`ddr-<activity>-<step>@dontdieretired.com`), so calendars that honour UIDs update rather than copy when the file is added again; the help line says plainly that the calendar does not follow later changes and some apps keep both copies. Get-ready entries are marked free (TRANSP:TRANSPARENT), the day itself busy. No calendar-app menu paths are given (Google's help page could not be opened today), only "open it with your calendar app, or use the app's import option".
  - **Also done (left over from day 4):** the activity page's saved plan now shows the reader's ticks (Done, struck through) and moves (Moved, with the reason) made in My year, via `DDRPlan.applyMarks()`.
  - **Tests:** 4 new in `tools/test_planner.js` (25 in all): every real activity's file is well formed with every line ≤ 75 octets, one event per step, unique UIDs, every detail, safety line, link and kit item present, no affiliate tag; summaries from `planner.yaml`; escaping; ticks left out and moves honoured; Unicode folding; My year's combined file keeps the automatic moves. `node tools/test_list_merge.js` unchanged (36; no storage change today). The files from a real browser run were parsed with Python's `icalendar`.
  - **Not checked:** opening the file on an iPhone (Safari saves a blob `.ics` to Downloads or offers it to Calendar depending on version) and importing into Google Calendar and Outlook by hand. Worth Robin trying once on his phone. No Plausible event yet (`plan_ics` is day 12).
- **Day 6 (6 Nov): the engine's training as dated sessions.** A plan for an activity with an engine event (ten of them) and a **day** now has every session of the engine's plan as a dated step, not just "start the N-week plan". On the activity page the sessions are drawn **a week at a time** ("Week 3 of 12: an easier week", the week's dates, its lead line, then "The 5 sessions this week, and why" opening each session with its date, words, detail and "Why:", and the engine's reasons for the week). The "Start the plan" step carries the engine's draft line ("not yet checked by a coach, physiotherapist or doctor"), the starting point, the week pattern, **"Stay safe while you train" (every engine safety line, shown open)**, and under "About this training plan": before you start, what each part is for, and the sources (only those whose statements the plan shows, all a subset of the engine plan's own list). My year shows the same week blocks with a **Done** box per session; the year cards count steps and training sessions separately; the calendar file has one entry per session still to do, reason labelled "Why:".
  - **Where the words come from:** `tools/engine.py` now has `weekly()` (the week-by-week plan worked out once; `plan()` prints the markdown from it, and every sample plan regenerated byte-for-byte identical apart from the date line, checked for all events at all three levels, default and minimum length) and `export(event)`, which `tools/planner.py training()` turns into `dist/planner/training/<event>.json` (25–72 KB each, strings stored once; loaded only by a page whose plan trains). Day names are left as `{d0}…{d6}` so the planner can put the key session on the reader's own day of the week: the engine's Saturday key session lands on the reader's weekday, its Monday and Friday rest days stay the fifth and first day before it, and the engine's own sentences ("Rest on Monday and Friday", "Tuesday's easy run") name the real days. No citation numbers in the planner; the sources are listed instead.
  - **Starting level (new):** "Where are you starting from?" on the Plan this form for these activities, three choices described by the engine's own first key session ("Starting out (the plan's first long hill walk: 2 hours)"), default **active** (the engine's own default), with the event's "before this plan" line under it. Stored as `level` on the list item (`DDRList.plan(cid, {date, level})`; additive, still version 1; part of the plan's identity, so changing it clears ticks and moves; `MAX_STEPS` for marks raised from 80 to 300 because a 20-week plan has about 100 sessions).
  - **"Choose more weeks" (decision):** where the engine warns that its default length would make the long session grow faster than 20 per cent a week from a starting point (Yorkshire Three Peaks, Camino, 10k, half marathon, open water, sportive, triathlon, Inca Trail, Whitney, for "starting out"), the engine's own advice is to choose more weeks, so `export()` also gives the shortest length, up to a year, that needs no warning (`full`), and the planner uses it when the date allows (e.g. Three Peaks from "starting out": 17 weeks, and the plan says why). If the date leaves fewer, the plan keeps the engine's warning and says which date gives the whole plan, with a "Use <date>" button. Never below the engine's minimum, as before.
  - **Month plans:** no sessions (the taper has to end on the day itself); the plan shows when to start and says so (`notes.train_month`). If the training file cannot be loaded, the same, with `notes.train_offline`.
  - **Found and fixed:** the engine event's "before this plan" line (e.g. "If you cannot yet run for 30 minutes… do the NHS Couch to 5K first") never reached the planner: `tools/planner.py` read it but did not pass it on, so the train step's detail was empty. It is now on the train step and under the level choice, with a test.
  - **Tests:** `node tools/test_planner.js` 30 (new: every engine activity, every level, every length from the minimum to `full`, the date on each day of the week: sessions inside their week, key session on the reader's weekday, none on a rest day, none on or after the day, every word one of the engine's strings; levels and "choose more weeks"; month and offline; My year at a saved level and the calendar). `node tools/test_list_merge.js` 40 (level stored, cleaned, part of the plan in a merge; 100 ticks kept).
  - **Words added** to `content/planner.yaml` → notes: `level_*`, `train_month`, `train_offline`, `train_week`, `train_about`, `train_level_long`, `train_level_short`, `cal_why_train` (checked by `tools/planner.py`).
  - **For the day 7 checkpoint:** the engine's event week says "two short, easy outings early in the week" but its table has one (Tuesday, 20 minutes; Thursday rest); it is the engine's own wording and is shown as is: worth fixing in the engine (§6r) on a build-out run. A Sunday event puts strength on the Monday before and Thursday, rest on Tuesday and Saturday: the pattern is kept, not re-thought. Sessions cannot be moved (training is never flexible, §6s), so a reader who cannot train on a given day has no way to say so yet. The plan page is long for a 17-week plan even with weeks folded; consider showing only the next few weeks open by default.
- **Superseded 7 Nov 2026 by §6t (Planner Plus).** Days 1–6 below were built; days 7–14 are folded into the §6t table.

### 6t. Planner Plus: the paid planner (Robin, 7 Nov 2026)
**What it is.** A paid planner that helps a member make the most of their year: any activity on the site (a course, festival, day out, trip, habit or physical event) or their own, put on one calendar with everything needed to do it well. Training plans from the engine (§6r) are one part, for physical events only. Target: **open for sale, including gifts, by 25 Nov 2026, and no later than 1 Dec.**

**"Plan this" on every activity page: two choices (Robin, 7 Nov 2026).**
1. **Plan it yourself (free):** the page lists the links needed to do it (book it, getting there, where to stay, places to eat, things to do nearby), built from the activity's place, with disclosed affiliate links where we have them, so a free reader's clicks can still earn. No dated plan, no calendar, nothing saved.
2. **Plan it with Planner Plus (paid):** opens the planner (the dated get-ready plan, the trip sections below, Add to my year, one calendar on any device) with the option to pay £2 a month or £20 a year. Non-members see the sample plan and the price before paying.
The same rules apply to both: links only where honest, no invented recommendations, affiliate disclosure beside the links.

**For each thing in a member's year, the plan gives:**
1. **Get ready:** what the existing planner does (§6s): booking dates, start steps, kit, safety, engine training where there is one.
2. **Where:** the activity's place (`places`) or, for anything not tied to one place, a town, venue or postcode the member types. Everything below hangs off this.
3. **Getting there:** journey-planner links for that place and day (UK: National Rail and Traveline; US: Amtrak and the local transit finder; everywhere: a map directions link from the member's home town). Affiliate train or travel links only once Robin has joined a scheme and put the id in `site.yaml` → `affiliates`.
4. **Where to stay:** for anything away from home (more than a set distance, or over a night), hotel search links for that town and those dates (affiliate once Robin has joined a hotel scheme), plus non-paying options where they exist (YHA, campsites, the venue's own list).
5. **Where to eat:** a map search for places to eat near the venue. **Never name or recommend a restaurant we have not checked** (no invented recommendations); a named place appears only from an official or venue page opened in a session.
6. **While you're there:** other Things to do in the same area (same `places` key), plus a Viator / GetYourGuide search for that town (affiliate, disclosed). Each has **Add to my year**, which plans it on a free day of the same trip.
7. **One calendar** on the member's account, on any device, with a live calendar feed they subscribe to once (Plus only), as well as the .ics download.

**Money from links (Robin, 7 Nov 2026):** the planner should earn from the events, travel, stays and tours it links to, as well as subscriptions. Rules as §6q: disclosed affiliate links only where a member would book anyway; non-paying options shown just the same; no "best", "on sale" or prices we have not checked live; no affiliate tags inside calendar files.

**Payments: Stripe** (Robin's choice, 7 Nov 2026). Stripe Checkout for the monthly and yearly subscriptions and the one-off gift; Stripe's Customer Portal for cancelling and changing card; a webhook sets membership. Build and test in **test mode** first; Robin switches to live. The Stripe account currently connected is named "Paws on the Sand" (both sites belong to Norifico Ltd): ask Robin whether to use it (and what customers see on their statement) or open a separate account for DDR before going live. **Tax:** Stripe does not act as merchant of record, so VAT and sales tax are Norifico's to handle. Ask Robin to check with his accountant before launch, in particular selling to EU customers; until he answers, sell to UK and US buyers only. Say only general information about tax; never advise.

**Accounts.** A small server on Robin's Cloudflare account (a Worker with a D1 database): sign-in by an emailed link (no passwords), membership status from Stripe, and the member's list and plans stored through the existing `DDRList` data and `merge()` (§6h "Ready for accounts"; never bypass it). Plans already saved in a browser stay theirs: on first sign-in they merge into the account; a non-member with plans saved before 7 Nov keeps seeing them read-only. Store as little personal data as possible (email, Stripe customer id, the list); update `/privacy/` in the same commit that starts collecting anything.

**Gifts.** A £20 one-off payment gives a code and a printable card (PDF) to put under the tree. Redeeming it gives 12 months of Plus with **no automatic renewal**; near the end the member is asked whether to subscribe. Codes are single-use and the card says how to redeem.

**Honesty (house rules apply).** Nothing is sold until it works end to end in test mode and Robin has tried it himself. The sales page describes only features that exist on launch day; anything later is labelled "coming". No member counts, reviews or "most popular". Cancelling must be as easy as subscribing. Subscription terms, the 14-day cancellation right for UK consumers and what happens when access starts straight away, and refunds go on a plain-English terms page before launch (general information; Robin to have it checked if he wishes).

**What only Robin can do (tell him exact steps when the day comes):** decide on the Stripe account and statement name; switch Stripe to live; ask his accountant about VAT/EU sales; join a hotel and a train/travel affiliate scheme and send the ids; sign up for an email-sending service for sign-in emails and add its DNS records in Cloudflare; create a Cloudflare API token for the Worker and add it as a GitHub secret; join Amazon Associates (still pending from 3 Nov); put the Cloudflare account on Workers Paid before accounts open (the calendar feed needs more than the free plan's CPU, day 17); turn off Resend's click and open tracking and Cloudflare Web Analytics' automatic injection (day 20).

**Robin, 11 Nov 2026: "This should be available as a Christmas gift. Prioritise it. Then it will need more content."** So: (1) everything the gift needs to go on sale comes before anything else (row 19's gift page and terms, then row 20's end-to-end test with the gift path first, row 21, Robin's try-out, row 22); the Today screen (row 18) moves after launch. The gift card is printed at home, so it suits last-minute buyers; gift posts (row 22) should go out in good time before Christmas. (2) After launch, the next work is content: the planner's winter months are thin (month-bound activities: about 9 to 11 each in Nov to Feb against 50 to 60 in Jun to Aug), only 2 activities have fixed yearly dates in `dates.yaml` and none has `where:` place data yet; start with the backlog's winter events (Up Helly Aa, Celtic Connections, Burns Night, Christmas markets, Hogmanay, seal pups, murmurations, northern lights), then `where:` and dates for the engine events and festivals. All of it needs official pages opened in the run.
**Robin, 11 Nov 2026 (live session), ideas for after launch, not yet decided or built:** (a) "connect with others doing the event, forum and individual chat" on the planner; (b) "for activities where products can be proposed: the best option, a good option and the cheapest good option, with reviews and links for sales"; (c) more content that makes life fun, interesting, satisfying and therapeutic (cooking, restaurants, Michelin, bread; rowing the Atlantic, Devizes to Westminster, a culinary college course, "flash dance"). Notes for whoever picks these up: (a) people talking to each other puts the site under the UK Online Safety Act's duties for user-to-user services (risk assessment, moderation, reporting) and conflicts with "asynchronous by design" only for live chat; a lighter first step is linking each event's own official community, clubs and training days. (b) house rules forbid our own "best" and invented reviews: the honest version is three price tiers taken from named independent reviewers' picks (opened that day, credited and linked), with the reviewer's words, never ours, and affiliate links disclosed. Both need Robin's go-ahead on that shape before building. (c) Five pages were added on 11 Nov (below in the day 17 log's follow-up); "flash dance" is waiting on Robin saying what he meant.
**Robin, 12 Nov 2026 (live session), decisions:** (1) **Reviewers' picks: approved** ("use, quote and reference independent reviewers"). Built: `picks:` on an activity (`item`, `why`, `options` of `tier` top|good|value, `name`, `label` = the reviewer's own words, `quote` ≤30 words copied from the review, `reviewer`, `review {title, url, checked}`, `buy` = Amazon UK search for that product), validated by `build.py` (no prices in quotes) and shown as "<item>: what reviewers recommend" (Top pick, Also recommended, Lower-cost pick; the reviewer's label and quote; Read the review; Find it on Amazon; disclosure that the words are the reviewers' and we have not tested them). The tier must be the reviewer's own designation (no overall pick on the page: no Top pick). Independent = an editorial publication comparing many brands, never a maker or shop. WebFetch gives summaries, so a checker must confirm each quote word for word. First three: `try-rowing-indoor` (Live Science), `sourdough-from-starter` (Woman & Home), `learn-the-ukulele` (Guitar World). Next: every activity with a `kit:` box where one thing matters most (walking boots, bikes, wetsuits, telescopes, binoculars, sewing machines, paints), a few per Things-to-do run, re-checked each spring. (2) **Connecting members: do the light version now and build on-site connections in the medium term.** Now: each event activity links its own official communities, clubs and training days. Medium term (after launch, early 2027): connections inside the site (a discussion space per event or activity, then member-to-member messages), which first needs an Online Safety Act risk assessment, reporting and blocking, moderation rules and terms; plan it as its own row set. (3) "Flash dance" meant a flash mob: `dance-in-a-flash-mob` added.
**Two build runs a day from 7 Nov 2026** (Robin raised his usage plan and wants it used): the rows are a sequence, not fixed dates. Each run does the next unfinished row, so the build can get ahead of the dates. Launch as soon as every row before it is done, Robin has tried it and his own steps (Stripe live, accounts) are in place, and not before. Dates may slip by a day or two; the launch date is the one to protect, and quality comes before it. If a day cannot be done honestly or well, do less and say why.

**Planner Plus build log.**
- **Day 7 (7 Nov, checkpoint; no code changed).** Five real plans saved through `DDRList.plan()` on a served `dist/` and photographed as a member would see them, at 390px and 1280px (no sideways scroll, no console errors): beekeeping (month, March 2027), an open garden (day out, Sun 22 Aug 2027), the Great North Run (engine half marathon, Sun 12 Sep 2027, level active: 6 steps and 56 sessions from 20 Jun), the Edinburgh Fringe (Sat 14 Aug 2027) and Rock Choir (month, August 2027), so August 2027 in My year combines a fitness goal with a festival away, a day out and a new weekly habit. The 12 Sep GNR date was a sample pick, not a sourced date. A mock-up (screenshot only, never committed) showed Robin where the trip sections go. Sent to Robin with the list below. **Decisions:** the trip sections sit as one "Your trip" panel under the day itself (Where, Getting there, Where to stay, Where to eat, While you're there, then the disclosure), and the "Sort out getting there" step points to it; on My year the panel opens from the day. **Found, to fix in the rows named:**
  1. **Places are too coarse for trips (day 8).** Only 82 activities have `places`, and they are regions (`scotland`, `london`, `DC`), not towns. "Where" needs a town and, where there is one, a venue or postcode, from the official page. Add a `where:` field (town, venue, country, optional postcode) to place-tied activities, starting with those that have a fixed date or an engine event; everything else asks the member.
  2. **Ballot and programme lead times (day 8 or 11).** The GNR plan's first step (20 Jun for a Sep run) says to sign up for a reminder of the next ballot, then "Enter the ballot" a week later: the plan's book steps hang off the 12-week training run-up, but a ballot event needs its own booking date, months earlier. The GNR also has no `dates.yaml` entry, so `suggest()` offers 30 Jan 2027. Add its yearly date and its ballot or entry window to `dates.yaml` from the official page, and date book steps from that window. The Fringe plan books shows on 29 May; check from the Fringe Society's own page when the programme and box office open and date the step from it (also replaces the Britannica source, as noted on day 3).
  3. **Start-step placement reads wrongly in three plans (small fix, day 8).** Fringe: "Reserve your train and somewhere to stay well ahead" prints on the day itself (set `start_at` so it lands with "Sort out getting there"). Open garden: "Pick a garden that is open on a day you are free" comes after the member has already chosen the day (reword in the activity, or `start_at`). Beekeeping (month plan): "handle bees at the apiary" falls on 28 Dec, outside the bee season the activity itself names, and "complete a full season with a mentor" sits two weeks after the start; it belongs at "a month in" or as an afterwards step.
  4. **Safety step for a month plan** says "the day before" and lands on the last day of the previous month; for a month plan say "before you start".
  5. **The kind's intro line** on an event that is not physical (the Fringe) still says "Physical events add a training plan"; show that sentence only when the activity has an engine event.
  6. **Trips do not talk to training (day 10).** The Fringe day (Sat) sits in GNR week 8 with the 70-minute long run the next morning; the planner raises no flag because it only looks at same-day clashes. When an away trip covers a training day or the day before a key session, add a "Worth a look" note (as a fact, not advice).
  7. **Engine wording, for an engine build-out run (§6r):** the strength session's reason says "keeps you steady on rough ground" in a road half marathon; the event week still says "two short, easy outings" with one in the table (noted day 6).
  8. **Length:** a 12-week plan is about 4,100px on a desktop and 6,400px on a phone even with weeks folded. On day 15 (the Plus plan page) show the next four weeks open and the rest as one line each.
- **Day 8 (7 Nov, second run): Where it is and getting there.** Under the day itself, on the activity's plan and (as a fold-out from the day) in My year, a **"Where it is and getting there"** panel: **Where** (the activity's own sourced place, or the town, venue or postcode the member types: "Where will you do it?"), **See it on a map**, then **Getting there**: Google Maps directions by public transport and by car (no origin in the link, so Google asks or uses the device's location: the member's home is never in our links), plus National Rail Enquiries and Traveline for Great Britain, Amtrak for the US. The line above them says to put in the plan's date. The "Sort out getting there" step now says the links are with the day.
  - **Shared code (for day 15's free "plan it yourself"):** `DDRPlan.trip(activity, settings, {place, when})` in `static/planner.js` (pure) builds both sections from `content/planner.yaml` → `trip:` (the words, and `links:` with `{q}` the only placeholder, `countries` any / GB / US, `section` where / getting); `country_q` adds "UK" or "USA" to a typed place when the activity is in one country only ("Bath" finds Somerset, not Maine). Every link is a search the reader runs; no affiliate travel ids yet (none in `site.yaml`); none go into calendar files. `tools/planner.py` checks the `trip:` block and passes it; `build.py` puts it in each page's `#pl-data`.

The build refuses one without a source. Activities without it get `country` from their regions (uk only → GB, us only → US) and `area` (their region names) as a hint under the box. **No activity has `where:` yet:** this run could not open any web page (see below), so every plan asks the member for now. First to fill: the Fringe, the National Book Festival and the engine events (Great North Run, Yorkshire Three Peaks, Marine Corps Marathon, Coast to Coast start, Camino, Kilimanjaro, Inca Trail, Whitney), then the other place-tied ones.

It belongs to the item, not the plan (a new date keeps it); removing the plan clears it; newest wins in `merge()`. Tests: `node tools/test_list_merge.js` 42, `node tools/test_planner.js` 33 (trip links only from planner.yaml, the place URL-encoded, country links right, a sourced place wins, typed text cleaned).

(4) A month plan's safety step is "Before you start: stay safe" (`steps.safety_month`). (5) "Physical events add a training plan…" is now `kinds.event.why_train`, shown only with an engine event.

Everything gets ready by the **first** day (the steps do not change); the day step reads "Sat 14 Aug to Wed 18 Aug" and "Your dates, 14 to 18 August 2027" (`steps.day_span`); the plan is "Your plan for 14 to 18 August 2027"; My year's year card and month view show the range; the calendar file has the stay as **one all-day block** (DTEND the day after the last day); a stay that overlaps any other dated plan is flagged ("Overlaps", `notes.clash_span`); the planner keeps flexible steps off every day of a stay, and steps after it move only to after the last day. Rendering: `stayEatHtml()` in `static/plan-page.js` and `static/my-year.js`; styles `.pl-stay`, `.pl-wkh-sub`. No storage change (`DDRList` untouched; the nights come from the plan's own dates). Nothing goes into the calendar file but the step's detail line.

Checked at 390px and 1280px on the Fringe (14–18 Aug, open), Great North Run (single day, folded) and an open garden (not away: food only) and My year's fold-out: no sideways scroll, no console errors, focus ring visible on the fold.
