A single day defaults to the night of the day (not the night before), with the help line, because many day-plans are afternoons.
- **Day 10 (8 Nov, second run): while you're there, goes well with, and trips against training.**
  - **While you're there** (in "Your trip", on the activity's plan and in My year's fold-out from the day; trips away only): up to six other activities tied to the **same area** (`places` key; headed "Also in Scotland" and so on, because our areas are regions, not towns), weekly habits left out, an afternoon's activities first. **Add to my year** gives one its own plan on a **free day of this trip** (a stay: its second day on, then the first day; a single day: that day; a month plan: that month), skipping days taken by the member's other plans. Offered only for an **afternoon's** activity (`time: afternoon`); anything taking weeks, months or a year, anything with set dates or of kind event, and anything with engine training links to its own page with the reason ("It takes more than an afternoon…", "It happens on set dates…", "It has a training plan…"). Something already in the member's year shows "In your year: <date> · See its plan" instead, so nothing is overwritten. On the activity page the Add buttons appear once the trip's plan is saved. Then **"Tours and things to do near <place>"**: a Viator and a GetYourGuide search for the place, with Robin's partner ids (via `DDR.affUrl`) and the disclosure; never in calendar files.

Only offered when that date builds a working plan.

Each plan was built on its own, so check how you will fit them around your trip."), or ends the day before the engine's long session. A fact, not advice; nothing is moved.
  - **Storage (`DDRList`, still version 1, additive):** a plan gains `with`, the id of the trip it was added to (only on a saved plan; never itself; cleared by a new plan without it or by removing the plan; newest wins in `merge()`, not part of the plan's identity). `combine()` honours it only while the date still falls inside that trip: then the two are not flagged as a clash, the day says "Part of your trip for <trip>", and the trip's training note covers it.

Checked at 390px and 1280px on the Fringe (14 to 18 Aug, Edinburgh, one nearby added), Dark Sky Places (goes well with, one added) and My year with the Great North Run: no sideways scroll, no console errors, focus moves to the added activity's name.

"Watch the solstice sunrise at Stonehenge" is suggested as "June 2027" because it has no `dates.yaml` entry: add its fixed date from English Heritage's page on a run that can fetch. Areas are regions, so "Also in Scotland" can be hours from Edinburgh; town-level `where:` data (day 11 onwards) will let this narrow later.

- **Day 12 (9 Nov): accounts, sign-in by emailed link (row 11 skipped: the test fetch of uphellyaa.org was refused again, "permission request not answered in time", so no activity could be sourced).**
  - **The Worker** (`worker/`, Cloudflare Worker + D1, to run at `https://api.dontdieretired.com`): `POST /auth/start {email}` stores a hash of a new 32-byte random token and emails a link `https://dontdieretired.com/account/#t=<token>`; `POST /auth/verify {token}` spends it in one statement (`UPDATE … WHERE used = 0 AND expires > now RETURNING`), creates the member if new, cancels the address's other links and sets the session cookie; `GET /auth/me`; `POST /auth/signout`; `POST /auth/delete {confirm: true}`. Links last 30 minutes and work once; sessions 90 days, renewed at most daily (Safari can cap the cookie at 7 days, so a daily renewal keeps people signed in). Cookie `__Host-ddr_s`: Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, host-only on the API (same site as the pages, so Lax is sent). Only hashes of links and sessions are stored; the database holds an email address per member and nothing else (Stripe columns come on day 14 as a new migration).
  - **Protection:** every change must come from `SITE_ORIGIN` as JSON (CSRF); CORS answers only the site, with credentials; the same answer whether or not an address has an account; limits of 5 emails an hour per inbox (`a+tag@` counts as `a@`), 30 requests an hour per network (IPv6 by /64) and 300 emails an hour in total; throttle keys are HMACs with `SALT`, never an email or IP. An hourly cron deletes spent links, ended sessions and old counts. Bodies over 4 KB are refused. The local test mode (`DEV_MAIL=1`) hands back the link instead of emailing it and is ignored unless `SITE_ORIGIN` is localhost.
  - **The page** `/account/` (`templates/account.html`, `static/account.js`; noindex, not in the sitemap, linked from nowhere yet): sign in or create an account (one email box), "Check your email", signed in (Go to My year, Sign out, Delete my account), "That link has already been used or has run out". **The token never reaches a server log or another script:** an inline script at the top of the page's head moves it out of the address before anything else runs, no analytics or ad scripts load on this page (`{% block thirdparty %}` in base.html), and it is spent only when the member presses **Sign me in**, so email scanners that open links cannot use it up. **Accounts stay closed until `site.yaml` → `accounts.api` is set:** while it is empty the page says "Accounts are not open yet" and `/privacy/` says nothing about accounts; setting it adds the privacy paragraph (what is stored, the cookie, the counts, Resend, deletion) in the same build. `DDR_ACCOUNTS_API=http://localhost:8787` builds a local test copy.
  - **Tests and checks:** `node tools/test_accounts.js` 26 (runs the Worker against Node's SQLite with the real migration: single use, expiry, other links spent, made-up tokens, no enumeration, cookie flags, renewal, sign-out, deletion, origin and content-type refusals, CORS, all three limits, email service failure, clean-up, dev mode refused on the real site). End to end on a real local Worker (wrangler 3.114 with local D1) and the built site in Chromium at 390px and 1280px: wrong address message, link sent, link opened (token gone from the address), signed in, cookie flags as above, reload stays signed in, the same link refused in a second browser, sign out survives reload; no sideways scroll, focus ring visible, no console errors. List 46 and planner 42 tests unchanged.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found, and this run fixed: other scripts could read the token before it was cleared (fixed as above); scanners could spend links (the button); plus-addresses and IPv6 got round the limits, and there was no overall cap; the privacy wording was not exact (links store the address before an account exists; clean-up was occasional, now hourly with indexes); an email-service error gave a bare 500; the session renewal never ran inside Safari's 7 days; the size check came after reading the body. Left for later: Cloudflare Turnstile on the sign-in form if abuse appears.
  - **Deployment** (`.github/workflows/worker.yml`): runs the tests on every change under `worker/`; deploys (migrations, Worker, a fresh `SALT`, the Resend key) only when four repository secrets exist, otherwise it notes what is missing and passes. `SALT` keys only the abuse counters, so a fresh one each deploy is harmless. **Decision:** Resend sends the sign-in emails (simple HTTP API, free tier; its API format is from knowledge and was not re-checked today: confirm on the first live test).
  - **Waiting on Robin (exact steps sent 9 Nov):** (1) Resend: sign up, add the domain dontdieretired.com, add the DNS records it shows in Cloudflare, create an API key with sending access; (2) Cloudflare: Storage & Databases → D1 → Create database named `ddr-accounts`, copy its id; (3) Cloudflare: My Profile → API Tokens → Create Token → "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template, add the permission Account → D1 → Edit, and Zone Resources → dontdieretired.com; (4) GitHub: repo Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret, four times: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` (on the Cloudflare account home page), `D1_DATABASE_ID`, `RESEND_API_KEY`; then Actions → Accounts worker → Run workflow. Make sure no DNS record named `api` exists. After it deploys, a run sets `accounts.api` and tries a real sign-in before anyone else sees the page.
  - **Next:** day 13 (sync through `DDRList.merge()`) adds a `lists` table as migration 0002 and extends account deletion to it.

- **Day 13 (9 Nov, second run): the list and plans on the account (row 11 skipped again: the one test fetch, uphellyaa.org, was refused, "permission request not answered in time").**
  - **How it works.** A signed-in device posts its whole list to `POST /list/sync {list, fields}`; the Worker merges it with the account's copy using **the browser's own `DDRList.merge()`** (`worker/src/accounts.js` imports `static/list-store.js`; wrangler bundles it, checked with `wrangler deploy --dry-run`), stores the result and sends it back; the device merges that in (account copy first, so both devices settle on the account's order) and, if anything changed, fires the same `storage` event another tab would, so My year, the list and the activity pages redraw with their existing handlers (never while someone is typing in a box: it waits for them to leave it). Both sides only ever merge, so nothing is overwritten: a plan made on a phone, a tick on a laptop and a removal on either all survive (tests). When: on every page that loads `list-store.js`, on load, on returning to the tab, on coming back online, and 1.5 s after any change (sent with `keepalive` if the tab is closing). Offline or signed out, the list stays on the device as before.
  - **Storage:** migration `0002_lists.sql`, one row per member (`data` JSON, `rev`). Writes are compare-and-swap on `rev` (a write that loses a race reads again and merges, up to 4 tries) and only while the member still exists; deleting the account deletes the row, and the hourly clean-up removes any orphan. `DDRList` data unchanged (still version 1), except that **a removed item now keeps only what a merge needs** (its plan, note, place and own title are dropped), so removed things do not pile up. New `DDRList.FIELDS` (6) counts the additive fields inside version 1: **raise it whenever a field is added**; a Worker older than the page answers 409 rather than drop a field it does not know. So **deploy the Worker before (or with) any site change that adds a list field.**
  - **Limits:** 256 KB per list, 2,000 things per list (checked before merging), 600 syncs per member per hour; everything else stays at 4 KB per request. Origin check, JSON only, session cookie, CORS for the site only, as day 12.
  - **Shared computers.** The Worker returns an opaque account id (the member's random id; nothing personal) from `/auth/verify`, `/auth/me` and `/list/sync`; the browser remembers which account its list belongs to (`ddr_list_acct_owner`). If a different account signs in on a browser holding someone else's list, nothing is sent: the account page asks "This browser has a list from a different account": **Show only my account's list** (fetches the account copy with `list: null` and replaces the browser's) or **Add this browser's list to my account**. A browser list that belongs to no account yet (saved before accounts) is merged on first sign-in, as Robin asked. New button **"Sign out and remove my list from this browser"** (`DDRList.forget()`; the account copy stays).
  - **Pages:** `/account/` (signed in) says the list and plans are kept with the account, shows the sync result in a status line ("up to date on your account (N things)", or a plain reason: too large, service needs an update, too many tries, could not reach it), and the delete text now says the list goes too. `base.html` has `<meta name="ddr-accounts">` only when `accounts.api` is set; with it empty (now) no sync code runs anywhere. `/privacy/`: the list paragraph says "never sent to us unless you sign in to an account", and the accounts paragraph (shown only once accounts open) says what is kept and that deleting the account deletes it. `worker.yml` also runs on changes to `static/list-store.js`.
  - **Not yet gated on membership:** any signed-in account syncs. Day 14 (Stripe) adds membership status; day 15 decides what a non-member keeps (spec: browser plans read-only). Accounts stay closed until Robin's secrets are in, so nothing is exposed meanwhile.
  - **Tests:** `node tools/test_accounts.js` 40 (sync needs session, origin and JSON; first sign-in; two devices keep each other's plans and ticks; deletion survives an older copy; no write when nothing changed; members never see each other's lists; a lost race re-merges; too big, too many, too new and too often refused with nothing stored; `list: null`; removed things slimmed; a sync racing deletion leaves nothing; delete removes the list). `node tools/test_list_merge.js` 56 (merge settles when re-merged; the browser side against a stand-in Worker: no contact before sign-in, sign-in sends the list, a second device merges both ways and redraws, both devices end in the account's order with no extra requests, a change is sent by itself, a sync asked during a sync waits for the next one, 401 stops it, a shared browser never sends one account's list to another, forget, sign-out). `node tools/test_planner.js` 42, unchanged.
  - **End to end** on a local Worker (wrangler 3.114, local D1 with both migrations) and the built site in Chromium, phone 390px and laptop 1280px: phone plans beekeeping, signs in; laptop plans an open garden, signs in and gets both; phone's My year shows the garden without a reload; a tick on the laptop reaches the phone; a removal on the phone reaches the laptop; sign-out keeps the device copy; a second person signing in on the phone gets the "different account" choice, "Show only my account's list" shows their (empty) list, "Sign out and remove" empties the browser, and the first person signing back in gets their list from the account. No sideways scroll, no console errors.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found, and this run fixed: one person's list could go into another's account on a shared browser (the owner check and choice above); devices could disagree on order and send three extra requests each time (account copy merged first); removed items kept everything forever until the 256 KB cap stopped sync for good, with a misleading message (slimmed; plain messages); a huge list cost CPU before being refused (item count and size checked first); a sync racing account deletion could leave a list behind (conditional writes, clean-up); the privacy page's list paragraph said "never sent to us" (fixed); an older Worker could silently drop a newer field (`FIELDS`); a redraw could wipe a note being typed (waits); the keepalive size counted characters, not bytes (fixed). Left: D1 keeps about 30 days of point-in-time history, so "removed straight away" holds for the live database (as for the email address since day 12); worth a line in the terms on day 19.

- **Day 14 (10 Nov): payments through Stripe, test mode only (row 11 skipped again: the one test fetch, uphellyaa.org, was refused, "permission request not answered in time").**
  - **What a signed-in account can do** (on `/account/`, once accounts are open and a Stripe key is in): a "Planner Plus" box with **£2 a month** and **£20 a year** (prices including VAT; "You pay on Stripe's secure page; we never see your card"; cancel any time, Plus runs to the end of what is paid). The button opens Stripe Checkout; coming back says "switching Planner Plus on…" and checks for up to 20 seconds until the webhook has landed ("Planner Plus is on. You are on the yearly plan, renewing on …"), or says Stripe has the payment and to refresh in a minute. Leaving the payment page says nothing was charged. A member sees their plan and renewal date, or "until … It will not renew" after cancelling, or a failed-payment line while Stripe retries, and **"Cancel, switch plan or change card"**, which opens Stripe's Customer Portal and returns to the account page. Only `?plus=welcome` or `?plus=cancelled` ever appears in our addresses.
  - **The Worker** (`worker/src/plus.js`, wired into `accounts.js`): `POST /plus/checkout {plan}` and `POST /plus/portal` (session, our origin, JSON, 20 a member an hour); `POST /stripe/webhook` (Stripe's signature instead of the origin check); `/auth/me` now returns `plus {member, plan, until, ending, status}` and `payments` (whether a Stripe key is set). Each account gets **one Stripe customer**, made on its first checkout (idempotency key per account), and checkout first asks Stripe for that customer's subscriptions, so a payment made in another tab cannot lead to a second subscription. The webhook **checks Stripe's signature** (HMAC-SHA256 of "t.body", v1 only, constant time, five-minute window, body counted as it arrives, 64 KB cap) and then **reads the subscription fresh from Stripe**, never the event's copy, so duplicate, late and out-of-order events are harmless; an ended subscription is never brought back by a slower read. Membership = status active, trialing or past_due (Stripe is retrying a card). A subscription is linked to an account only through the Worker's own account id or the account's existing customer, so one person's payment can never make someone else a member. Test events are ignored by a live key and the other way round. Stripe API version pinned to `2025-03-31.basil` (period end read from the subscription item, as that version requires; checked in Stripe's changelog through Stripe's documentation search this run).
  - **Test mode only:** the Worker refuses a live key unless it also has `STRIPE_LIVE=1`, which only Robin sets when he switches; the set-up script and the deploy step refuse live keys too.
  - **Deleting an account** first closes any payment page still open for it and **deletes its Stripe customer**, which ends its subscriptions at once (no further charges); if Stripe cannot be reached, nothing is deleted and the member is asked to try again. A subscription paid for an account deleted meanwhile is ended by the webhook (only this site's own, by `metadata.site`). The account page's delete text says Plus ends at once.
  - **Storage:** migration `0003_plus.sql`: `users.stripe_customer` (unique) and `memberships` (subscription id, account, status, monthly/yearly, end of paid period, ending flag). No card details. `/privacy/` gains "Paying for Planner Plus" (shown only once accounts open). `DDRList` untouched.
  - **Set-up in Stripe:** `worker/stripe-setup.mjs`, run by the Accounts worker workflow after each deploy once the `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` repository secret exists. It adds only what is missing: product "Planner Plus" with £2 monthly and £20 yearly prices, product "Planner Plus gift (12 months)" with a £20 one-off price (sold from day 16), all VAT-inclusive and found by lookup key (`ddr_plus_month`, `ddr_plus_year`, `ddr_plus_gift`; no price ids in config); a Customer Portal set-up (cancel at the end of the paid period, switch monthly/yearly, change card, receipts, link to `/privacy/`); and the webhook endpoint `https://api.dontdieretired.com/stripe/webhook`, whose signing secret goes straight into the Worker and is never printed. "Run workflow" with **rotate webhook** ticked makes a new one if ever needed. Secrets are now given only to the workflow steps that use them.
  - **Tests:** new `node tools/test_plus.js` 27 (signature rules; test-only keys; closed until set up; checkout needs session, origin, JSON and a real plan; one customer per account; no personal data in return addresses; forged, stale, unsigned and oversized webhooks refused; member after payment; events in any order; cancel at period end, ended, past due; a second subscription; nobody else made a member; mode mismatch; Stripe down gives 500 for a retry; portal; limits; deletion order and failure; the review fixes; the set-up script makes everything once and refuses a live key). The D1 stand-in moved to `tools/d1_stub.js`, shared with `test_accounts.js` (40, unchanged); list 56, planner 42. The workflow runs both Worker test files. `wrangler deploy --dry-run` bundles the Worker.
  - **End to end (local):** the real Worker behind a small Node server with a stand-in Stripe, the built site in Chromium at 390px and 1280px: sign in, buy yearly, Stripe's page (stand-in), webhook, back to "Planner Plus is on", cancel at period end shows "until … will not renew", the portal button reaches Stripe's portal address, leaving checkout says nothing charged; no sideways scroll, no console errors, focus ring on the buttons.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found, and this run fixed: two tabs or a quick second payment could make an untracked second subscription that would bill forever (one customer per account, Stripe asked before each checkout); deleting an account during or just after payment could leave a subscription charging (customer deleted, open checkouts closed, orphans ended by the webhook); `expand[]=items.data.price` could be refused by Stripe (removed: the price comes whole); a slower read could store "active" over "canceled" (ended states are final); a body without a length was read in full (counted as it arrives); the Stripe key was visible to every deploy step (per-step secrets). Checked and fine: signature, test/live separation, injection, return addresses, open redirects, CSRF, limits.
  - **Not done, and why:** nothing is charged for yet (accounts are closed, no key is in, and Plus does not unlock anything until day 15's paywall); the gift checkout and codes are day 16; the terms page link in the portal comes with day 19's terms page. **Selling to UK and US buyers only** (until Robin's accountant answers on EU VAT) is not enforced by Checkout: this run found no Checkout setting that limits which countries may buy a subscription (not checked in depth); Checkout collects the billing address (`billing_address_collection: required`) so Norifico's records show each buyer's country. Robin, please raise this with your accountant together with the VAT question.
  - **Waiting on Robin (test mode, exact steps sent 10 Nov):** (1) decide which Stripe account takes Planner Plus (the connected one is named "Paws on the Sand"; a separate account for Don't Die Retired is cleaner) and the statement name; (2) in that account, switch to **test mode** (Developers → API keys), reveal the **Secret key** starting `sk_test_`; (3) GitHub: repo Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret named `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` with that test key; (4) the four account secrets from day 12 must be in too, then Actions → Accounts worker → Run workflow. Never paste a live key (`sk_live_`): it would be refused.

- **Day 15 (10 Nov, second run): two ways to plan, the paywall, read-only plans and the sample (row 11 skipped again: the test fetch of uphellyaa.org was refused, "permission request not answered in time").**
  - **"Plan this" on every activity page now offers two ways.** (1) **Plan it yourself** (tag "Free"): a fold-out "Show the links" with the activity's own links ("Book it and find out more"), Where (its sourced place, or a town the reader types; kept only on the page, never saved), See it on a map, Getting there, Where to stay (undated hotel search, hostels and campsites), Where to eat, the tour searches, "Also on our list near there" and "Goes well with" as plain links to their pages, and the affiliate disclosure when a tagged link is shown. No dates, no Add to my year, nothing stored. (2) **Plan it with Planner Plus**: the dated planner as before for a member; for anyone else, what Plus does, the price, **Join Planner Plus** (to `/account/`), **See a sample plan** and "Already a member? Sign in".
  - **The paywall switch.** `site.yaml` → `plus.paywall` (**false now**) and, for the server, `worker/wrangler.toml` → `PLUS_PAYWALL` (**"0" now**). **Turn both on together on launch day, not before.** While off: the planner works for every reader exactly as since 3 Nov (the Plus heading reads "Plan it with dates" and no price appears anywhere), account sync works for every account, and only the new free links are new for readers. `build.py` refuses `plus.paywall: true` without `accounts.api`. `DDR_PLUS_PAYWALL=1` (with `DDR_ACCOUNTS_API`) makes a local test build. With it on, pages carry `<meta name="ddr-plus" content="paywall">`.
  - **Who is a member:** `static/plus.js` (`DDRPlus.access()`, pure `decide()`): with the paywall on, a device that has signed in asks the Worker's `/auth/me`; only `plus.member === true` counts. A reader who has never signed in on the device is never sent to the Worker. Offline, it uses what the account said last time (flag `plus`, set only from the Worker's own answers once the paywall is on), so a member is not locked out of their plans on a train. Pages start read-only until the answer comes. **The browser paywall is a door, not a lock:** the planner runs in the browser, so a determined person could open it; what is kept on the server (the list and plans on the account, every device) is enforced by the Worker.
  - **Server:** `/list/sync`, with `PLUS_PAYWALL = "1"` and payments set up, is **read-only for an account without Plus**: it returns the account's stored copy (a lapsed member sees their plans on a new device) and stores nothing it is sent; replies carry `plus: true|false` (and `readonly`). The device then keeps its own list and sends no follow-ups. "Show only my account's list" on a shared browser never empties the browser when the account is read-only (it may be the other person's only copy; reason `readonly-keep`, with a plain message). The account page hides "kept with your account" and says why when read-only; after payment it syncs again at once.
  - **Read-only plans for non-members** (paywall on): a plan saved in the browser shows in full with "This is the plan saved in this browser…", Add to my calendar, Join Planner Plus and Remove this plan (their own data, so removing and the calendar file stay free); no change, place, Save, Add or "Use <date>" buttons. **My year** shows every plan without ticks, moves or Add, with the same note and Join; with nothing saved it shows what Plus does, the price and the sample.
  - **The sample plan, `/planner-plus/sample/`** (noindex, not in the sitemap, linked only from the paywall boxes): a real plan built in the browser for the Edinburgh Fringe, Sat 14 to Wed 18 Aug 2027, place Edinburgh, made on 1 Mar 2027 so nothing reads "Gone by" (`content/planner.yaml` → `plus.sample_plan`; **dates picked for the sample, said on the page**, not the Fringe's own). Drawn by `plan-page.js` in sample mode: read-only, ignores the reader's own list, never writes. Price and Join show only with the paywall on.
  - **Words:** `content/planner.yaml` → `plus:` (checked by `tools/planner.py`, passed in `#pl-data` and `activities.json`). They describe only what works today: no live calendar feed, Today screen or gifts until those rows are done (a test refuses those words). Styles `.pl-way`, `.pl-wayh`, `.pl-guest`, `.pl-price`, `.pl-ro`, `.pl-sample-*`.
  - **Tests:** `node tools/test_planner.js` 45 (decide(); every activity's free links only from planner.yaml, no dates, nearby only for a trip away, no activity twice, links only; the sample builds a full trip plan from sourced words). `node tools/test_list_merge.js` 58 (a read-only account: device keeps its change, nothing stored, no follow-ups, the `plus` flag only from the Worker's word; replace never empties the browser when read-only). `node tools/test_plus.js` 28 (paywall on: non-member reads only, stores nothing; pays: stored; lapses: reads its copy, stores nothing new; off, or on without payments: day 13 behaviour and no `plus` in the reply). `node tools/test_accounts.js` 40, unchanged.
  - **Checked in Chromium at 390px and 1280px**, on a normal build and a local paywall build with a stand-in Worker: free links with a typed place (focus moves to the place), the guest box, a read-only saved Fringe plan, My year read-only, a member gets the form, the sample page; plus a beekeeping plan saved and ticked in the normal build (nothing changed for today's readers). No sideways scroll, no console errors.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found no XSS and no way for a non-member to store data or reach another account, and found, all fixed: the server would have gone read-only as soon as Stripe was set up while the site was still open (now its own `PLUS_PAYWALL` switch, and the account page's "kept with your account" lines depend on it); "Show only my account's list" could wipe someone's only copy when the account keeps nothing; every device would have recorded "has Plus" before launch (now only from the Worker's explicit `plus`); a guest could get the full planner for a moment before the membership answer (now read-only until it comes); the "Use <date>" button showed on read-only plans and threw on the sample page.
  - **Left, and decisions:** the sample is a modest plan (5 steps, no training): the Fringe was chosen because it shows every trip section; its date step still links Britannica until a run can open the Fringe Society's page (day 3 note). Checkpoint item 8 (a long plan shows the next four weeks open) is not done yet: for day 21's pass. The list page's "Plan this →" links still go to the activity page, where the box decides. `/my-year/`, `/account/` and the sample go into the menu and sitemap at launch.

- **Day 16 (11 Nov): gifts (row 11 skipped again: the one test fetch, uphellyaa.org, was refused, "permission request not answered in time").**
  - **What it does.** On `/account/`, a signed-in account (accounts are free) sees **Give Planner Plus**: £20 including VAT for twelve months, no renewal. **Buy a gift for £20** opens Stripe Checkout (one-off payment, billing address collected); coming back (`?gift=thanks`, removed from the address at once) waits for the code and shows it under **Your gifts** with **Print the card**. The card prints on its own (a copy is put straight into the page body while printing, and removed after): the sunrise mark and name, "A year of Planner Plus", what Plus does today (only working features), the code, and "To use it: go to dontdieretired.com/redeem, sign in with your email address and type the code… It does not renew". Optional "To" and "From" names are typed in the browser and never sent. Each gift shows "not used yet", "used on <date>" (never by whom), "refunded" or "cancelled: the payment was disputed". **Use a gift code** (every signed-in account, once payments are open) takes the code in any case, with or without dashes or spaces, and says plainly what happened. **`/redeem/`** (the address on the card) is the account page headed "Use a gift code", with the code box first once signed in.
  - **Rules.** A gift gives Plus for twelve calendar months from the moment it is used, **or from the end of Plus the member already has** if that is set to end (a second gift follows the first). If the member has a subscription that **renews**, the code is refused unspent ("stop the renewal first"), so nobody pays twice. A gift member is not offered a plan until **45 days before the gift ends**; then the account page asks whether to carry on, and a plan chosen then starts with a Stripe trial to the gift's last day, so the first payment is taken when the gift ends (under three days left it starts at once, as Stripe needs 48 hours). If a member later renews a subscription in Stripe's portal while a gift still covers them (or paid in another tab while redeeming), the webhook moves that subscription's next payment to the gift's end (trial, no proration).
  - **Codes.** 16 characters from a 31-letter alphabet without 0, O, 1, I or L (about 79 bits, the Worker's cryptographic random source, no bias), shown as XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX. Stored as a hash (to redeem) and, until used, refunded or disputed, an AES-GCM copy under the Worker secret **`GIFT_KEY`** (bound to its gift) so the buyer can see and print it again. Spent in one D1 batch with the start of the year: two people using one code at once, one gets it. Ten tries an hour per account and thirty per network. Never in a URL, an email or a log.
  - **Worker:** `worker/src/gifts.js`; routes `POST /gift/checkout`, `/gift/mine`, `/gift/redeem` (session, our origin, JSON, as every other route); `/auth/me` adds `gifts` (on sale or not) and `plus.gift_until` / `plus.then`. The webhook now also handles `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`, `charge.refunded` and `charge.dispute.created`; for a gift it re-reads the Checkout session and its payment (refunded or disputed before we heard: a dead gift is recorded) and makes **one gift per payment** (unique on the session). A full refund or a chargeback cancels an **unused** code; a used one stays with its owner (Robin decides any exception by hand). Gifts are on sale only with payments set up **and** `GIFT_KEY` present. Migration `0004_gifts.sql`. Membership: a gift is a `memberships` row (`plan: gift`, never renews) that counts only until its end. Account deletion: gifts it bought keep working, its copy of the codes goes; a gift it used stays used; the hourly clean-up does the same for leftovers.
  - **Set-up:** `worker/stripe-setup.mjs` subscribes the webhook to the new events (an endpoint made on day 14 is brought up to date, keeping its secret). The Accounts worker workflow makes `GIFT_KEY` **once**, only if `wrangler secret list --format json` reads cleanly and lacks it; it must never be replaced (unused codes would still redeem, but buyers could no longer see them). `/privacy/` gains "Planner Plus gifts" (shown once accounts open).
  - **Tests:** new `node tools/test_gifts.js` 19 (alphabet and spread; sealed copy bound to key and gift; leap day; closed without payments or key; checkout needs session, origin, JSON, nothing personal in the form; one gift per payment however often Stripe says so; unpaid, other site, live in test, forged: no gift; redeem once, any spelling, buyer told only "used"; two at once; throttles; stacking behind an ending plan and refusal on a renewing one; expiry; paywall sync for a gift member; plan after a gift with trial; refunds and part refunds; account deletion; refund or dispute before the webhook; chargeback; "Renew" in the portal moves the payment). `test_plus.js` 28 (set-up script brings an old webhook's events up to date), `test_accounts.js` 40, list 58, planner 45. `wrangler deploy --dry-run` bundles.
  - **End to end (local):** the real Worker behind a small Node server with a stand-in Stripe and a signed webhook, the built site in Chromium at 390px and 1280px: buyer signs in, buys, the code appears, names added, card printed (print view checked: card alone, code and names on it); a second person signs in on `/redeem/`, a wrong code says so, the right one (typed in lower case) gives "Planner Plus as a gift until 11 November 2027"; a third person is told it has been used; the buyer sees "used on". No sideways scroll; the only console lines are the expected 404 and 409 answers.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found nothing critical and these, all fixed: a refund or chargeback landing before the webhook could leave a working code (payment re-read when the gift is made); chargebacks were not handled (`charge.dispute.created`); renewing a subscription in the portal after a gift was queued behind it would have double-charged (payment moved to the gift's end); the workflow could have replaced `GIFT_KEY` if wrangler's output changed (parsed as JSON, stops on doubt); a missing key now logs the session id; the privacy text now says we keep each gift's Stripe payment reference. **Left (low):** one account redeeming two codes in the same instant could get overlapping years rather than consecutive ones (rare; Robin can extend by hand); a 100%-off coupon would make no gift (none are used); no backup of `GIFT_KEY`.
  - **Also fixed:** today's Things to do run gave "Learn glass fusing" its safety line as a list, which broke `node tools/test_planner.js`; made it one line and `tools/planner.py` now refuses a list there.
  - **Decisions:** the buyer must be signed in (free), so the code lives on their account and can be printed again, rather than being emailed or put in a link. The card is printed from the browser (print or "save as PDF"), not a PDF file we make, so the names never leave the browser. Gift codes do not expire; the twelve months start when used.
  - **Not done, and why:** nothing is on sale (accounts closed, no Stripe key). The gift sales page and the terms page (refunds, the 14-day cancellation right, what happens to an unused gift) are day 19. No reminder email near a gift's end (the account page asks; an email would need more than the sign-in sender): consider after launch.

- **Day 17 (11 Nov, second run): the members' live calendar feed (row 11 skipped again: the one test fetch, uphellyaa.org, was refused, "permission request not answered in time"; the kit half of row 17 waits for the same reason).**
  - **What a member can do** (on `/account/`, signed in, Plus once the paywall is on; every account while it is off, as with sync): a section **"My year in your own calendar"**: **Make my calendar address** shows a secret address once, with **Copy**, **Add to Apple Calendar** (a `webcal:` link) and one line each for iPhone/Mac, Google Calendar ("Other calendars", +, "From URL") and Outlook ("Add calendar", "Subscribe from web"); then "Your calendar address is on. A calendar last looked at it on …". **Make a new address** (stops the old one) and **Stop my calendar address**, with a line saying either stops the calendar already added. My year's calendar box gains "Or add a calendar that keeps itself up to date… on your account" (only once accounts are open). The download stays as it was and stays free.
  - **The feed** (`worker/src/feed.js`, `GET`/`HEAD /feed/<43 random characters>.ics` on the API): built on each change from the list kept on the account with **`DDRPlan.calendar()`**, a new pure function in `static/planner.js` that is exactly My year's old "Add to my calendar" path (`combine()` then `ics()`); `my-year.js` now calls it too, so the file and the feed cannot differ (test). The Worker fetches the site's `/planner/activities.json` and the training files it needs (no copy in the Worker), keeps the last calendar in D1 with its key (code version, list rev, data hash, UK day), and so does no work when nothing changed. `ics()` takes `refresh` (asks apps to look every 6 hours: `REFRESH-INTERVAL`, `X-PUBLISHED-TTL`; each app decides). Plain links only, no affiliate ids. Headers: `text/calendar`, `nosniff`, CSP `sandbox`, no CORS, `no-cache`, `noindex`.
  - **Rules:** one address per account; only a SHA-256 of it is stored, so it is shown once; a new one replaces it at once; stop and account deletion delete it (and the hourly clean-up any orphan). With the paywall on, a lapsed account's address is kept but serves one entry, "Your Don't Die Retired calendar has stopped updating", saying the plans are still on the account, that rejoining restarts it and that "Add to my calendar" saves a file; it updates again by itself on rejoining. A feed covers the soonest **120** undone plans up to **24 months** ahead (CPU, see below). Limits: 60 reads an hour per feed, 30 unknown addresses an hour per network, 10 new addresses an hour per account. If the site cannot be reached or sends something unreadable, the last calendar is served (else 503 for the app to retry); a calendar built while a training file could not be fetched is sent but not kept.
  - **Storage:** migration `0005_feeds.sql` (`feeds`: hash, account id, when made, last day read, the cached calendar and its key). `/auth/me` adds `feed {ok, on, made, read}`, never the address. `/privacy/` gains "Your calendar address" (shown once accounts open). `DDRList` untouched. `content/planner.yaml` → `plus.what` now says the plans go into "your own calendar through an address that keeps itself up to date" (the test now refuses only "Today screen" and "gift" there).
  - **Tests:** new `node tools/test_feed.js` 11 (calendar() equals the old path; make needs session, origin and JSON; only a hash stored; the feed equals My year's file for that list; folding; HEAD; changes on another device appear; unchanged list served from the kept copy without rebuilding; new data rebuilds; new address and stop kill the old; malformed and unknown addresses; accounts see only their own; all three limits; paywall: Plus only, stopped entry, back on rejoining; site down; the review fixes; deletion and clean-up). The Accounts worker workflow now runs it (with Python for `tools/planner.py`) and also runs on changes to `static/planner.js` and `tools/planner.py`, because the Worker bundles `planner.js`. Others unchanged: accounts 40, plus 28, gifts 19, list 58, planner 45. `wrangler deploy --dry-run` (3.114) bundles.
  - **End to end (local):** the real Worker behind a small Node server with the D1 stand-in, the built site in Chromium at 390px and 1280px: phone plans beekeeping and the Great North Run, signs in, makes the address (focus moves to it); the feed answered `text/calendar` with 68 entries including the training; the laptop signs in, the section says when a calendar last looked, plans an open garden, and the feed has 71; stop gives 404. A second person signing in in the same tab after the first signed out does not see the first person's address. No sideways scroll, no console errors.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code) found, and this run fixed: building a big calendar costs a lot of CPU (a list at the 256 KB cap took about a second, and over 1.5 MB was never kept): now capped at 120 plans and 24 months; a failed training fetch was remembered and its thin calendar kept for the day (failures no longer remembered or kept); unreadable site data threw on every request (now falls back to the last calendar); the address pane could stay on show for the next person on a shared computer (cleared on every sign-in and sign-out; also fixed the day-12 "Sign me in" button staying disabled for a second link opened in the same tab); after stopping a lapsed member's address, focus and the message fell into a hidden section (kept on show, focus to its heading); the pane had no name (group labelled by the address label), no warning beside the buttons (added), buttons wrong if the refresh failed (set directly); a lone carriage return or control character in a member's own title went into the file raw (`icsText` now strips control characters and turns every line break into `\n`, file and feed); 15 minutes of client caching contradicted "stops at once" (`no-cache`); the privacy text said "the day it was made" (it is when). **Left (low):** the unknown-address limit runs after the database lookup (addresses are 256 random bits, so guessing is hopeless; this is cost only; a Cloudflare rate-limiting rule on `/feed/*` is the place if it is ever abused). D1's 30-day point-in-time history applies here too (terms page, day 19).
  - **Decisions:** the address is shown once (only its hash is kept), the usual way for secret calendar addresses; a lapsed member's address is kept, not deleted, so rejoining needs nothing redone. The feed's "today" is the UK day. A training file that changes within a day reaches the feed the next day, or sooner if the list or the site's data changes. The Google and Outlook menu names are from knowledge and were not checked today (no fetches): re-check on a run that can fetch. The Planner Plus sales words already promised "your own calendar"; they now say it keeps itself up to date, which is true once accounts open.
  - **Content added the same evening (live session with Robin, web fetches allowed):** five new medium pages, each researched from official pages opened that day and re-checked by an independent checker (about 30 corrections made: unsupported "most", wording not the source's, expired fee tiers, a closed course discount, a non-record called a record, prices without "as last listed"): `eat-at-a-michelin-starred-restaurant` (the Guide's distinctions table; no restaurant named), `take-a-bread-making-class`, `go-to-cookery-school` ("Take a course at a cookery school or college": Leiths, Ballymaloe, ICE, Cornwall College, Trident), `row-across-the-atlantic` (World's Toughest Row; the organiser publishes no fee) and `paddle-devizes-to-westminster`, with link terms and `dates.yaml` entries (DW 26 March 2027; the Atlantic start 12 December 2026). DW's www address refused a fetch; the bare dwrace.co.uk opened.

In Cloudflare: Workers & Pages → Plans → Workers Paid. Nothing else new: no new secret.

- **Day 19 (12 Nov): the gift page, the terms, the sales page and the privacy update (row 11 still waits on web fetches: a test fetch of GOV.UK's returns-and-refunds page was refused again, "permission request not answered in time").**
  - **Pages** (all three noindex and out of the sitemap while `plus.paywall` is false; each shows "Not on sale yet" then; with the paywall on they are indexed and in the sitemap): **`/planner-plus/`** (`templates/plus.html`): what Plus does (only working features: dated plans, engine training, "Your trip", My year with its overlap and training notes, the account on every device, the calendar address), what stays free, the price, the 14-day refund, the gift, "good to know" (general information, affiliate links, what the account holds). **`/planner-plus/gift/`** (`plus-gift.html`): £20, twelve months from first use, no renewal, code never runs out, how to buy (sign in free, buy, code on the account, print the card, names never sent), how to redeem (`/redeem/`), questions (already a member, after the twelve months, refunds, a different email). **`/planner-plus/terms/`** (`plus-terms.html`, "Version 1, 12 November 2026"; change `TERMS_V` with the wording): who (Norifico), what Plus is, paying, renewing, cancelling, refunds, gifts, if we stop Plus, information, your rights. Styles `.pp-*`, `.gi-gift`.
  - **Links, only with the paywall on (launch):** a home-page band "Give a year of Planner Plus" under Things to do; "More about Planner Plus · Give it as a gift" in every Plan this guest box and on the sample page; a gift line under My year's calendar box; "Planner Plus terms" in the footer. Always on: the account page links the terms beside the prices and the gift. `/privacy/` (accounts section, shown once accounts open) gains "Where account information is kept" (Cloudflare hosts the server and database; its rolling history of up to 30 days; no selling, no advertising use; link to the terms). `worker/stripe-setup.mjs`: the Customer Portal links the terms (`business_profile.terms_of_service_url`); a set-up made before today is brought up to date once (test added: `test_plus.js` 28).
  - **Policy decisions (Robin to confirm or change before launch):** (1) **refund within 14 days of any payment, first or renewal, in full, even if Plus was used**, and the membership ends. Chosen because it gives at least what a UK consumer is owed without the page having to explain the rules on losing the right to cancel once a service starts, and because it already covers the renewal cooling-off that the coming subscription rules are expected to add (search results this run, not opened, say the DMCC Act subscription regime was delayed to spring 2027). After 14 days, no part refunds, but cancel stops the next payment. (2) **Unused gift codes refundable within 30 days of purchase** (so a late-November buyer is covered past Christmas); a used one within 14 days of purchase, the recipient's twelve months then end. (3) Price changes: email at least 30 days ahead. (4) If Plus closes: 30 days' email and refunds of unused time, including used gifts' remaining months, and of unused codes. (5) "Made for people in the UK and the US" (not "sold only to": nothing enforces the country). (6) Deleting an account ends Plus at once with no automatic refund; the terms say to cancel instead to keep the paid time.
  - **Robin, 12 Nov 2026 (live, after this run): refunds confirmed as written: "Keep it simple"** (a full refund within 14 days of any payment, even if used; unused gift codes within 30 days; nothing after that unless we got something wrong). He had asked whether it could be "no refund after it is ordered unless damaged"; he was told that UK online sales generally carry a 14-day right to cancel that terms cannot remove (from knowledge; GOV.UK could not be opened), and that the legal minimum (immediate start with a pro-rata refund) was the alternative. Do not tighten the refund terms without asking him.
  - **How each promise is kept (all by hand in Stripe, test mode first):** a 14-day refund = Payments → the payment → Refund (full), **then** Subscriptions → that subscription → Cancel immediately (a refund alone does not end it; `charge.refunded` only acts on gifts). An unused gift = refund in full (a part refund leaves the code working); the webhook cancels the code. A used gift within 14 days = full refund, then end that member's gift by hand (no tool yet: ask a run to add one if it ever happens). Refunds within 14 days of the request. Price changes and closure need an email to members by hand (no bulk email exists).
  - **Security and accuracy review** (a separate checker that had not seen the work, against the Worker and page code) found, all fixed: plans after cancelling are read-only on the account (the terms had said they could be removed; also the calendar address stops); a plan chosen in a gift's last three days is charged at once (terms and the account page's note now say so; `static/account.js` gives that note); renewal dates follow a gift or a switch, not "the date you joined"; "sold to UK and US only" was not enforced (reworded); the sales page understated what the account holds; "ready the moment you buy" depends on the webhook ("usually within a minute"); "usually" dropped from the refund timing; the 14-day refund now says the membership ends and how to ask; a used gift within 14 days of purchase is now refundable. Checked and true: cancel at period end, proration on switching, deletion ends Plus and deletes the Stripe customer, gift stacking and refusal, "used/when, not by whom", reprint until used, refunds and disputes void unused codes, the 45-day carry-on offer, no affiliate tags in calendars, nothing personal in addresses; the portal update call is valid.
  - **Checked** in Chromium at 390px and 1280px on a local paywall build (sales, gift, terms, home band, privacy) and the normal build: no sideways scroll, no console errors, focus ring on the buttons, buttons at least 44px. Normal build: the three pages carry noindex, none in the sitemap, no gift band on the home page. Tests: list 58, planner 45, plus 28, gifts 19, accounts 40, feed 11.
  - **Not done, and why:** the terms say nothing about the law itself (no GOV.UK or legislation page could be opened this run); a run that can fetch should re-read them against GOV.UK's "Accepting returns and giving refunds" and the Consumer Contracts Regulations and say whether anything is missing. Gift purchases make no Stripe invoice, so their receipt is Stripe's emailed receipt (see Robin's step 1).

(2) **renewal reminders (recommended):** in Stripe, Settings → Billing → Subscriptions and emails → "Send emails about upcoming renewals" for yearly plans; the terms do not promise it yet, so tell a run once it is on and the terms can say so. (3) **Read the terms** (`/planner-plus/terms/` on the live site; it is unlinked and noindex until launch) and say if any of the six policy decisions above should change; have them checked if you wish.   - **`tools/e2e_plus.js`** (new, kept for every later change): the whole member journey in Chromium, phone 390px and laptop 1280px, against the **real Worker code** (`worker/src/*.js` on Node's SQLite with every migration) and a **stand-in Stripe** that answers the calls the Worker makes, plays Stripe's Checkout and portal pages, and sends webhooks signed as Stripe signs them. Nothing leaves the machine. Run: `DDR_ACCOUNTS_API=http://localhost:8787 DDR_PLUS_PAYWALL=1 python3 build.py --no-social`, copy `dist` somewhere, `node tools/e2e_plus.js <that copy> [screenshot dir]`, then rebuild normally. Steps (all pass): a guest sees the free links, the price and the sample and is never sent to the Worker; signs in by emailed link on the phone (token only after `#`, gone from the address at once); buys yearly; "Planner Plus is on"; plans two things, both stored on the account; signs in on the laptop and sees both; a plan made there reaches the phone; cancels in the portal ("will not renew", still plans); the paid time runs out (plans read-only, My year read-only, the account stores nothing new); a friend whose browser already holds a list is asked before it goes to her account (below), buys a gift, the code shows, names added, the card prints; the first person redeems it on `/redeem/` (a wrong code refused, the right one typed in lower case accepted) and can plan again; the friend sees "used on" (never by whom); a second gift refunded unused shows "refunded", its code is hidden and refused; the friend deletes her account: her Stripe customer goes, the gift she gave keeps working. Every page checked for sideways scroll and console errors; **all 446 addresses the browsers asked for checked: no email, no token or code in any query, no gift code anywhere**. 14 screenshots looked at.
  - **Security review** (a separate checker that had not seen the code, across the Worker, the account pages and the workflow). **Fixed:** (1, high) **test-mode purchases would have carried into live mode**: a test-card membership or gift code made while the real site runs on a test key (as Robin's try-out will) would have stayed valid after the switch to live. Now every membership, gift and Stripe customer records its mode (migration `0006_mode.sql`; everything before it is test) and only the current key's mode counts: test gifts are refused and unlisted in live mode, test memberships give nothing, a test customer is replaced by a new live one at the first live checkout (its own idempotency key), the portal and deletion leave a test customer alone once live. (2, medium) **two plan pages open at once could both be paid** (two subscriptions): checkout now closes any open plan page of that customer before opening a new one (gift pages untouched; Stripe down = no new page), and the webhook logs an error if an account ever has two live subscriptions, so Robin can refund one. (4, low-medium) **someone could send their own sign-in link** ("open this to see my plan") and a browser's list would have gone to their account: a list that belongs to no account yet is now added only after the member presses **Add it to my account** under "Add this browser's list to your account?", which shows the address signed in, or **This is not my account: sign out** (the list stays). Robin's "plans already saved merge on first sign-in" holds, with one tap. (6) deletion now forgets the Stripe customer id as soon as Stripe has deleted it. (7) the workflow made a **new `SALT` on every deploy**, resetting every limit; now made once, only when missing. Also: after redeeming, the success line scrolls into view on a phone. Tests: list 58, planner 45, accounts 40, plus 31, gifts 20, feed 11; `wrangler deploy --dry-run` bundles.
  - **Not fixed, decisions:** (3, medium) **an ad network's script on any page could use a signed-in member's session** (the Worker trusts the whole site origin; the account pages already load no third-party scripts, but `list-store.js` talks to the Worker from every page). Ads are off, so nothing is exposed now; `build.py` now **refuses `ads.enabled: true` together with `accounts.api`**. Before ads go on: move the account pages to their own origin with a strict Content-Security-Policy and let only that origin read the email, gift codes and calendar address. (5, low) one attacker with many IPv6 networks could use up the 300-an-hour sign-in emails for everyone: if it ever happens, a Cloudflare rate-limiting rule or Turnstile on `/auth/start` (left as day 12 said). (6, low) an account with a Stripe customer cannot be deleted while no usable key is set (only if the key were removed). (9, low) refunding a **subscription** payment does not end the membership by itself: the terms' manual step stays (refund, then cancel in Stripe), because the shape of subscription charges in the pinned API version could not be checked today.

  - **Found while testing the 14 Nov "Left (low)" race** (a renewal webhook and a second gift at the same instant). A new test tries the two in 18 interleavings. The race itself is already closed by `redeem()`'s fingerprint check (the second code is refused as "renewing", or the webhook sees it). But it showed a **real gap without any race:** a member whose plan is set to end, who uses **two gifts** (two years stacked behind it), then presses **Renew** in Stripe's portal, would be charged at the plan's end, inside time the gifts already give: Stripe can put a payment off by about two years at most (`TRIAL_MAX`, 700 days), so the old code did nothing. **Now** `syncSubscription()` (`worker/src/plus.js`) sets such a subscription to **end with what is paid for** (`cancel_at_period_end`), and records what Stripe answers; the gifts carry the member on, and near the last gift's end the account page offers a plan to follow, as for any gift. A Stripe refusal logs an error for Robin (and Stripe retries the event). No idempotency key on that call on purpose (setting it twice is harmless; a cached reply must not hide a later Renew).

- **Fixes run (17 Nov, second run; rows 23–24 in use, nothing new for sale): booking windows, so a ballot step is no longer dated after the ballot (checkpoint item 2).** Robin's secrets are still not in (the Accounts worker's deploy job skipped its steps on 17 Nov), so rows 20 (real test mode) to 22's Stripe half still wait.
  - **The bug (in every Great North Run plan made today):** "Enter the ballot" was dated by the 12-week run-up (27 June 2027 for 12 September), long after Great Run's second ballot, which it says will be held in January 2027. The Devizes to Westminster plan had the same fault: "Book the DW Training Day (12 December 2026)" fell on 8 January 2027.
  - **The fix:** `content/dates.yaml` entries may carry `book:` for their `next` date: `step` (which of the activity's start steps, from 0), `from` (a day, or YYYY-MM when the page gives only the month: the 1st is used and the note says so) and/or `by` (last day), `for` (must equal `next`), `note` (what the page says) and `source`. `tools/planner.py` checks all of it and drops the window once that year's date has passed. In `DDRPlan.build()` (step 2b) that step goes on the day the window opens (today if already open), never after `by`; with only a `by` that the run-up would miss, a week from today (or `by` if sooner), so a place that may fill is not left to the last day. It is **fixed** (My year never moves it), its reason is `steps.book_window.why`, its detail is the note and it links the source. The steps before it, and "Check this year's dates", come no later than a week before it. A window already closed puts the step on today with `notes.book_closed` ("…ended on <date>, before today. There may be other ways in…"). Only for a plan on that `next` date with a day (not a month plan, not another year).

- **Stripe review against Stripe's own integration planner (19 Nov 2026, live session with Robin; no code changed).** The planner's recommended path for Planner Plus is: Stripe-hosted Checkout (no Managed Payments for now), flat-rate prices, pay up front, Customer Portal (cancel at period end), Smart Retries with automated emails, Stripe Tax free threshold monitoring. **Already matches:** hosted Checkout in subscription and payment modes, flat prices by lookup key, Customer Portal (cancel at period end, switch plan, change card), signed webhooks (v1 HMAC, 300 s tolerance, raw body, size cap, mode check), idempotency keys, pinned API version, one customer per member. **Differences, for the next build run (payment code: separate checker before pushing):** (1) the planner's blueprint listens to `invoice.paid` and `invoice.payment_failed`; ours follows `customer.subscription.updated` for status (past_due/active), which covers access, so leave the code and instead have Robin turn on, in the DDR account's Dashboard → Billing → Revenue recovery: Smart Retries, failed-payment emails with a link to update the card, expiring-card emails, and "cancel the subscription" after the last retry (the terms say a failed renewal ends Plus); (2) set a product tax code (`txcd_10000000`, electronically supplied services) on the three products in `worker/stripe-setup.mjs` and confirm the head-office address, so Stripe Tax's free threshold monitoring works with no collection; (3) flexible billing mode (`subscription_data.billing_mode[type]=flexible`) is Stripe's new default for subscriptions but needs a newer API version than our pinned `2025-03-31.basil`: consider when the version is next raised, with the e2e re-run.

**Robin's launch steps, in order (gathered 14 Nov 2026 from the day 12 to 20 logs; tick here as they are done).** Everything is built and tested locally; these are the steps only Robin can take. Steps 1 to 9 take about an hour; nothing is charged in test mode.
1. **Decide** which Stripe account takes Planner Plus (the connected one is named "Paws on the Sand"; a separate Don't Die Retired account is cleaner) and the name on customers' card statements. **Robin, 19 Nov 2026: use his existing Stripe login, with "Don't Die Retired" on customers' card statements.** So: a **new account under the same login** (Dashboard, click "Paws on the Sand" top left → New account), for Norifico Ltd with the same company details (and the same bank if he likes), not Planner Plus products inside the Paws on the Sand account. Stripe's "Multiple separate accounts" page says separate websites must use separate accounts, and each has its own public name and statement descriptor. In the new account, Settings → Business → Public details: name Don't Die Retired, website dontdieretired.com, statement descriptor `DONT DIE RETIRED` (16 characters; Stripe does not allow an apostrophe), shortened descriptor `DDRETIRED`. The Worker sets no descriptor of its own, so the account's descriptor is what card statements show. Its test key (`sk_test_`) is the one for step 6. Waiting on Robin to create it. **Tax and where we sell (Robin, 19 Nov 2026):** launch on the original plan (normal Stripe, Norifico is the seller, UK and US buyers only, statement DONT DIE RETIRED); **sell globally after the Christmas period.** Two ways to do that, to decide with Robin in January 2027: (a) **Stripe Managed Payments** (Stripe is merchant of record and registers, files and remits VAT/sales tax in 80+ countries incl. UK, EU, US; GB businesses and automated digital products eligible after Stripe's review; but statements read `LINK.COM* DONT DIE RETIRED`, checkout, receipts and emails are "Sold through Link", customers manage subscriptions on link.com, Stripe may refund if we do not answer within 48 hours, an additional 3.5% per transaction on top of normal Stripe fees (shown in Stripe's account set-up, 19 Nov 2026); code change: `managed_payments` on the Checkout Session, digital tax codes on the products, terms and privacy pages, e2e re-run); or (b) stay as we are and register for VAT abroad (EU non-Union OSS etc.) with Stripe Tax, on the accountant's advice. Until then the pages keep "made for people in the UK and the US".
2. **Resend** (sign-in emails): sign up at resend.com; add the domain dontdieretired.com; add the DNS records it shows in Cloudflare; turn **off** click tracking and open tracking for the domain; create an API key with sending access.
3. **Cloudflare, database:** Storage & Databases → D1 → Create database named `ddr-accounts`; copy its id.
4. **Cloudflare, token:** My Profile → API Tokens → Create Token → "Edit Cloudflare Workers" template; add Account → D1 → Edit; Zone Resources → dontdieretired.com. Copy the token. Note the Account ID from the account home page. Make sure no DNS record named `api` exists.
5. **Cloudflare, plan and analytics:** Workers & Pages → Plans → **Workers Paid** (US$5 a month; the calendar feed needs it); in Web Analytics, make sure automatic set-up is not injecting its script (or exclude `/account/` and `/redeem/`).
6. **Stripe, test mode:** in the chosen account switch to test mode (Developers → API keys) and reveal the secret key starting `sk_test_` (never a `sk_live_` key: it is refused). Settings → Business → Customer emails → turn on "Successful payments" and "Refunds" (the terms promise a receipt). Optional: Settings → Billing → Subscriptions and emails → renewal reminders for yearly plans (tell a run, and the terms can say so). **Also (Stripe review, 19 Nov):** Billing → Revenue recovery (in the Dashboard): turn on Smart Retries, the failed-payment emails with a link to update the card, and the expiring-card emails, and set "if all retries fail" to **cancel the subscription** (the terms say a failed renewal ends Plus). Settings → Tax: check the head-office address is Norifico's, so Stripe's threshold monitoring works (no registrations needed: nothing is collected until one is added on the accountant's advice). Do the same in live mode at step 11.
7. **GitHub:** repo Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret, five times: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `D1_DATABASE_ID`, `RESEND_API_KEY`, `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (the test key). Then Actions → Accounts worker → Run workflow.
8. **The next build run** sees the deploy, sets `accounts.api`, and does the whole journey on the real site with Stripe's test card (4242 4242 4242 4242, any future date, any CVC): subscribe, cancel, gift, redeem on a second device, refund.
9. **Robin tries it on his phone** the same way (and, for one plan, with VoiceOver on), taps the Booking.com, Viator and GetYourGuide links once from a saved trip plan to see the dates and places come through, and reads `/planner-plus/terms/`.
10. **Accountant:** VAT on these sales, and selling outside the UK and US (until then the pages say "made for people in the UK and the US").
11. **Go live (row 22):** a run adds the live switch to the workflow (it refuses live keys today); Robin swaps `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` for the live key, turns on the receipt emails in live mode, makes one real purchase and refunds it. Then a run turns the paywall on (`site.yaml` → `plus.paywall` and `worker/wrangler.toml` → `PLUS_PAYWALL` together) and Plus and gifts are on sale; the same switch adds My year to the menu, the account and Planner Plus links to the footer, and the sample to the sitemap. In the same run: set `launch:` in `content/plus_posts.yaml` to that day, run `python3 tools/plus_posts.py` (it writes the gift posts' social packs, dated from launch, none after 24 December) and rebuild (the cards are drawn only once the paywall is on).
Also, any time: allow web fetches for the Planner Plus scheduled task (automatic approval in the task's settings, if the organisation allows it), so the activity rows and place data can be done; send hotel and train affiliate ids when joined; Amazon Associates is still pending.

#### 6u. Home page led by Things to do (Robin, 13 Nov 2026: "I think this is now the core of the website… it is more than the daily articles")
The site is reframed around Things to do: activities are the reason to visit, stories are the proof that someone did it, guides are the how. What changed:
- **Home order:** the line and a one-sentence promise; the **finder** (search box sending `/list/?q=`, topic chips sending `/list/?topic=`, Everything, My list with its count, and "Tell us what interests you"); **today's story** (still the first story, §6n) with a "Have a go yourself" line to the activities tied to it (`story:`/`guide:`); **Good to do in <month>** (six activities whose best months, from the planner's `when`, include this month and are not all year); **Read up before you go** (six activities with full `detail` sections); then Picked for you, Latest stories, the tiles, the manifesto, topics and the rest as before. The two activity rows rotate with the build date (stable within a day), photographs first, and never repeat each other or the tied activities. Cards carry "Add to my list" (Plausible `list_add` with `from: home`). Code: the `home` block in `build.py` (the home page is now written after the activities), `templates/index.html`, the "Home led by Things to do" block at the end of `static/style.css`.
- **No count on the home page** (Robin, 27 Oct 2026): "Things worth doing", never a number.
- **`/list/?q=`** fills the search and scrolls to the results (`static/list.js`); `?topic=` now scrolls too. A `home_find` Plausible event counts finder searches; the search words never leave the browser.
- **Menu:** Things to do is first. Stories stays under Topics (a separate Stories link pushed the header past 1280px).
- **Home title and description** now lead with things to do: "Don't Die Retired: things worth doing, and how to start".
- **The app's Today screen (13 Nov 2026)** follows the same order: greeting, "What do you fancy doing?" with a search box (`/list/?q=`) and topic chips, three "Good to do in <month>" activities, the story with its "Have a go yourself" line (only when the story shown is today's), My list, then the ten-minute plan or weekly challenge (its old page heading is now a section heading), three "Read up before you go" activities, puzzles and Scam checker. Data: `dist/app/home.json`, written by the same `home` block in `build.py`. Cache name raised to `ddr-app-v14`.
- **Pottery (13 Nov 2026):** added `try-hand-building-with-clay` (WAES and the Office for the Arts at Harvard) and `try-raku-firing` (Stanwick Lakes, UK only: no US raku page opened yet), both `collection: arts`, with link terms raku and hand-building.
- **Done 19 Nov 2026:** Start here is now "Find your first thing to do" (finder first, then the three questions); the home lead and About say what the site is (§1 positioning); `site.description` matches. The menu was left as it is: Things to do already leads, and "My year" joins it at the Planner Plus launch (`plus.paywall`), which gives the Find / Plan / Stories / Play order without widening the header now.
- **Next, not done yet:** social and the newsletter could carry seasonal activities as well as stories; the Create and Events sections (`claude/activity-backlog.md` in the Project) slot in as topics or collections.

### 6u. Deep hubs: one subject covered in depth (Robin, 17 Nov 2026; ultra running first)
Robin asked for an ultra running section that lists the big ultras worldwide from the main brands, with all the data needed so the page is "a primary source on the internet", plus guides on kit for different races and environments with links to equipment, as a **test case for expanding other categories to internet-leading pages**. He chose: main brands plus classics (about 150 to 200 races over time), hub now then daily build-out runs, and a reusable pattern.
- **Files:** `content/hubs/<id>.yaml` (one per subject): `slug` (the page is `/<slug>/`), `category`, `label`, `h1`, `seo_title`, `description`, `lead`, `intro`, `tags` (search words), `activities` (Things to do ids that lead in), `facets` (labels for continent, terrain, entry, series), `events`, `guides`. Templates `hub.html` and `hub-guide.html`, finder script `static/hub.js` (filters the events already in the page; no storage, works without JS as a plain list), styles at the end of `static/style.css` (`.hub-*`). `build.py` loads hubs before Things to do (so an activity can carry `hub: <id>`, which shows a box linking to the hub) and renders them after it. Links in: the activity's hub box, "In depth" chips on `/list/`, a line on the category page.
- **An event:** `id` (permanent), `name`, `series`, `place`, `country`, `continent`, `km` (list of the main distances, used for the distance bands), `distances` (text), optional `climb`, `terrain` (list), `format` single/stage/timed, optional `month` (usual months, 1 to 12, shown as "Usually"), `entry` (list: open, ballot, qualifier, application), `entry_note`, `note`, `link` (label, url, checked). The build refuses missing fields, unknown facet values and prices. Every fact must come from the linked official page (or another official page) opened on `checked`; no superlatives without a source; leave `month` out rather than guess it. The finder never states a total.
- **A guide:** `slug`, `title`, `short`, `seo_title`, `description`, `standfirst`, `detail` (h with p, table or list, as on activities), `faq`, `kit` (Amazon UK search links, `why` says what to check, never a quality claim), `sources` (each opened on `checked`). The footer says it is written with AI from those pages and not reviewed by a coach or doctor.
- **Research tip:** WebFetch only opens addresses that appeared in a search, so search with `allowed_domains` set to the organiser's domain: the results come from the official pages and give their URL. Several organisers' domains in one search work for families (all `*.utmb.world` subdomains at once), but one big organiser tends to fill the results.
- **State, 17 Nov 2026:** `/ultra-running/` live with 33 races (UTMB Mont-Blanc and 15 more UTMB World Series events, Western States, Hardrock, Leadville, Badwater 135, Comrades, Two Oceans, MDS Legendary, MDS 120, Grand Raid Réunion, Spartathlon, Tor des Géants, Lakeland 100/50, Dragon's Back, Winter Spine, four Centurion 100s), two guides (`kit-by-environment`, `how-to-get-a-place`) and the activity `run-an-ultramarathon` (`hub: ultra-running`). Tor des Géants' facts rest on 2020 pages: re-check. Centurion 100s have no month (not on the pages opened).
- **State, 18 Nov 2026 (build-out run 1):** 48 races (RUNBOOK only; the site never states a total). Added: Javelina Jundred, Black Canyon Ultras, Cocodona 250 (series `aravaipa`), JFK 50 Mile, Arrowhead 135, Patagonia Bariloche by UTMB, Paraty Brazil by UTMB, Transgrancanaria, MIUT, Mt.FUJI 100 (series `wtm` = World Trail Majors, a new facet value, used only where the organiser's page says so), Istria 100 by UTMB, West Highland Way Race, Arc of Attrition by UTMB, RMB Ultra-trail Cape Town, Ultra-Trail Kosciuszko by UTMB. Centurion Thames Path, South Downs Way and North Downs Way 100s now have months and 2027 pages. New guide `first-ultra` (ITRA categories, Centurion and Lakeland entry and advice, ISSN 2019 figures, Hew-Butler 2017 on hyponatraemia, Scheer 2021 and Burgess 2025 on injury, NHS running injuries and heat exhaustion). Finder filters on phones: selects now shrink to their column (`minmax(0,1fr)`, `min-width:0`); before, long options pushed the page to 479px at 390px. **Held back:** Cape Wrath Ultra (its page says the 2026 event was cancelled after Ourea Events went into administration; 2027 is a different non-stop race, "The Wrath"); HK100 (only 2024/25 pages found); Ultra-Trail Ninghai (no current event page; official pages disagree on April or October); Tor des Géants re-check (tordesgeants.it gave client errors to every fetch, 18 Nov). Black Canyon, UTCT and Mt.FUJI 100 are not UTMB events (only UTMB Index pages exist): never tag them `utmb`.
- **State, 19 Nov 2026 (build-out run 2):** 65 races (RUNBOOK only). Added: Grindstone 100M (`utmb`: its site is now grindstone.utmb.world), Bigfoot 200, Tahoe 200, Moab 240 (new series facet `destination-trail`), Iditarod Trail Invitational, Yukon Arctic Ultra, Atacama Crossing and Gobi March (new facet `racingtheplanet`), Kepler Challenge, Trail 100 Andorra by UTMB, Salomon Ultra Pirineu 100K, Highland Fling, Race to the King (new facet `threshold`), Montane Summer Spine, Kaga Spa Trail Endurance 100 by UTMB, Trojan Om Die Dam, 90km du Mont-Blanc. The independent checker caught: Race to the King "old route to Winchester" (no page says it: removed), "the bigger danger" (our comparison: now "can be dangerous"), Kepler "first come, first served" (not on the page), Atacama field size (two pages disagree, 150 and 200: dropped), Tahoe single-track wording, Grindstone priority window (2023 page only: dropped), Andorra "refuges" (page says shelters), NSAID and "may worsen" wording, Autumn 100 drop bag at mile 100: all fixed. **Held back:** Highland Fling month (its page says both 18 August and 18 September 2027: month left out); Ultra Fiord (only the 2024 edition online); Buffalo Stampede (only 2025); Ultra-Trail Mount Kilimanjaro (no official current page; Ultra X Tanzania only has old pages). Ultra Pirineu's requirements come from its 2026 regulations PDF. pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov sometimes serves a captcha or a permission prompt to WebFetch; search first, then fetch, and retry once later.
- **State, 20 Nov 2026 (build-out run 3):** 76 races (RUNBOOK only). **Priority step 1 done:** a page per race at `/<hub slug>/<event id>/` (template `hub-event.html`; a race hub opts in with `event_pages: true`, set on ultra-running only, so the marathons run can switch it on for `/marathons/` when it reaches step 1; the build refuses an event id that clashes with a guide slug). Each page: the row's facts, entry, note, official link and `more` links with checked dates, the hub's guides, up to six "Races like this" (same series, then same region and distance band, then same band, then same region), breadcrumbs, canonical to itself, in the sitemap and site search. **No Event markup** (rows have only a usual month). Finder rows link the race name and a "Race page and similar races" line; the hub's ItemList points at the race pages. Guide sections may now carry `after:` (a small note under a table).
