New guide `crewing-and-pacing` (Western States pacer, crew and crew-suggestion pages; Centurion Autumn 100 rules and supporters pages; SDW100 2026 crew instructions; Lakeland 26 June 2026 spectating post; UTMB 2027 regulations penalty table for OCC/MCC/ETC). The checker caught (fixed): "a pacer keeps you company and nothing more" (WSER pacers may help fill bottles and restock at aid stations), Lakeland's Dalemain exception applies to 50 runners' friends and family only (not "four miles", not the 100), Lakeland supporters only at set points, SDW100 pacer cell, WSER "one at a time" has shared stretches, UTMB supporter wording, Dolomiti medical certificate only for the longer races and "loop", Oman Jabal Classic is "100K" (no exact km) and "almost 8,000 m", Puerto Vallarta MyUTMB/portal steps not on the pages opened, Northburn 28-hour 100 km limit unsupported and 48 hours is the buckle standard. **Held back:** Race to the Stones (racetothestones.com shows 2015 and 2019 pages only; enter page blocked by robots; nothing on thresholdsports.com), Patagonian International Marathon (2027 has no ultra distance; only 2013 to 2015 editions had one), Alpine Challenge (latest page 2023), Great North Walk 100s (site shows 2021). No South American race added this run. Re-check Oman by UTMB after its 10 to 12 December 2026 race.
- **Priority order for the ultra build-out (Robin, 19 Nov 2026: agreed "yes do it" to the plan below).** Why: the finder filters in the browser, so Google sees one page and the race data cannot rank; race-name searches will always go to organisers, UltraSignup, UTMB, ITRA and the big magazines, so we aim at the comparison searches no organiser answers (ultras with no qualifier in Europe, 100-milers in June, UK ballots, generous cut-offs). Each build-out run does **one numbered step (or the next part of it) first**, then adds races as time allows (8 to 12 instead of 12 to 20 while a step is in progress), then the guide. Record progress against the number here.
  1. **(Done 20 Nov 2026)** **A page per race** at `/<hub slug>/<event id>/` (event ids are permanent, so the address is too): everything in the row, the official link and checked date, a "Plan this race" link once step 6 exists, related races (same series, region, distance band), links to the guides. The finder rows link to it; the sitemap lists it; canonical to itself. Use `SportsEvent`/`Event` structured data **only** with an exact `startDate` taken from the official page on its checked date; with only a usual month, use no Event markup (Google treats missing or guessed dates as misleading). Breadcrumbs as on the guides.
  2. **Landing pages for common filters**, each a real, indexable page (not a query string): by continent and country with enough races (UK, USA, France, Spain, Italy), by distance band (50K, 100K, 100 miles, beyond 100 miles, stage races), by month, by entry (open entry, no qualifier needed, ballot), by series. A short intro in our own words built only from the rows' facts; never a total count of races on the site ("12 races here" on a filtered page is fine only if computed by the build); only build a page with at least 4 races. Same template, no thin pages.
  3. **Time limit field and an "ultras you can walk" filter** (the angle that suits DDR readers). Add `time_limit` (hours, from the official page; many rows already carry it in the note) and optional `walkers: true` only where the organiser's page says walkers, hikers or walking are welcome, or offers an early start for slower runners (e.g. Race to the King, Highland Fling). Filter label "Generous time limit / walkers welcome"; never our judgement of what is "easy". Back-fill existing rows on their next re-check, with a new `checked` date.
  4. **Entry calendar and alert sign-up.** Optional `entry_opens` (month, or exact date when the page shows one, with the year) per race; a "Ballots and entries opening soon" block on the hub and a monthly page. Sign-up goes to Buttondown with a tag (`int-ultra`); the page may promise alerts **only once a send is actually scheduled** (house rules: never claim a cadence that is not running) — until then the wording is "join the email list" with no promise. Social posts for big ballot openings (UTMB, Western States, Lakeland, Comrades) follow §6d.
  5. **Reviewers' picks for vests, headtorches and waterproof jackets** (first make `hub-guide.html` and the build render and check `picks:` as on activities, §6q/§6t), each from an independent review opened that day, quote checked word for word by the checker.
  6. **Ultra event in the guidance engine (§6r) and a "Plan this race" button** on race pages, so a reader can build a get-ready plan to a chosen race in Planner Plus. Training statements only from sources the engine already holds or new ones opened that day; no coach review claimed.
  7. **Real stories** of people who took up ultras after leaving work, from sourced reporting (house rules), linked from the hub and the matching race pages.
  - **Revenue notes (not yet built, check before claiming):** specialist running retailers' affiliate schemes (Awin and others) were suggested 19 Nov; no programme or rate has been checked. Race listings are never paid for: sponsorship, if ever, is labelled and kept off the finder and race pages.
- **Next for ultra (daily build-out run):** priority step 2 (landing pages for common filters) is next, with 8 to 12 races. Races still to add: the rest of RacingThePlanet (Last Desert needs an `antarctica` continent facet first, as marathons has), Ultra X races beyond Jordan (ultra-x.co), Self-Transcendence 6/10-day races, the other Centurion 50s (North Downs Way 50, Wendover Woods 50) and Centurion 100s not yet listed, GB Ultras, MDS Ultra, Ultra-Trail Australia's sister races, UTMB Asia (Ninghai, Xiamen, Malaysia), South America outside UTMB and RacingThePlanet (Ultra Fiord when a 2026/27 page appears; Torres del Paine), Hong Kong (HK100 when a current page appears), Canadian Death Race, Lavaredo's neighbours (Cortina Trail), Race to the Stones when a current page appears, Highland Fling month once its page agrees with itself; Tor des Géants re-check. Guides still to write: night running, heat, poles, and reviewer picks (`picks` rules as in §6q) for vests, jackets and headtorches (hub guides do not yet render `picks:`: add that to `hub-guide.html` and the build checks first; priority step 5). The guidance engine still needs an ultra event before the planner can give ultra training plans (§6r).

The finder shows only filters with more than one value in use, and only options some event uses (`h.used` in build.py).

Sky (total solar eclipse 2 Aug 2027 across Spain, North Africa and Egypt; aurora; meteor showers), living (bioluminescent bays, synchronous fireflies, murmurations, salmon runs, the wildebeest migration, the sardine run), earth (tidal bores, geysers, glow-worm caves), seasons (cherry blossom, autumn colour, bluebells). Needs its own facets (kind, season, continent) rather than race fields: generalise the event schema first (make `km`, `distances`, `terrain`, `format` optional when the hub sets `kind: phenomenon`).
- **Nature's spectacles, state 19 Nov 2026 (built):** `content/hubs/natures-spectacles.yaml`, page `/natures-spectacles/` (category explore), 21 entries (RUNBOOK only): 2027 total solar eclipse in Spain and Gibraltar (IGN, Gibraltar government) and across North Africa and Egypt (NASA); northern lights in the UK (AuroraWatch UK), Norway, Iceland, Finnish Lapland (official tourism); Perseids and Geminids (Royal Observatory Greenwich); starling murmurations (RSPB, Ham Wall); Puerto Rico's bioluminescent bays (Discover Puerto Rico); Smokies synchronous fireflies (NPS lottery); Brooks Falls bears and salmon (NPS Katmai); the great migration (Magical Kenya, TANAPA); the sardine run (South African Tourism); Waitomo glowworm caves (Tourism NZ, operator); Severn bore (Visit Dean Wye); Strokkur (Visit South Iceland); Old Faithful (NPS); bluebell woods (Woodland Trust); cherry blossom and autumn leaves in Japan (JNTO). Guide `2027-total-solar-eclipse` (where, how long, UK partial start, eye safety from NASA, RMG and RAS). Activities `see-a-total-solar-eclipse`, `see-the-northern-lights`, `watch-old-faithful-erupt`, `dark-sky-place`, `see-the-cherry-blossom-in-dc` carry `hub: natures-spectacles`.
  - **Schema (`kind: phenomenon`):** an entry has `id`, `name`, `type` (facet: sky, living, earth, season), `place`, `country`, `continent`, optional `month` (usual months; three or more read as a range "September to April", so list them in season order), optional `when` (text shown instead of "Usually", for one-off dates or "late March to early May"), `see` (facet: `own` on your own/no tour needed, `guided`, `boat`, `booked` booking/lottery/permit required, `vehicle`), `see_note`, `note`, `link`, optional `more` (extra official links). No `km`/`terrain`/`entry`/`series`. "Free to see" was deliberately not used as a facet: no page states fees, and the site gives no prices. The hub sets `about` (its own sourcing line), `button_text`, `chip_text`, `back_text`, `review_text`, `kit_text`, `search_hint` (all optional on any hub; defaults keep the race hubs unchanged, verified by diffing the built ultra and marathon pages).
  - **Checker caught (fixed):** North Africa eclipse is not "morning" in Egypt (10:07 UT is about 1pm local): only Spain's is "around 10:50 in the morning"; Puerto Rico swimming (the two official pages disagree: now "Discover Puerto Rico says swimming is not allowed in the bio bays"); AuroraWatch "go somewhere dark" not on its page (removed); Waitomo `booked` (booking only recommended: dropped) and "ends with a boat ride"; Old Faithful's 102 minutes is a median; Geminid rate lower under light pollution; RAS glasses are "CE-marked"; Brooks Falls September and October.
  - **Held back:** Luxor and "longest until 2114" (Egypt's State Information Service pages refuse fetches; no other official page names Luxor); Westonbirt autumn colour (its page gives no months); Serengeti month-by-month herd positions (not on the TANAPA pages opened); a Perseid hourly rate (RMG gives 100 on one page and 150 on another); Ham Wall's county (not in the page text).
  - **State, 20 Nov 2026 (build-out run 1):** 33 entries (RUNBOOK only). Added: Quadrantids (RMG, 2027 peak), Farne Islands puffins (National Trust), Donna Nook grey seals (Lincolnshire Wildlife Trust), Richmond and Bushy Park deer rut (Royal Parks), Shenandoah autumn colour (NPS), Natural Bridges monarchs (California State Parks), Iguazú and Perito Moreno (Argentina's Parques Nacionales; new continent facet `south-america`), Victoria Falls (Zimbabwe Tourism Authority, Zimbabwe side only: no Zambian government page was confirmed), Christmas Island red crabs (Parks Australia), Phillip Island Penguin Parade (Phillip Island Nature Parks, `booked`: booking essential), Liyu Lake fireflies, Taiwan (Taiwan Tourism Administration; 2026 dates, `guided` not `booked` because the page does not say registration is required). New guide `northern-lights-when-and-where` (AuroraWatch UK alert levels and channels, Met Office, NOAA SWPC, Visit Norway, Visit Iceland, Icelandic Met Office, Visit Finland, RMG, NASA). Activities `see-grey-seal-pups`, `see-puffins`, `watch-the-deer-rut`, `see-victoria-falls`, `see-iguazu-falls`, `watch-a-meteor-shower` now carry `hub: natures-spectacles`. Build more activity hubs"):** long-distance walks, big bike rides and open-water swims, all race-kind hubs with their own words. Research by three helpers (one fetch at a time, official pages only), then a second, independent helper per file re-opened every link and source and corrected the files before anything was built. All rows checked 20 Nov 2026. Counts in RUNBOOK only (the site never states a total).
  - **Hub wording options (race hubs, all optional; ultra, marathons and Nature's spectacles render byte-for-byte as before):** `word`/`words` (race/races: finder heading, "<Word> page and similar <words>", "The <word> at a glance", "<Words> like this", "Independent <word>", the event-page disclosure, which then reads "We list <noun_one> only after opening its official page"), `entry_label`/`entry_any` (the Entry filter), `entry_h2` ("How you get a place"), `getting_in` (row label), `series_dt` (event-page label for the series), `stage_text` ("stage race") and `stage_option` ("Multi-day stages"), `seo_suffix`/`seo_short`/`desc_mid` (event-page title and description), `trust_text` (the line under the official link), `month_range: true` (three or more months read "May to September", as on phenomenon hubs; list them in season order), `month_dt`/`month_filter`/`month_suffix` (walks say "Season" and "the season the official page gives").
  - **Event `activity:` (new, any race hub):** the Things to do id with our how-to-start guide for that event. The build refuses an unknown id. The finder row gets "How to start: <activity>", the event page an "Our guide to getting started" section, and the activity page's hub box links straight to that event page. Use it whenever an activity is about one event or route.
  - **Long-distance walks** (`content/hubs/long-distance-walks.yaml`, `/long-distance-walks/`, category travel): 41 routes. 23 UK and Ireland (15 England and Wales National Trails: Pennine Way, South West Coast Path, Offa's Dyke, Hadrian's Wall, Cotswold Way, South Downs Way, Thames Path, Pembrokeshire, Cleveland Way, Ridgeway, North Downs Way, Peddars Way and Norfolk Coast Path, Yorkshire Wolds Way, Glyndŵr's Way, Coast to Coast; five of Scotland's Great Trails; Wales Coast Path, Wicklow Way, Causeway Coast Way) and 18 abroad (Camino Francés, Kumano Kodo Nakahechi, Tour du Mont Blanc, Alta Via 1, Laugavegur, Milford, Routeburn, Kepler, Abel Tasman, Heaphy, Overland Track, Larapinta, Appalachian Trail, Pacific Crest Trail, John Muir Trail, Continental Divide Trail, West Coast Trail, Kalalau). Facets: network (`national-trail`, `great-trail`, `us-nst`, `nz-great-walk`, `pilgrim`, `independent`), terrain (coast, lowland, hills, mountain), what you must book (`any`, `huts`, `permit`, `guide`; no row uses `guide` yet), length bands (under 100, 100 to 249, 250 to 999, 1,000 km+). `format: single` everywhere; days go in `distances`. Guides `planning-a-long-distance-walk` (choosing, sections, waymarking, beds and baggage, feet, Countryside Code and Scottish Outdoor Access Code, AdventureSmart, calling mountain rescue) and `treks-you-must-book` (huts, quotas, permits, Nepal's licensed-guide rule from 31 March 2023, booking-day tips, NHS altitude advice). 28 activities carry `hub: long-distance-walks`; 26 routes carry `activity:`. **Research notes:** nationaltrail.co.uk often fails to load: GOV.UK's "Visit a National Trail in England" pages (June 2026) cover 14 trails and load reliably. Coast to Coast became a National Trail on 26 March 2026 (GOV.UK news). Where GOV.UK and National Trails give different lengths (Thames Path 298 v 294 km, North Downs Way 246 v 245 km, Pembrokeshire 299 v 300 km) the note says so. No National Trail page mentions permits, so rows never say "no permit needed" (only the Wales Coast Path says it). `month` only where a page names a season (Pennine Way, TMB, Laugavegur, Milford, Routeburn, Kepler, West Coast Trail); Hadrian's Wall ("Easter to October") and Thames Path have the page's words in the note instead. Kumano Kodo (68 km) and Alta Via 1 (129.5 km) lengths are our sums of the official stage lengths, labelled as such. TMB: La Chamoniarde prints "168 km (156 miles)"; we say about 104 miles. **Held back:** Tongariro Northern Circuit (DOC's page says both that it is not operating as a Great Walk while Oturere Hut is replaced and that it will be available for 2026/27), Inca Trail, W Trek, Kilimanjaro, Everest Base Camp, Annapurna Circuit, GR20 (no official route length found; GR20 booking rules and Nepal's guide rule are in the guide), Via Francigena (pages would not load), Lycian Way and Isle of Man (not researched).

Facets: kind of ride (`sportive`, `gran-fondo` only where the organiser times and ranks riders, `charity`, `tour`, `audax`), terrain (road, mountain, gravel, cobbles), entry as marathons plus `qualifier`. **Never call a sportive a race in our words** (British Cycling: "personal challenge ride"). Guide `your-first-sportive` (British Cycling pages, Highway Code rules 59 to 82, event rules). Activities with `hub: big-bike-rides`: ride-a-sportive, ride-a-century, ride-the-etape-du-tour, ride-the-fred-whitton-challenge, ride-london-to-brighton. **Held back:** RideLondon-Essex 100 (organiser: "indefinite pause", last held May 2024), RAGBRAI (2027 route and distances revealed 23 January 2027: add it then; registration opened 15 Nov 2026), Haute Route Alps (no current page), Ride the Rockies (robots block fetching). **Re-check:** Lake Taupō after its 28 Nov 2026 ride; UNBOUND after its 21 Nov lottery; Dragon Ride and Hotter'N Hell (2026 routes shown); L'Eroica's ticket table (mixed years); Dragon Ride Medio Fondo is 153 km in the page text, 157 km in its meta description.

### 6v. Gift ideas hub (Robin, 19 Nov 2026: "a section or hub that helps people select and buy presents for friends and family that want to do activities, or they want to do activities. Make it so it is favourable for searches and easy for people to share"; then "provide reviews, why to select, why the person would want the gift, a range of options by price and functionality… include the planner… promote the puzzle books as stocking fillers… can become a general present market after Christmas"; "Do everything you have suggested")
- **Pages.** `/gifts/` (hub), `/gifts/<group>/` for 14 interests (walkers, runners, swimmers, cyclists, on-the-water, wildlife, stargazers, makers, food-and-drink, music-and-dance, adventure, gardeners, curious-minds, big-trips) and occasion pages (`retirement`, `experiences` = every idea with a booking option, `together`), all indexable with BreadcrumbList + ItemList JSON-LD and their own link-preview card (`/static/img/gifts/og-<id>.png`). `/gifts/shared/` (noindex) shows a shortlist: your own (`#mine`, from localStorage `ddr_gifts`, max 30) or one someone sent (`?k=ideas|hint&g=id.id&n=Name`; the name is shown as text only). `/gifts/gifts.json` feeds it. Templates `gifts.html`, `gift-group.html`, `gift-shared.html`, `_gifts.html`; script `static/gifts.js`; styles `.gift-*` at the end of style.css.
- **Data.** `content/gifts.yaml`. Every idea is an activity id, so facts, kit, official links and booking links come from the activity. The file adds the gift's name, optionally `book` (Viator/GetYourGuide **searches**, labelled as searches, only where the activity has no `book`) and optionally `picks: <key>` from `content/picks.yaml` (same rules as §6t row 17: reviewer's own label, quote ≤30 words copied from the review opened on `checked`, top only for their overall pick, value only for their budget/value pick, no prices). Added 19 Nov: `telescopes` (Space.com), `running-watch` (TechRadar), `changing-robe` (OutdoorsMagic). The Viator/GYG search pages could not be opened from the cloud session (not on the allowlist): a run with a browser should spot-check them. No prices, no "best"/"most popular", no delivery promises.
- **Ways to give it** on each card: book it (affiliate params added by app.js), give your company (free activities), reviewers' picks (top / also recommended / lower-cost = the price and feature range Robin asked for) or the activity's kit list (labelled as ideas, not tested), where to start (official link), full guide, Save, Share.
- **Season.** `gifts.yaml` → `season` (from 19 Nov to 25 Dec 2026): Christmas h1/titles/kicker, the crimson home tile (`gifts_season`), the "Gifts" menu link (on phones it takes the Email link's place). After `until` the pages stay with year-round wording (birthdays, retirement). Next season: move the dates.
- **Books and Planner Plus.** Every gift page carries the stocking-filler panel (Christmas Puzzle Book, Christmas Quiz Book UK/US; facts from `/puzzle-books/`; shown while their `until` has not passed) and the Planner Plus gift panel: "Not on sale yet… join the email list" until `plus.paywall` is true, then "£20 for twelve months… Give Planner Plus" linking `/planner-plus/gift/`.
- **Links in.** Home tile, menu, footer, and a line on every activity page that is a gift idea ("Thinking of giving this as a present?").
- **Social.** `content/gift_posts.yaml` + `tools/gift_posts.py`: one post a day at 10:30 UK, 19 Nov to 9 Dec, then 11, 13, 16 and 20 Dec (crimson text cards, Facebook picture post with the link in the text). Never edit a sent post. The tool also draws a tall pin per gift page and writes **`/feed/gifts.xml`** for a Pinterest "Gift ideas" board (Robin to connect it: Pinterest → Settings → Bulk create Pins → Auto-publish → add `https://dontdieretired.com/feed/gifts.xml` to a new board).
- **Email.** `newsletters/2026-48.md` (send_on 29 Nov) is the gift-guide edition; the workflow makes it a Buttondown draft if `BUTTONDOWN_API_KEY` is set, and Robin sends it.
- **Waiting on Robin.** (1) Red Letter Days affiliate via Partnerize (`site.yaml` → `affiliates.red_letter_days`); once the id is in, add "Give it as a voucher" links to UK ideas that fit. (2) Planner Plus launch (§6t launch steps) so the gift panel can sell. (3) Pinterest feed above.
- **Stocking fillers (added 19 Nov 2026, Robin: "think stocking fillers, people buy all manner of gifts"; "Let's have more content. Let's get the traffic to site").** `content/stocking_fillers.yaml` → `/gifts/stocking-fillers/` (hub: interest tiles, the books panel, a how-to-choose guide, the promise card, Planner Plus panel, links to the bigger-present pages) and one indexable page per interest at `/gifts/stocking-fillers/<id>/` (17: walkers, runners, swimmers, cyclists, on-the-water, gardeners, cooks, stargazers, wildlife, makers, music, puzzlers, travellers, campers, dog-walkers, photographers, winter; 117 items), aimed at "stocking fillers for <hobby>" searches. Each item: `name`, `why`, `check`, `q` (an Amazon UK search; app.js adds the tag and sends US readers to amazon.com) and optional `for` (an activity id: "Read up" link, which keeps readers on the site). The build refuses prices and "best"/"top pick"/"most popular"/delivery promises, unknown activity ids and gift groups. Facts in `check` are plain product facts; the eclipse glasses line follows the NASA wording already on `see-a-total-solar-eclipse`. Templates `stocking.html`, `stocking-section.html`, macros `sf_item`, `promise_panel`, `sf_link` in `_gifts.html`; styles `.sf-*`, `.pc-*` at the end of style.css. Each gift group page links its matching stocking-filler page (`group:`), and the /gifts/ hub has a Stocking fillers tile and the promise panel. **Promise card** `/gifts/promise-card/` (`static/promise.js`): pick a free activity from `promise.ids` (or write your own), add names, print; text only, nothing stored or sent; Plausible event `promise_card` if Plausible is ever back. **Social:** 20 extra gift posts at 15:30 UK (slot), 20 Nov to 12 Dec (`gift-sf-*`, `gift-promise-card*`); `tools/gift_posts.py` now knows these pages (`path`), draws their link-preview cards (`og-stocking-fillers.png`, `og-sf-<id>.png`, `og-promise-card.png`) and adds them to `/feed/gifts.xml` for Pinterest. **Not done:** price bands ("under £10") were left out because we cannot open Amazon to check prices; nothing here has been tested or reviewed. Next: reviewers' picks (picks.yaml rules) for the few items people compare most (head torch, secateurs, tuner), and after Christmas retitle to year-round wording ("small gifts").
- **Christmas present list on every activity page (Robin, 19 Nov 2026: "add a temporary Christmas present list for people interested in the activities. Do for all activities so it is found for Christmas present searches"; he chose a section on each page over separate pages).** While `gifts.yaml` → `season` is on, `templates/challenge.html` shows a section headed "<season name> present ideas for someone who wants to <activity title>" (an h2, so search engines read it) in place of the separate Book it and Kit boxes: Give the experience (the activity's `book` links and its gift page), Kit to give (its `kit`), Stocking fillers (any `stocking_fillers.yaml` item whose `for:` is this activity, with its check line), Or give your company (promise card, for free and low-cost activities), the affiliate disclosure and links to /gifts/ and stocking fillers. It appears on every activity with kit, booking links, a gift page or a stocking filler; activities with none of these (money, admin, civic and crisis-support roles) keep their normal page. After `season.until` the old boxes come back by themselves. Titles and descriptions were left alone so the pages keep ranking for their main searches. Styles `.xgift*` at the end of style.css. Next season: move the dates.
- **Next.** More reviewers' picks for gift kit (head torch, paddleboard, bike multi-tool, ukulele exists, pottery tools, sketching set), one or two items per run; a January page ("Given an experience voucher? How to book it") from 27 Dec; after Christmas, birthday wording.
- **Removing from the list (20 Nov 2026, Robin: "I added an activity to my list I now can't remove it").** Remove used to sit only inside each row's collapsed "Date, note, move". Now: every "Add to my list" button (cards, home, activity pages; not the friend-invite line) gets a dashed **Remove** beside it while the activity is on the list (`static/list.js` paintAdds wraps them in `.fl-addrow`); each row of My list has a visible **✕ Remove** next to its status. Every removal can be undone (the Remove button turns into **Undo** for 15 seconds; on My list an "Undo" link appears), which puts back the item with its plan, ticks and note through `DDRList.reinstate(snap)` (tested in `tools/test_list_merge.js`). Removing still clears the plan (a removed item keeps no plan, as before); `plan-page.js` listens for the `ddr:list` event so an open plan disappears or returns at once. Plausible event `list_remove` (no id). Also fixed the double tick on "✓ On my list ✓" chips.

## 6p. Links between pages (Robin, 27 Oct 2026: "if a person is in one page they are naturally led to others")
- **Foot of every story and guide:** "Try it yourself" shows up to three activity cards: the activities tied to the piece (`story:`/`guide:`), then those sharing its subject (activity id words against the piece's tags, ignoring place names and loose words), then a stable pick from the same topic. "Keep reading" shows three pieces ranked by shared tags, then topic, newest first.
- **Activity pages:** "Read next" (two stories or guides on the same subject, else the same topic) and "More like this" as coloured cards.
- **Links in the text:** `content/link_terms.yaml` maps a term to a page. The build links its first mention in story bodies and in activity guide sections: at most four links a page, never in headings, quotes, captions or other links, never to the page itself. Capitalised terms match exactly; lower-case terms ignore case. When a run writes a new activity with a distinctive name (a mountain, trail, event, sport), add its term. Never add a word that can mean more than one thing.
- Tags are what the matching runs on, so give every new piece specific subject tags (swimming, rowing, birds), not only general ones (women, history).

### 6k. Accessibility and search basics (set 7 Oct 2026: keep these when changing templates or styles)
A scripted probe (axe-core plus keyboard and zoom tests) took the site from 388 automated failures to 4. The rules that got it there:
- **The profile questions are a native `<dialog>`** opened with `DDR.openProfile()` (static/app.js). It never opens by itself: no timers, no scroll triggers. It has a 44px close button at the top; Escape closes it and focus returns to the opener.
- **The Topics menu scrolls inside itself** (`max-height` + `overflow-y`) and closes on Escape, an outside click or when focus leaves. The header wraps to two rows at 1040px and below, so adding a menu item must never cause sideways scroll: after any header change, check widths from 320 to 1280.
- **A picture link beside a text link to the same place is hidden from assistive tech** (`aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1"` on `.card-img`). Links inside grey label text are underlined. Tap targets are at least 44px on touch screens. The focus ring is `--focus` (dark gold), not `--gold`.
- **Headings go in order**: one `h1`, then `h2`, then `h3`. Use the classes `.h3` / `.h4` / `.foot-h` to get a smaller look without skipping a level.
- **Search results.** Article `<title>` is the headline, with the site name added only if both fit in 60 characters (`seo_title` in front matter overrides the headline). The description is `description:` from front matter if present, else the standfirst, cut to 155 characters at a sentence (`metadesc`). New articles should set `description:` (one plain sentence, 120 to 155 characters).
- **Honest freshness.** `dateModified` and the sitemap `<lastmod>` come from the last "Updated <date>:" or "Corrected <date>:" line at the foot of the article, else the publication date. Never the build date.
- Still worth a human pass some day: a screen reader (VoiceOver, TalkBack) on the dialog, the app and the Gumroad hand-off.
- **Search Console, state at 7 Oct 2026** (read in Robin's browser; the cloud session cannot reach it). 51 pages indexed, 28 not: 21 "Crawled - currently not indexed", 4 "Alternative page with proper canonical tag", 3 "Excluded by noindex". The build itself is clean (203 sitemap pages, each with a matching canonical, no internal link to a redirect). What was wrong:
  - **`http://` pages answered 200 instead of redirecting**, because "Enforce HTTPS" was off in the repo's Settings → Pages, so Google met every page twice; about half the "not indexed" examples were the `http://` copies. Only Robin can tick it (the Pages API is closed to the cloud session). Check from a browser, not the shell: `http://dontdieretired.com/connect/` must end up on `https://`.
  - **`https://www.dontdieretired.com` has no valid certificate** (DNS is right: `www` CNAME → `rrhutchins.github.io`). Removing and re-saving the custom domain in Settings → Pages makes GitHub issue one covering both names.
  - The plans are `noindex` on purpose (email-gated), `/search/?q=…` and `?via=friend` addresses are alternates on purpose: those rows are expected and need no fix.
  - Do not write `href="${…}"` inside an inline script: crawlers read it as a real link (the search page produced `/search/${a.url}`, a 404). Spell the attribute through a variable, as `templates/search.html` now does.
  - **Re-read 12 Oct 2026:** 93 indexed, 46 not (32 alternate-canonical, 10 noindex, 2 redirect, 2 crawled-not-indexed: the newest story and `feed.xml`). The `www` certificate is now fixed (`https://www…` redirects to the bare domain), but `http://` pages still answer 200 without redirecting, so "Enforce HTTPS" is still off; every one of the 32 alternate-canonical rows is an `http://` copy. The 10 noindex rows are plans and Your say, as intended.
  - **27 Oct 2026:** Robin has ticked "Enforce HTTPS" (Settings → Pages). Next time Search Console is read, the alternate-canonical rows for `http://` copies should start falling; if `http://` pages still answer 200 a week later, tell Robin.
  - "Crawled - currently not indexed" on `https://` pages of a nine-day-old site is Google deciding in its own time; do not churn the pages or their dates to chase it. Re-read the report weekly once Enforce HTTPS is on.

### 6n. Personalised order must never hide the new story (Robin, 9 Oct 2026)
Readers who answer the profile questions (level, age band, interests) get pages reordered in `static/app.js` (`fit()`). Robin found that after answering them the site looked the same every day. Rules now:
- **Today's story is always the first story on the home page**, for everyone, directly under the Things to do finder (since 13 Nov 2026, §6u). Personalisation never replaces it or moves it down.
- **"Picked for you"** (home page, from `/index.json`) never repeats the hero. Newness weighs as much as interests: a story from today or yesterday gets +6, within three days +3, within a week +1, on top of a newest-first rank bonus. Stories this browser has already opened (`ddr_seen`, article paths only, kept on the device, last 300) drop by 8, so the list changes as the reader reads. Cards from the last day that the reader has not opened carry a "New" tag.
- **Topic pages** reorder with the same score (cards carry `data-date` and `data-url`).
- **`/stories/` (Real Stories) lists every daily story from every topic, newest first**, not only the few filed under `stories`.
- If you change the scoring, check with a saved profile that today's story is still first on the home page and near the top of "Picked for you" the morning after a new one is published.

## 7. Guard-rails
- Never invent facts, quotes, ages or names. If the source can't be fetched, don't run the story.
- Never give medical advice or dosing; always "check with a GP first".
- Label sponsored content; keep the affiliate disclosure on every page (it's in the footer).
- No ageist framing, no "despite their age" tone, no before/after body shaming.
- Respect the `hidden`-by-default consent banner: ads load only after consent (handled in app.js).
- Keep the same URL for an article once published (slug in front matter).

### 7a. Copyright (set 2 Oct 2026 — applies to every article, guide, plan, product, image and social post)
- **Write it yourself.** Facts are free to report; another writer's sentences are not. Read the source, then tell the story in our own words and our own structure. Never paste or lightly reword a passage, and never follow a source paragraph by paragraph.
- **Quote people, sparingly.** At most three or four short quotes per story, each under about 30 words, and no more than 15 per cent of the body in total. Every quote is attributed to the speaker and the outlet, and the outlet is linked in `sources`. Prefer what the person said over the journalist's own prose; paraphrase the journalist. `build.py` prints a COPYRIGHT WARNING when a story goes over these limits: fix it before publishing.
- **Never reproduce** song lyrics, poems, book passages, or more than a line or two of any article, even with credit. Name the work and describe it instead.
- **Story artwork (set 4 Oct 2026).** Each article gets two images at build time: the headline card (`static/img/<slug>.png`, used for social and link previews) and the on-site artwork (`static/art/<slug>.svg`, from `art.py`): the logo's sun in the topic colour, rising over a scene picked from the slug, headline and tags. After writing an article, look at its artwork in `dist/static/art/`; if the scene does not suit the story, set `art:` in the front matter to one of: sea, hills, mountains, road, rails, books, city, houses, fields, forest, cliffs, grid, steps. Avoid the same scene twice running on the homepage. A photograph replaces the artwork only under the rules in §6l.
- **Images (changed 7 Oct 2026, Robin).** Three kinds only: (1) images made by `build.py`, the product builder or our own brand files; (2) photographs from Wikimedia Commons under a public-domain, CC0, CC BY or CC BY-SA licence, fetched and credited by `tools/photos.py` exactly as §6l sets out; (3) a photograph whose owner has given us written permission (keep the email; set `photo:`, `photo_credit:`, `photo_alt:`, `photo_caption:`). Never copy a photograph, screenshot, chart, logo or illustration from a news site, social media or an image search, whatever the credit. Never use an AI-generated picture of a person or a place on the site; the one exception is the short story videos, which follow §6m. Stock-library photos of models are not used: they read as the person in the story.
- **Video.** Embed with the official YouTube player only (as `videos.yaml` and the story films in §6l do), from the channel of whoever made the film. Never download, re-upload, clip or screenshot someone else's film. Blurbs are our own words.
- **Fonts and code.** Fraunces and Source Sans 3 are used under the SIL Open Font License; keep the OFL text files beside the font files wherever they are stored. Check the licence before adding any other font, icon set or library.
- **Names and brands.** Mention organisations and products by name only to refer to them. Never use another organisation's logo, or imply that a person or organisation in a story endorses the site or its products.
- **If someone complains.** Any request to remove or correct something goes to Robin the same day; take the item down first if the claim looks plausible, then sort it out.

## 7b. Contact address (state at 7 Oct 2026)
`hello@dontdieretired.com` **works (since 25 Oct 2026).** The domain's DNS is on Cloudflare (moved from GoDaddy's nameservers that day; registration is still at GoDaddy) and its mail is Google Workspace: dontdieretired.com is a secondary domain on Robin's Workspace (primary domain pawsonthesand.co.uk) and hello@ is an alternate address on his user, so mail lands in his Workspace inbox. MX and SPF are complete. **Still to do (Robin):** DKIM, which Google would not generate on the first day (it needs 24 to 72 hours after Gmail is switched on): Admin → Apps → Google Workspace → Gmail → Authenticate email → dontdieretired.com → Generate new record → add the TXT `google._domainkey` in Cloudflare → Start authentication. The `_dmarc` record was set to `v=DMARC1; p=none;` during the move; put it back to `p=quarantine` only after DKIM shows as authenticating. `site.contact_email_live` is `true`, so contact links use the address through the `contact()` macro in `_macros.html`. **Always use `contact()` for a contact link, never a bare `mailto:`.** The sponsor page no longer promises a reply time; add one back only if Robin says how quickly he will answer. The puzzle books printed before 25 Oct send mistakes to the Facebook page, which still works; later editions can use the address.

## 8. Hosting and DNS (one-time)
1. Create GitHub repo `dontdieretired`, push this folder. Settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions.
2. At the registrar, add DNS: `A` records for `@` → 185.199.108.153, 185.199.109.153, 185.199.110.153, 185.199.111.153; `CNAME` `www` → `<github-user>.github.io`. Enable "Enforce HTTPS" in Pages once the certificate issues.
3. Netlify alternative: "Import from Git", build command `pip install -r requirements.txt && python build.py --no-social`, publish dir `dist`.
